# VectorCraft command catalog

Generated from `command_list` (VectorCraft 0.21.0, 911 commands). Run any of these with `command_run {id, params}`
(or several with `command_batch {steps}`); read one live with the `vectorcraft://command/{id}` resource or
`command_list {filter}`. `params` use the engine's shorthand: `?` = optional, `a|b` = choices, `→ {...}` = what it returns.
Grep this file (`grep -n 'object.align'`) rather than reading it whole.


## action

- `action.batch`: {commands: [{command, params}] (the action's steps), source?: folder (default) \| datasets, folder? (required for folder: the source folder), includeSubdirs?: false, destination?: none (default: leave each file open) \| saveAndClose (save over the source and close) \| folder (save or export into destinationFolder and close), destinationFolder? (required when destination is folder; also the folder Override Save/Export write into), datasetFileNaming?: fileNumber \| fileDataSetName (default) \| dataSetName (used only for source datasets; ignored for folder), overrideOpen?: true (skip document.open / file.open steps: the batch opens each source file), overrideSave?: true (point document.save / file.save / file.saveAs at destinationFolder/{stem}.{ext}), overrideExport?: true (point document.export / document.exportForScreens / document.exportSelection the same way), stopOnError?: true, label?} play the commands on every readable file in folder (and its subfolders when includeSubdirs), or on every data set of the open document. Data set names use the document stem plus a three-digit ordinal, the document stem and an underscore plus the data set name, or the data set name alone. Invalid file name characters become `-`; a name already used in the batch or whose output already exists in a destination folder the batch may read and write gets the next free `-2`, `-3` suffix before the format-derived extension. Caps: 500 files or data sets, 8 folder levels, 256 steps. Batches cannot nest. → {processed: [{path\|dataset, result\|error}…], count, failed, stopped}

## addon

- `addon.arrange.grid` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {layout?: "grid" (rows and columns) \| "row" (one row) \| "column" (one column), columns?: 1..10000 (grid; default ⌈√count⌉; never more than the count), gapX?: pt −1000000..1000000 (10), gapY?: pt −1000000..1000000 (gapX), centerOnArtboard?: false (true: the grid is centred on the artboard under the selection's centre, else the first; false: it starts at the selection's top-left)} lay the selected objects (the top-level ones) out in their stacking order from the back, left to right and top to bottom, as one undo step. Each cell is as wide as the widest object in its column and as tall as the tallest in its row, each object is centred in its cell, and everything is measured by visual bounds (strokes and effects as drawn) → {count}
- `addon.arrange.swap` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {size?: false (true: each also takes the other's width and height), scaleStrokes?: the Scale Strokes & Effects preference} swap the places (bounds centres) of the two selected objects, measured as object.setBounds measures them (Use Preview Bounds), as one undo step. Off unless exactly two objects are selected
- `addon.arrange.replace` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {fit?: "keep" (the copy keeps its size) \| "bounds" (scaled to the replaced object's width and height) \| "proportional" (scaled to fit inside them, in proportion) ("keep"), scaleStrokes?: the Scale Strokes & Effects preference, keep?: false (true: the replaced objects stay, under their copies)} put a copy of the topmost selected object centred on each of the other selected objects, in that object's place in the stacking order, as one undo step; sizes and centres are measured as object.setBounds measures them, and the copies are selected → {ids}
- `addon.arrange.size` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {width?: pt, height?: pt (at least one; each 0.001..1000000), proportional?: false (with only one of them given, the other follows in proportion; else it stays), reference?: 0..8 (the 9-point grid row by row from the top-left; 4, the centre: the point of each object that stays put), scaleStrokes?: the Scale Strokes & Effects preference} give each selected object (the top-level ones) that width and height, measured as object.setBounds measures them (Use Preview Bounds), as one undo step → {count}
- `addon.arrange.groupOverlapping` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {} group the selected objects (the top-level ones, at most 20000) wherever their visual bounds overlap or touch, chains included (when A overlaps B and B overlaps C, all three go in one group), as one undo step. Only objects in the same layer or group are grouped together, objects that overlap nothing stay as they are, and each new group goes where its frontmost member was in the stacking order; the new groups and the objects left alone stay selected → {groups: [id…], grouped (how many objects went into groups)}
- `addon.arrange.randomizeOpacity` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {min?: % 0..100 (30), max?: % 0..100 (100; not less than min), seed?: whole number (0; the same seed, objects and range give the same opacities), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} give each selected object (the top-level ones) its own opacity, picked at random and evenly from min to max, as one undo step; it is the object's opacity, as the Transparency panel sets it → {count}
- `addon.arrange.sort` (Object › Plug-ins › Arrange Tools): {by?: "width" \| "height" \| "area" (width × height) \| "x" (left edge) \| "y" (top edge) \| "opacity" (%) \| "stacking" (stacking order, from the back) ("area"), apply?: one of the same ("stacking"), order?: "ascending" (small to large) \| "descending" (large to small) \| "random" ("ascending"), distribute?: false (true: the values step evenly from the smallest to the largest; false: the objects trade the values they have), seed?: whole number (0; the order "random" picks), scaleStrokes?: the Scale Strokes & Effects preference (width, height and area), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} sort the selected objects (the top-level ones, at most 20000) by the attribute `by` and give them the values of `apply` in that order, as one undo step: the first in order gets the smallest value. Sizes and places are measured and set as object.setBounds measures them (Use Preview Bounds), a width, height or area about each object's centre (an area in proportion); stacking order always trades places, and only among the objects in the same layer or group. A run that changes nothing makes no step → {count}
- `addon.cleanup.simplify` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {tolerance?: 1..100 (20; higher removes more anchors and lets the outline move further from where it was, up to tolerance × 0.02 pt), protectCorners?: bool (false; anchors where the path turns by more than 30° stay), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} remove the anchors each path under the objects can do without, refitting the handles beside each so the outline keeps its shape, as one undo step; open ends stay, and locked and hidden paths are left out → {ids, before, after} anchor counts
- `addon.cleanup.check` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {scope?: "selection"\|"document" (default: the selection, or the document while nothing is selected), kinds?: [kind…] (default: all: emptyText, strayPoints (paths of one anchor), unpainted, zeroOpacity, duplicateObjects (a path stacked on an identical one, or type on the same type within 1 pt), offArtboard, duplicateAnchors (neighbours within 0.01 pt), tinyHandles (under 0.01 pt), collinearAnchors (corners on a straight run, turning less than sine 0.005), nearPoints (anchors within 0.01 pt of other paths' anchors), nearlyClosed (ends within 0.01 pt), filledOpenPaths (filled, unstroked open paths), needlessClipGroups (an unpainted clip that clips nothing, hides everything, or is a rectangle round all its art), needlessCompounds (one path or none), needlessGroups (one member or none, nothing of their own), emptyArtboards (the document only))} find issues without changing anything; locked and hidden objects are left out → {scope, total, findings: [{kind, label, count, ids (the objects, at most 1000), artboards (indexes)}]} for the kinds found
- `addon.cleanup.show` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {kind: kind (see addon.cleanup.check), scope?: "selection"\|"document"} select the objects with that issue → {kind, count, ids, artboards}
- `addon.cleanup.fix` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {scope?: "selection"\|"document", kinds?: [kind…] (default: all; see addon.cleanup.check)} fix the issues addon.cleanup.check finds, in up to three rounds while a round still finds some (a fix can leave issues for another kind), as one undo step: what is empty, unpainted, invisible, duplicated or off the artboards goes, anchors merge, snap or go, tiny handles retract, paths close and needless groups, compound paths and clipping groups give way to their contents → {scope, total, fixed: {kind: count}}
- `addon.cleanup.deepUngroup` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {includeClipGroups?: bool (false: clipping groups, and the art inside them, stay as they are; true: they are ungrouped too and their clipping paths become plain paths), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} ungroup every level of the groups among the objects, as one undo step; each object keeps the opacity of the groups it was in; locked and hidden groups stay whole, and locked and hidden objects stay locked or hidden and are not selected → {ids (the objects selected: those left, or with no group to take apart the selection as it was), ungrouped (groups)}
- `addon.cleanup.rejoin` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {gap?: pt 0..6 (0.1), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} weld the open paths under the objects whose ends lie within the gap of each other back into paths: an end joins one other end, the one the path turns least to go on into, pieces may run either way, and a chain whose ends meet closes; each path takes the place and paint of its topmost piece, as one undo step → {ids, joined (paths made), removed (pieces merged into them)}
- `addon.cleanup.breakText` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {by?: "lines"\|"paragraphs"\|"words"\|"glyphs" (lines), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} each horizontal point or area type object becomes point type objects, one per line, paragraph, word or glyph, each where its characters were and with their character styles, as one undo step; spaces between pieces go, a paragraph's lines stay lines (a wrapped line ends in a line break), justified lines close up, and a paragraph broken out whole keeps its bullet (not its number; other pieces have no list markers). Type on a path, vertical type, threaded type and area type whose text doesn't fit stay as they are → {ids (the pieces, selected), skipped}
- `addon.cleanup.combineText` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} merge one-line horizontal point type into as few objects as it can, every glyph staying where it was: pieces turned and sheared alike whose baselines line up (within 0.4 em; the difference becomes baseline shift) join left to right, a space goes between pieces at least half a space apart, and tracking on the character before each piece keeps the spacing; each object keeps its character styles, and the leftmost piece of each run its place and paint, as one undo step → {ids (the objects made, selected), combined (pieces merged into them)}
- `addon.cleanup.joinText` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} join one-line horizontal point type into one point type object, as one undo step: pieces turned and sheared like the top-left one are read as lines (baselines within 0.4 em of each other), top to bottom, each line's pieces left to right with a space between them; each line becomes a paragraph, left aligned, and keeps the distance between baselines as its leading. The text keeps its character styles; the object takes the top-left piece's place and paint, and the other pieces go → {id (selected), joined (pieces merged into it), lines}
- `addon.cleanup.removeBreaks` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {kind: "line"\|"paragraph", ids?: [id…]} remove the forced line breaks (line) or paragraph returns (paragraph) in the characters selected with a Type tool, else in all the text of the selected type, as one undo step; a break between two words becomes a space → {ids, removed}
- `addon.cleanup.splitText` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {} while a Type tool edits horizontal point or area type: the text before the selected characters, they and the text after become point type objects of their own (at a cursor: before and after it), each where its characters were, as one undo step; spaces at the ends of each piece go, and editing ends with the pieces selected → {ids}
- `addon.cleanup.alignInPlace` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {align: "left"\|"center"\|"right", ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} align every paragraph of each horizontal point type object so, moving the object so its longest line stays where it is (one line: the text doesn't move), as one undo step → {ids (the objects changed)}
- `addon.cleanup.resetTextTransform` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {scale?: bool (false: the horizontal scale stays; true: it goes too, the object's and every character's), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} take the rotation, shear and reflection off each point type object, its size and the centre of its text staying, as one undo step → {ids (the objects changed)}
- `addon.cleanup.replaceFonts` (Object › Plug-ins › Artwork Cleanup): {font?: family (default: the default type font, Source Sans 3; any of its names)} set every character of the document's type (the type in the art of its symbols included) whose font family isn't available in `font`, in the style it has closest to the one each had, as one undo step → {runs (style runs changed), font}
- `addon.color.select` (Object › Plug-ins › Color Effects): {kind: "spotColors" (objects painted with a spot colour swatch, solid or in a gradient's stops) \| "colorEffects" (objects carrying a visible colour adjustment effect, on themselves or on a fill or stroke) \| "rgbImages" \| "cmykImages" \| "grayImages" (embedded or linked images whose pixels are RGB, CMYK, or grayscale or 1-bit)} select those objects among the ones Select All reaches, inside groups too; locked and hidden ones are left out → {count}
- `addon.color.filter` (Object › Plug-ins › Color Effects): {effect: a colour adjustment's effect id (adjust.brightnessContrast, adjust.curves, adjust.hueSaturation, adjust.levels, adjust.shiftToColor, adjust.temperatureTint, addon.color.exposure, addon.color.desaturate, addon.color.duotone or addon.color.invert), params?: {…} (as effect.apply; missing keys take the dialog defaults), fill?: true, stroke?: true, includeImages?: true, includePatterns?: true, ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} recolour the objects and everything inside them as the live effect would draw them, but in their colours, as edit.colors.invert does (fills, strokes, type, gradient meshes, embedded images and pattern tiles), as one undo step; their effects stay. Duotone links a colour to its one tone's swatch as the live effect does → {changed}
- `addon.color.printFixes` (Object › Plug-ins › Color Effects): {scope?: "selection" (the selection, or `ids`; by default, the document while nothing is selected) \| "document" (every layer), inkLimit?: total ink % 1..400 (0 or left out: off), excludePureBlack?: true (black-only and grey colours keep their ink), removeWhiteOverprint?: false (white fills and strokes stop overprinting), overprintBlack?: black % 1..100 (black-only and grey fills and strokes this dark or darker overprint, as edit.colors.overprintBlack; 0 or left out: off), textOnly?: false (Overprint Black on type only), fill?: true, stroke?: true, ids?: [id…]} prepare a CMYK document's inks for print as one undo step: CMYK colours (solid, gradient stops, gradient meshes) with more total ink than inkLimit are scaled down to it (colours of spot swatches and Registration stay), white overprint is removed and black overprint set. Refused in RGB documents (File › Document Color Mode › CMYK Color first) → {changed, inkLimited: paints and meshes scaled, whiteOverprintRemoved: objects, blackOverprinted: objects}
- `addon.color.nearestSwatch` (Object › Plug-ins › Color Effects): {library?: a swatch library's id or name (swatch.library.list: the built-in ones, and the colour books and libraries the user saved or loaded) or "document" (the document's swatches; the default), color?: colour (look this colour up; default: the selection's), fill?: true, stroke?: true (the selected objects' fills and strokes, their type's and those of the objects inside them: solid colours and gradient stops, once each, the first 100), count?: 1..50 (5), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} list the library's colour swatches closest to each colour, closest first, by CIEDE2000 difference (ΔE; under 1 hardly tells apart, about 2 to 3 close). Tint swatches, None and Registration aren't matched → {library, colors: [{values, hex, matches: [{name, values, hex, spot, deltaE}]}]}
- `addon.color.applyNearestSwatch` (Object › Plug-ins › Color Effects): {library?: as addon.color.nearestSwatch (default: "document"), fill?: true, stroke?: true, ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} give each solid colour and gradient stop of the selected objects' fills and strokes (their type's and those of the objects inside them; gradient meshes too) the colour of its nearest swatch in the library, as addon.color.nearestSwatch finds it, as one undo step: a global or spot swatch's colour links to it. A library's swatches the colours take are added to the document's first, as swatch.library.add adds them (a spot colour stays spot); images and patterns keep their colours → {changed: paints changed, swatches: [the document swatches the colours took], added: [swatches added to the document]}
- `addon.color.swatchLegend` (Object › Plug-ins › Color Effects): {names?: [swatch name…] (default: every colour swatch of the document, in panel order), columns?: 1..50 (4), size?: chip side in pt 8..1000 (72), gap?: pt 0..1000 (12), values?: [hex\|rgb\|cmyk\|lab\|gray…] (each at most once; default: each swatch's own colour model; spot swatches also say spot), x?, y? (the legend's top-left; default: the first artboard's, inset by `gap`)} draw a legend of colour swatches: one square chip per swatch filled with it (linked to it), labelled inside with the swatch name and the values asked for, in black on light chips and white on dark ones, as one group in one undo step; the group is selected → {id, count} (#4919)
- `addon.document.saveTimestampedCopy` (Object › Plug-ins › Document Tools): {folder? (default: the document's own folder; needed for a document never saved), format?: vectorcraft\|pdf\|svg\|svgz\|ai\|eps (default: the document's own, else vectorcraft), time?: Unix seconds (default: now)} write a copy of the document named <name>_YYYY-MM-DD_HH-MM.<ext> (the date and time in UTC; -2, -3… added when that name is taken) as File › Save a Copy writes it: the document keeps its path, title and modified state, and no undo step is made. Off where there is no clock (the web) → {path, format, bytes, warnings}
- `addon.document.exportAllOpen` (Object › Plug-ins › Document Tools): {folder, format?: vectorcraft\|pdf\|svg\|svgz\|ai\|eps (default: pdf)} write a copy of every open document, in tab order, to `folder` as File › Save a Copy writes it, each named after its document (<name>.<ext>; -2, -3… added when that name is taken, so no file is replaced), one self-contained file each with all of its art (images embedded or included, every artboard; no remembered save options apply): every document keeps its path, title and modified state, and no undo step is made. A document that can't be written is listed in `failed` and the others are still written; the active document stays active → {files: [{title, path, warnings}], failed: [{title, error}], format}
- `addon.document.symbolsFromLayers` (Object › Plug-ins › Document Tools): {layers?: [layer id…] (top-level layers; default: those highlighted in the Layers panel when two or more are, else every one), replace?: bool (false: the art stays; true: each layer's art is replaced by an instance of its symbol in the same place)} make one symbol of each layer's art as symbol.new makes one (registration in the centre), named after the layer (with a number added when a symbol has that name), as one undo step. Layers with no art are skipped, and with replace so are locked layers; with replace the instances are selected → {symbols: [name…], ids: [instance id…] (with replace), skipped: [{layer, name, reason: "empty"\|"locked"}…]}
- `addon.pathtools.erase`: {points: [[x, y] \| [x, y, pressure 0..1]…] (the brush's path, at most 100000 points), diameter?: pt 0.1..2500 (20; a point's pressure scales it), tolerance?: 1..100 (10; the outline may move up to tolerance × 0.02 pt), ignoreTolerance?: bool (false: every anchor under the brush that can go goes, its neighbours refitted), plain?: bool (false: true deletes them without refitting the handles beside them), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} remove the anchors of the paths under the objects that the brush passes over and that can go while the outline stays within the tolerance, as one undo step; open ends stay → {ids, removed}
- `addon.pathtools.extend`: {id, subpath?: index (0), end: "start"\|"end", length?: pt (negative shortens; never all of it), to?: [x, y] (instead of length: the length that brings the end nearest this point), mode?: "straight"\|"bezier" (stretch the last segment's curve, no anchor added)\|"arc" (along the circle the end curves on, at most half way round) ("straight")} lengthen an open path at an end the way it runs there, or shorten it back along itself, as one undo step; with Keep Shared Points Together on, anchors coincident with an end that moves go with it → {id, length (pt)}
- `addon.pathtools.inspect`: {id, x, y, tolerance?: pt (3; how near an anchor counts as over it)} what the path reads at the point nearest (x, y): {subpath, closed, anchors, length, area (pt², an open subpath closed by a straight line), segment, t, point: [x, y], segmentLength, tangent (degrees counter-clockwise), radius (of curvature, null where straight), anchor?: {index, x, y, smooth, angle (at the anchor, null at an open end), in?: {length, angle}, out?: {length, angle}}}
- `addon.pathtools.setAnchor`: {x?, y?: pt (on the page), inLength?, outLength?: pt (0 retracts), inAngle?, outAngle?: degrees counter-clockwise} move the selected anchors (with their handles) and set their handles, as one undo step: direct-selected anchors, or every anchor of a path selected whole; a smooth anchor's other handle turns with the one set; with Keep Shared Points Together on, anchors coincident with one that moves go with it → {anchors}
- `addon.pathtools.smooth` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {ratio?: 0.1..0.5 (0.3905: a square's corners make a circle)} make the selected anchors smooth: a lone handle is mirrored; without handles, both stand at right angles to the bisector of the angle at the anchor, ratio × the nearer neighbour's distance long; two handles are put in line; an open end without handles aims at its neighbour's handle. One undo step → {anchors (changed)}
- `addon.pathtools.corner` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {retract?: bool (true: the handles go; false: they stay where they are and move apart)} make the selected anchors corners, as one undo step → {anchors (changed)}
- `addon.pathtools.equalize` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {} give each selected anchor's two handles their average length (a missing one is made opposite the other), as one undo step → {anchors (changed)}
- `addon.pathtools.removeKeepShape` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {} remove the direct-selected anchors, refitting the handles beside each so the outline keeps its shape as best it can, as one undo step; open ends stay → {removed}
- `addon.pathtools.addPoints` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {count?: 1..100 (1)} add count anchors to each selected segment (a segment between two selected anchors, or every segment of a path selected whole), splitting it into pieces of equal length; the shape doesn't change. One undo step → {added}
- `addon.pathtools.close` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {mode?: "handles" (keep the ends' handles)\|"straight" (a straight closing segment)\|"smooth" (the ends made smooth), ratio?: 0.1..0.5 (0.3905, for smooth)} close the open subpaths of the selected paths, as one undo step → {closed}
- `addon.pathtools.crossings` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {cut?: bool (false: add anchors where the paths cross; true: also cut them there), selfCrossings?: bool (false: where a path crosses itself too), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} where the paths under the objects cross one another, as one undo step; at most 10000 segments → {ids, crossings (anchors placed)}
- `addon.pathtools.slideAnchor`: {id, subpath?: index (0), anchor: index, t: -0.98..0.98 (where to: positive, a curve parameter on the segment after the anchor; negative, back from the end of the one before)} move an anchor along its path, the segments beside it refitted so the outline stays, as one undo step; ends of open paths don't slide; with Keep Shared Points Together on, anchors coincident with it go with it → {id, strayed (pt)}
- `addon.pathtools.divideDashes` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {dash?: pt 0.01..1000000 (10), gap?: pt 0..1000000 (0: the pieces touch), fit?: bool (false: exact lengths, the last piece of each run what is left; true: fit to corners and ends, every run between corners and ends holding whole dashes, all stretched or squeezed alike so it starts and ends on a dash), removeGaps?: bool (true: the gaps are left empty; false: they stay as pieces too)} cut the selected paths into separate open paths along the curve, each dash long with gap between them and keeping the path's appearance, as one undo step. Only the direct-selected segments (or those between two direct-selected anchors) are divided when there are any, the dash pattern running on across segments that follow one another; the rest of such a path stays, as pieces of its own. The pieces are selected; at most 100000 → {ids, dashes}
- `addon.pathtools.expandDashes` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} turn the dashed stroke of each path and compound path under the objects into separate open paths, one per dash, laid out as the stroke draws them (its dash pattern, offset and Align Dashes to Corners and Path Ends), each keeping the path's appearance with the dashes off, so the gaps are real gaps: a dash running all the way round a closed path stays closed, and with a round or projecting cap a dash of no length (a dot) becomes a path on its point. The top stroke's dashes are the ones expanded; its arrowheads go, and the path's fills stay on it, below its dashes. Paths without a dashed stroke stay as they are. One undo step; the dashes are selected; at most 100000 → {ids, dashes}
- `addon.pathtools.shape.create`: {center: [x, y], kind?: "rectangle"\|"ellipse"\|"polygon" (default)\|"star"\|"ring"\|"arrow"\|"cross"\|"heart"\|"gear"\|"bubble" (a speech bubble), width?, height?: 0.01..16384 pt (100), rotation?: degrees clockwise (0: upright), count?: 3..1000 (a polygon's sides, a star's points, a gear's teeth), a?, b?: the kind's own values (star: inner radius 0.01..1 of the outer, 0.5; ring: hole 0.01..0.99 of the size, 0.5; arrow: head length and shaft width 0.05..0.95, 0.4 and 0.4; cross: bar offset −0.9..0.9 (0, − up) and arm thickness 0.05..0.95 (1/3); heart: notch depth and point roundness 0..1 (0.4 and 0); gear: tooth height 0.01..0.9 (0.2); bubble: tail height 0.05..0.9 (0.25) and tail tip −1..1 across (−0.4)), radius?: corner radius 0..16384 pt (0; no corner rounds past the middle of a side)} draw a parametric shape with the current paint, selected, as one undo step; it stays editable with addon.pathtools.shape.set until its anchors are edited (moving, turning or scaling it keeps it) → {id, shape}
- `addon.pathtools.shape.set`: {id, kind?, center?, width?, height?, rotation?, count?, a?, b?, radius?} change a parametric shape's numbers (as addon.pathtools.shape.create takes them; a new kind takes its own defaults for the values not given), as one undo step; with none, or the same ones, nothing changes → {id, shape (its numbers now)}
- `addon.pathtools.shape.convert` (Object › Plug-ins › Path Precision Tools): {ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} make each plain closed path that is a rectangle, an ellipse or a polygon with equal sides and corners (stretched along its own axes at most) a parametric shape of the same outline, as one undo step → {ids (converted)}
- `addon.pathtools.cornerSet`: {ids?: [id…] (the selection), anchors?: [index…] (of each path's outline with no corners cut; else its direct-selected anchors, or all of them), items?: [{id, anchors?}…] (instead of ids: each path with its own anchors), radius: pt 0..1000000 (drawn no larger than fits), kind?: "round"\|"invertedRound"\|"chamfer" (each corner's own)} cut live corners at the anchors that can have one, as one undo step → {ids, corners (how many)}
- `addon.pathtools.cornerRemove`: {ids?: [id…] (the selection), anchors?: [index…] (of each path's outline with no corners cut; else its direct-selected anchors, or all of them), items?: [{id, anchors?}…] (instead of ids: each path with its own anchors)} make those live corners sharp again, as one undo step → {ids, corners (how many)}
- `addon.pathtools.mergeEdge`: {a, b: two path ids (they needn't be selected), at: [x, y] (where), diameter?: pt 0.25..200 (20; how near `at` the edge is looked for), tolerance?: pt 0..3 (0.002; how near two anchors count as coincident), follow?: "average" (half way between the two)\|"a"\|"b" (keep that path's outline), ends?: bool (false: true makes an end even where the paths could merge)} where the paths touch at two coincident anchors, one each side of `at`, replace the stretches between them with one shared edge; stretches much longer one than the other (by over 10%) or leaving an end at over 120° aren't merged. Otherwise make an end there: two near anchors meet, and an anchor only one path has there is matched by a new one on the other. Each path stays closed or open; one undo step → {a, b, made: "edge"\|"end"\|"nothing" (merged already)}
- `addon.pathtools.movePointsTogether`: {enabled?: bool (default: the opposite of now)} Keep Shared Points Together: whether anchors coincident (within the Shared Edge Merge Tool's `tolerance`) with one that Slide Anchor Along Path, Extend Path or Set Anchor moves move with it, on any editable path (the `together` option of tool `sharedEdge`, kept with the preferences; off by default) → {enabled}
- `addon.pen.append`: {id, subpath?: index (default: the last open subpath), x, y, in?: [x, y], out?: [x, y], segment?: "straight" (the segment into the new anchor is a line: the previous anchor's handle on it goes, and the new anchor's incoming one)\|"curved" (the previous anchor gets a handle on it if it has none: in line with its other handle, or with a straight segment before it)} add an anchor at the end of an open subpath of path id (default: the handles as given, the previous anchor as it is), as one undo step; the new anchor is selected → {anchor}
- `addon.pen.close`: {id, subpath?: index (default: the last open subpath), segment?: "straight"\|"curved" (default: the closing segment takes the handles its ends have)} close an open subpath of two anchors or more, as one undo step
- `addon.pen.segment`: {id, subpath?: index (0), segment: index, curved: bool} make a segment straight (its handles retracted) or curved (each end without a handle on it gets one, in line with its other handle or with a straight segment before it), as one undo step → {changed}
- `addon.pen.removePoint`: {id, subpath?: index (0), anchor: index, plain?: bool (false: the handles beside it are refitted so the outline keeps its shape, and an open end takes its segment with it; true: it goes and the handles beside it stay as they are)} remove one anchor, as one undo step; a subpath keeps two anchors (three when closed)
- `addon.pen.togglePoint`: {id, subpath?: index (0), anchor: index, ratio?: 0.1..0.5 (0.3905)} a smooth anchor becomes a corner with its handles where they are; any other anchor is made smooth by the auto-smooth rule (a lone handle mirrored; without handles both at right angles to the bisector of the angle at it, ratio × the nearer neighbour's distance long). One undo step → {smooth}
- `addon.pen.reshape`: {id, subpath?: index (0), segment: index, t?: 0..1 (where it was grabbed, 0.5; held to 0.05..0.95), dx, dy: pt, mode?: "free" (both handles move, the one nearer the grab more)\|"keepAngles" (each handle keeps its direction)\|"symmetric" (both handles at right angles to the chord, at one length) ("free")} bend a segment so the point grabbed moves by (dx, dy); missing handles grow out of their anchors and a smooth anchor stays smooth. One undo step
- `addon.pen.nextSegment` (Object › Plug-ins › Smart Pen): {curved: bool} whether a click with the Smart Pen Tool adds a curved segment or a straight one (the tool's `next` option, kept with the preferences) → {next: "straight"\|"curved"}
- `addon.pen.closeJoin` (Object › Plug-ins › Smart Pen): {} with exactly two open path ends direct-selected, join them as path.join does; else close the selected open paths keeping their ends' handles, as addon.pathtools.close does. One undo step

## appearance

- `appearance.addFill` (Window › Appearance): {ids?\|id?, target?: "object"\|"contents"} add a fill on top of each selected object's own stack, on an image right below its Contents row (a copy of its top fill; type without one: its characters' fill; else the default fill). With a fill row active (appearance.setActiveItem), no `ids` and no `target: "contents"`, the new fill is a copy of that row, right above it, and becomes the active row
- `appearance.addStroke` (Window › Appearance): {ids?\|id?, target?} add a stroke on top of each selected object's own stack, on an image right below its Contents row (a copy of its top stroke, else the default stroke)
- `appearance.clear` (Window › Appearance): {ids?\|id?, target?} leave each object one empty fill and stroke (None; a group none of its own) and reset its opacity and blend mode
- `appearance.reduceToBasic` (Window › Appearance): {ids?\|id?, target?} keep only one visible fill and stroke (without ids: the Appearance panel's active row in place of the topmost one of its kind; else the topmost), without their own opacity, blend mode or effects (a group keeps no rows it lacked), and drop the object's effects
- `appearance.setItem`: {index: paint-order item index, ids?\|id? (default: the selection), target?: "object"\|"contents", opacity?: 0..100, blend?: name, visible?: bool, weight?: pt (strokes), color?\|none?\|swatch?\|gradient? (as paint.setFill), tint?: 0..100, keepModel?: bool (both as paint.setFill), aligned?: bool (index and item are rows of the first target's stack: each other target edits its equal one, as the Appearance panel does for a mixed selection)} edit one fill/stroke of each target object's own appearance stack
- `appearance.removeItem`: {index \| indices: [..] (paint-order item indices), ids?\|id?, target?, aligned?: bool (index and item are rows of the first target's stack: each other target edits its equal one, as the Appearance panel does for a mixed selection)} remove those fills/strokes from each target object's own stack
- `appearance.addEffect`: {…as effect.apply} same as effect.apply (an alias): {effect: id, params?: {…}, item?: appearance item index\|null, ids?, target?} → {ids, index, item}
- `appearance.duplicateItem` (Window › Appearance): {index \| indices: [..] (paint-order item indices), to?: paint-order index of the copy (with several, of the lowest copy: they land together in their order; default: each right above its item; not with aligned rows of a mixed selection), contents?: count (with `to`: how many items paint below the Contents (Characters) row afterwards; default: it stays between the same items), ids?\|id?, target?, aligned?: bool (index and item are rows of the first target's stack: each other target edits its equal one, as the Appearance panel does for a mixed selection)} copy fills/strokes with their effects, as Alt-dragging rows places them
- `appearance.moveItem`: {from: paint-order item index \| "contents", indices?: [..] (several items instead of from: they move as one block in their order, the lowest landing at `to`; a block landing next to the Contents row keeps its lowest item's side), to, contents?: count, ids?\|id?, target?, aligned?: bool (refused when the targets' appearances differ)} move fill/stroke `from` to paint-order index `to`; on a group, layer or type the Contents (Characters) row stays between the same items (a moved item landing next to it keeps its side) unless `contents` says how many items paint below it afterwards. from "contents": move that row so `to` items paint below the members (characters) and the rest above → {index: where the item landed \| contents: the row's slot}
- `appearance.copyFrom`: {source: id, ids?, pickUp?, apply? (trees as eyedropper.setOptions, merged over the Eyedropper Options for this call), reverse?: bool, append?: bool, defaultsOnly?: bool} copy the attributes both picked up and applied (by default the whole appearance stack, opacity and blend mode, and from type to type the character and paragraph attributes) from `source` to ids (default: highlighted characters while editing type, otherwise the object selection; highlighted characters reject enabled non-neutral object transparency), the fill, stroke and weight also to the paint defaults; `defaultsOnly` updates only paint defaults and cannot be combined with ids, reverse or append; `reverse` (Alt-click) copies from the first selected object (or ids) onto `source` instead, or with nothing selected what the Eyedropper picked up last (a click without Alt); `append` (Shift+Alt-click) adds the source's fills, strokes and effects on top of each target's stack; placed gradients land at the same place relative to each target's bounds (defaults: fitted to new art) → {ids}
- `appearance.setActiveItem`: {index: paint-order item index in the first selected object's stack \| null} make that fill/stroke row the target of the paint.setFill/setStroke, stroke.set/setAdvanced, paint.editGradient/setGradientGeom, transparency.set and effect.* calls that omit `item` (a fill row brings the Fill proxy forward, a stroke row the Stroke proxy); null or any selection change clears it → {index}
- `appearance.showAllHidden` (Window › Appearance): {ids?\|id?, target?} make every hidden fill, stroke and effect (the object's and each item's) of each selected object visible again; errors when nothing is hidden → {ids}
- `appearance.targetContents`: {ids?\|id?, target?: "object"\|"contents"} select the members of the selected groups and layers (or `ids`), as double-clicking the Appearance panel's Contents row does: the panel then lists, and appearance edits change, their own appearance; errors when none has members → {ids}
- `appearance.transfer`: {source: id, target: id, copy?: bool} give `target` (a layer, group or object) the appearance (fills, strokes, effects) and transparency (opacity, blend mode, isolation, knockout) of `source`, as dragging a target circle onto another in the Layers panel does; the source is left with a cleared appearance (as appearance.clear) unless `copy` (Alt-drag). Opacity masks stay; placed gradients keep their place relative to each object's bounds → {source, target}
- `appearance.setNewArtBasic` (Window › Appearance): {on?: bool (omitted: toggle)} the Appearance panel option (the preference newArtBasic, on by default): on, new art takes one fill and stroke (with the Stroke panel's options); off, the whole appearance of the last selected object (every fill, stroke and effect, opacity and blend mode) → {on}
- `appearance.newArt`: {} what the next object drawn gets → {basic: New Art Has Basic Appearance, appearance: {items, effects} (the model's JSON; placed gradients relative to the unit box), opacity: 0..100, blend, graphicStyle: the style it is linked to or null, inherited: taken from the last selection}

## artboard

- `artboard.new` (Window › Artboards): {x?, y?, width?, height?, name?, color?: color (its background color, as artboard.setProps takes it; default: none), showCenterMark?: bool, showCrossHairs?: bool, showVideoSafeAreas?: bool (as artboard.setProps takes them; default false), videoRulerPixelAspectRatio?: number 0.1–10 (as artboard.setProps takes it; default 1)} (default place: right of the last)
- `artboard.delete` (Window › Artboards): {index \| indices: [index…]} delete artboards (with their guides) in one step; one is always left, and a locked one is an error
- `artboard.deleteEmpty` (Window › Artboards): {} delete every artboard with no artwork on it (no object, hidden, locked and template art included, whose visual bounds meet or touch it, clip groups and clipping layers by their members inside the clip; guides don't count), with its guides, in one step; locked artboards stay; when every artboard is empty and none is locked, artboard 1 stays (with a locked one left, artboard 1 can go) → {deleted: [index…]} (the indices before deleting)
- `artboard.setProps` (Window › Artboards): {index \| artboards: [{index, name?, color?, locked?, showCenterMark?, showCrossHairs?, showVideoSafeAreas?, videoRulerPixelAspectRatio?, x?, y?, width?, height?}…] (several artboards in one undo step, each with its own fields; the art inside each before any of them changes goes with it, and an object inside several goes with the first listed that moves or scales its art), name?, color?: color ("#rrggbb", {c,m,y,k}, … as the paint commands take: the background color the canvas draws the artboard's paper in, multiplied with the Simulate Colored Paper color while that is on; exports and print leave it out) \| null (no background color), locked?: bool (the Artboards panel's lock column: a locked artboard keeps its rectangle (a new x, y, width or height is an error unless locked is false), can't be moved, deleted or cut, and Rearrange and Fit to Artwork Bounds leave it as it is; the art on it (each object of a layer or sublayer whose bounds with strokes overlap it, a group as one object) acts as locked art until it is unlocked: clicks, marquees, select.set, select.all and the tools pass over it (art drawn or pasted onto it stays selected and editable until a selection leaves it out or it is moved off the locked artboards), it is deselected, and when another artboard moves or scales it stays unless lockedAndHidden), showCenterMark?: bool (Artboard Options' Show Center Mark: the canvas draws a mark at the artboard's center), showCrossHairs?: bool (Show Cross Hairs: the canvas draws a line from the middle of each side through the center), showVideoSafeAreas?: bool (Show Video Safe Areas: the canvas draws the action-safe area 3.5% and the title-safe area 5% in from each edge, as EBU R 95 sets them; the three are screen-only, as the artboard's edges are: exports and print leave them out), videoRulerPixelAspectRatio?: number 0.1–10 (Video Ruler Pixel Aspect Ratio: the width of one video pixel over its height, which View › Rulers › Show Video Rulers counts the artboard in; default 1, square pixels), x?, y?, width?, height?, moveArt?: bool (default false; a position-only change moves the contained art with its artboard), scaleArt?: bool (Scale Artwork with Artboard: resized, the artboard takes the art fully inside it (locked and hidden art only with lockedAndHidden?: bool, default prefs moveLockedWithArtboard) and its guides from its old rectangle onto the new one, each side by its own ratio; strokes/corners/patterns as object.scale), strokes?: bool, corners?: bool, patterns?: bool (the three as object.scale, for art that moves or scales with the artboard), lockedAndHidden?: bool (locked and hidden art moves along; default: the preference; recorded in the journal so a replay matches)} → null, or with scaleArt {scaled: the art scaled}
- `artboard.fitToArt` (Object › Artboards): {index?: n \| indices?: [n…] (0-based; the app passes the selected artboards: the Artboard tool's, else the active one; default: the first)} each artboard fits the art on it that shows (with its strokes and effects; hidden objects and layers, guides and template layers don't count), in one undo step: an object counts for every artboard its bounds overlap, and a group or clipping layer counts as one object; an artboard with no art, or a locked one, stays as it is, and the command fails when every artboard it names is locked or none has art
- `artboard.fitToSelection` (Object › Artboards): {index?: n (0-based; the app passes the active artboard; default: the first)} the artboard fits the selected art (with its strokes); a locked artboard stays as it is (an error when the fit would change it)
- `artboard.move`: {index \| indices: [index…], dx, dy, moveArt?: bool, copy?: bool, patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} move artboards (and the art fully inside them; locked and hidden art, and art also on another locked artboard, only with prefs moveLockedWithArtboard), several in one step (an error when one is locked); `copy` (Alt-drag) leaves them and moves copies, which start unlocked → {index (the first), indices: the artboards moved or the copies, moved: the art moved or the copies}
- `artboard.convertToArtboards` (Object › Artboards): {} turn each selected object's bounds into a new artboard (the objects are removed) → {artboards}
- `artboard.rearrange` (Object › Artboards): {layout?: gridByRow\|gridByColumn\|row\|column (gridByRow; byColumn?: true is gridByColumn), order?: leftToRight\|rightToLeft (leftToRight: right-to-left mirrors the order along the rows), columns?: n (2; the rows for gridByColumn; row and column ignore it), spacing?: pt (20), moveArtwork?: true (each object of a layer or sublayer moves with the artboard its center is on; locked and hidden art, and art on a locked artboard, only with prefs moveLockedWithArtboard), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), byColumn?: bool (an older way to ask for layout gridByColumn)} lay the artboards out in their order, the grid's top-left at the first artboard's; a locked artboard stays where it is → {artboards, rows, columns}
- `artboard.reorder`: {index, to} change an artboard's number
- `artboard.duplicate` (Window › Artboards): {index?: n \| indices?: [n…] (default: the artboards the Artboard tool has selected), art?: bool (default: the Artboard tool's moveArt option), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} a copy of each artboard, the copies placed together right of the last artboard as they lay, with copies of the art fully inside each when `art` (locked and hidden art only with prefs moveLockedWithArtboard), in one undo step → {index: the first copy's index, indices: the copies' indices, ids: the art's copies}
- `artboard.copy`: {index? \| indices?, art?} (defaults as artboard.duplicate) put the artboards, and the art fully inside each when `art`, on the clipboard: edit.paste adds a copy of each, together right of the last artboard as they lay, edit.pasteInPlace/InFront/InBack where they were, in this or another document (one undo step; edit.pasteOnAllArtboards pastes only the art); edit.copy runs this while the Artboard tool is chosen → {copied: objects}
- `artboard.cut`: {index? \| indices?, art?} artboard.copy, then delete the artboards and that art in one undo step (one artboard is always left: the cut fails when it names every artboard, or a locked one; art also on another locked artboard stays, and is left off the clipboard, unless prefs moveLockedWithArtboard); edit.cut runs this while the Artboard tool is chosen → {copied: objects}

## assets

- `assets.add` (Object › Collect for Export): {ids?: [id…] (default: the selected objects; a layer id collects the art under it, including in its sublayers), multiple?: true (an asset per object; false: one asset of them all), name?: (the asset's name, when it makes one)} collect art as assets for Window › Asset Export and Export for Screens' Assets tab, named after the object (else Asset 1, Asset 2…); art already collected as such an asset keeps it → {assets: [asset id…] (in order), added: n}. Assets follow their art: moving it moves the crop, deleting it removes it from the asset
- `assets.remove`: {assets: [asset id…]} take assets out of the Asset Export panel (their art stays) → {removed: n}
- `assets.rename`: {asset: id, name} rename an asset (its files' names)
- `assets.list`: {} → {assets: [{id, name, nodes: [id…], bounds: [x, y, width, height] \| null (the crop: the bounds the renderer measures for what its art draws, effects included)}]} the Asset Export panel's assets, in order
- `assets.export`: {assets?: [asset id…] (default all), folder?, zip?, openLocation?, formats?, preset?, settings?, prefix?, subfolders?, antiAlias?} each asset's art alone, cropped to it, in every format row: document.exportForScreens {assets} at the document's export settings (document.exportSettings) under the ones given; with subfolders false every file goes into the folder itself, mobile and density rows too → what document.exportForScreens returns (no folder: the files, or one zip, as dataBase64). Leaves the remembered settings as they are (assets.settings.set changes them)
- `assets.settings.set`: {exportPreset?: (a preset of document.exportPresets.list: its formats or preset, its format settings over the saved ones, its prefix (none when it has none), its folders (or subfolders: rows or none) with subfolders on for rows, and antiAlias; the other keys go over it), formats?, preset?: mobile\|density\|"" (none), settings?, prefix?, subfolders?, antiAlias?} change the export settings the Asset Export panel and Export for Screens share (document.exportSettings; the values of document.exportForScreens; null removes one): formats replace a preset, a preset replaces the formats → {settings}. Not an undo step

## attributes

- `attributes.info`: {ids?\|id?, item?, target?: "object"\|"contents"} → {ids, overprintFill, overprintStroke, showCenter, imageMap: "none"\|"rectangle"\|"polygon", url, note, fillRule: "nonZero"\|"evenOdd" (of the paths and compound paths in them), reversed (their subpaths run counter-clockwise on screen: Reverse Path Direction On), recentUrls: [newest first]} the Attributes panel's values for `ids` or the selection, overprint aimed as object.setOverprint aims; a value is null where they differ (or nothing has a fill, stroke or path)
- `attributes.set`: {overprintFill?: bool, overprintStroke?: bool (aimed as object.setOverprint's fill and stroke, item? too), showCenter?: bool (the canvas shows the selected object's centre point), imageMap?: "none"\|"rectangle"\|"polygon", url?: string ("" removes it; SVG export links the object), note?: string, ids?\|id?, item?: appearance item index\|null, target?: "object"\|"contents"} set the Attributes panel's values on ids or the selection, as one undo step; a URL given joins the recent URLs (attributes.info) → {changed: objects}

## brush

- `brush.list`: {} → {brushes: [{name, type}], current}
- `brush.get`: {name} → the brush definition (JSON)
- `brush.selectionArt`: {ids?\|id?} → {art: the object brush.new takes as a new scatter or art brush's art and as a new pattern brush's side tile (several objects become one group), or null when nothing is selected or ids name no object}
- `brush.apply`: {name, ids?\|id?} apply a brush to the strokes of the selected paths (adds a stroke if missing) and make it current → {ids}
- `brush.remove`: {ids?\|id?} remove brushes from the selected strokes → {ids}
- `brush.setCurrent`: {name: string\|null} the brush the Paintbrush tool paints with (not an undo step)
- `brush.new`: {type: calligraphic\|scatter\|art\|pattern\|bristle, name? (trimmed; blank: New <Type> Brush; a bristle brush name, with the number a taken name gets, has at most 31 characters), params?: {…definition fields}, ids?\|id?} new brush; art/scatter/pattern brushes take the selected art (pattern: side tile) unless params give it → {name}
- `brush.delete`: {name} \| {names: [name…]} delete brushes; strokes using them become plain strokes
- `brush.move`: {name? \| names?: [brush names of one type, in order], to?: index among the brushes of that type in brush.list order (they go before the brush of that type now there that isn't moving; default: after the last one), type?: calligraphic\|scatter\|art\|pattern\|bristle (the type they go into; default: theirs)} reorder brushes within their type, as one undo step (none when the order stays as it was); brushes of different types, or a type that isn't theirs, are refused (brushes move only within their type) → {moved}
- `brush.duplicate`: {name, newName? (trimmed; blank: <name> copy)} \| {names: [name…]} → {name} \| {names}
- `brush.unused`: {} → {names: [the brushes no stroke paints with, in library order]} (the Brushes panel selects them): strokes in the art (opacity masks included), symbols, graph designs, pattern tiles (with the tiles that pattern editing's Cancel puts back) and graphic styles count, and so do strokes in the art of a brush that is used
- `brush.options`: {name, params?: {…fields to change (brush.get shows them; values outside their ranges are clamped). calligraphic: angle −180..180°, roundness 0..100%, size 0..1296 pt, variation: [angle°, roundness%, size pt], modes: [fixed\|random\|pressure\|tilt\|bearing\|rotation for angle, roundness, size] (pressure goes from value − variation at the lightest pen pressure to value + variation at the heaviest; tilt from value − variation with the pen lying flat to value + variation with it upright; bearing and rotation add variation × the pen's bearing (which way it leans, counter-clockwise from leaning right) or its barrel's counter-clockwise rotation over 180°; the angle turns on past ±180°). scatter: size [a, b] 1..10000%, spacing [a, b] 1..10000%, scatter [a, b] −1000..1000%, rotation [a, b] −180..180°, modes: [fixed\|random\|pressure\|tilt\|bearing\|rotation for size, spacing, scatter, rotation] (fixed: a; random: between a and b; pressure: a at the lightest pen pressure to b at the heaviest; tilt: a with the pen lying flat to b upright; bearing and rotation: a at −180° to b at 180°), rotation_relative_to_path. art: width 1..1000%, width_mode: null (fixed)\|pressure, width_max 1..1000% (pressure: width at the lightest pen pressure to width_max at the heaviest, half way on a stroke drawn without pressure), scale: {mode: proportional\|stretch\|betweenGuides, start, end: 0..1 of the art length}, direction: leftToRight\|rightToLeft\|topToBottom\|bottomToTop, flip_along, flip_across, overlap. pattern: scale 1..10000%, spacing 0..10000%, fit: stretch\|addSpace\|approximate, flip_along, flip_across, outer_corner and inner_corner: corner tile art or null, outer_source and inner_source: what that kind of corner draws without its art: none\|autoCentered\|autoBetween\|autoSliced\|autoOverlap (a tile made from the side tile), or null (the other kind of corner's art); side_tile, outer_corner_tile, inner_corner_tile, start_tile, end_tile: a document pattern name, original, or none (side refuses none); corner tiles also take autoCentered\|autoBetween\|autoSliced\|autoOverlap. Each tile also accepts {pattern: name} to select a swatch whose name is a reserved choice. scatter, art and pattern: colorization: {method: none\|tints\|tintsAndShades\|hueShift, key: colour (hueShift: the art colour that becomes the stroke colour; without a key: the solid color the art's fills and strokes use most, black when they use none)}; colorization and the art brush's scale change only the fields given}, newName? (trimmed; blank: the name is kept; a new bristle brush name has at most 31 characters, and a longer name the brush has is kept)} edit a brush definition (strokes using it update) → {name}
- `brush.freehand`: {…params as path.freehand, brush} paint a freehand stroke with a brush → {id}
- `brush.libraries`: {} → {libraries: [{id, name, category: "builtIn"\|"user" (User Defined: the .vectorcraft files in the user library folder, rescanned now)\|"loaded" (brush.loadLibrary), count, path?}], userFolder} every brush library the library panel opens, in menu order
- `brush.library`: {library: id or name} → {id, name, category, brushes: [{name, type}] (as brush.list)}
- `brush.addFromLibrary` (Window › Brush Libraries): {library: id or name, name? \| names?: [brush names in the library] (default: all of it), apply?: false (also apply the first one to paths, as brush.apply does), ids?\|id? (the paths apply takes; default the selected ones), swatchConflict? (as edit.paste)} copy library brushes into the document's Brushes as one undo step (with the apply; named Apply Brush when no brush is added and it only applies one), with the resources their art uses (patterns, symbols, swatches, graphic styles, images). A brush the document already has (same name and definition) isn't added again; a name another brush has gets a number ("Ribbon 2"). The first one becomes the current brush → {library, names: [each brush's name in the document], added: [names added], existing: [names already there], applied: [ids of the paths it was applied to]}
- `brush.saveLibrary` (Window › Brushes): {path?, names?: [brush names] (default: all), name?: library name (default: the document's), user?: false (save into the user library folder, listed under User Defined)} save the document's brushes, with the resources their art uses, as a brush library: a .vectorcraft document holding only them → {path, count, library?: id when saved to the user folder}; without path or user → {dataBase64: the file's bytes, count}
- `brush.loadLibrary` (Window › Brush Libraries): {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, name?: file name (default: the path's)} load the brushes of any document VectorCraft opens (see document.formats; one without brushes of its own has the default ones), a saved brush library included, for the library panel (Window → Brush Libraries lists it until the app quits; a file of the user library folder, or a file with the same bytes as one there, opens as that User Defined library) → {library: id, name, count}

## charStyle

- `charStyle.list`: {} → {styles: [{name, attrs, uses}]}
- `charStyle.new` (Window › Type › Character Styles): {name? (trimmed, as rename trims `to`), attrs?: {…} (default: selected text), id?, start?: byte, end?: byte} → {name}; a nonempty character range samples its first selected character; start alone keeps insertion affinity; paragraph styles sample start
- `charStyle.apply`: {name, clearOverrides?, ids?\|id?, start?, end? (id with start and end: a Type tool range; character styles style it, paragraph styles apply to the paragraphs it touches)} → {count}
- `charStyle.redefine` (Window › Type › Character Styles): {name, id?, start?: byte, end?: byte} from selected text; nonempty character ranges sample their first selected character, start alone keeps insertion affinity, paragraph styles sample start; users keep their overrides
- `charStyle.setAttrs`: {name, attrs: {…}} replace the style's attributes; users keep their overrides
- `charStyle.duplicate` (Window › Type › Character Styles): {name} → {name}
- `charStyle.rename`: {name, to}
- `charStyle.delete` (Window › Type › Character Styles): {name} \| {names: [name…]} delete styles in one undo step (text keeps its look; never the Normal style)
- `charStyle.unused` (Window › Type › Character Styles): {} → {names: [the styles no text uses, in panel order; never the Normal style]} (the panel highlights them)
- `charStyle.edit`: {name, to, attrs: {…}} replace name and sparse attributes in one undo step; users keep local overrides

## clipboard

- `clipboard.exportSvg`: {} → {svg} the copied objects as standalone SVG (null when the clipboard is empty)
- `clipboard.importSvg`: {svg \| dataBase64 (SVG or SVGZ bytes), center?: [x, y]} replace the clipboard with the SVG's objects (and the images, patterns and symbols they use), centred on `center` (default: the first artboard) → {count}; then run edit.pasteInPlace
- `clipboard.conflicts`: {} → {swatches: [{name, document, clipboard, spot}]} the global or spot swatches the copied objects use whose name the active document gives another colour (`document`/`clipboard`: #rrggbb): Paste asks Merge or Add for each (pass the answer as edit.paste* {swatchConflict}). Pasting back into the document the objects came from raises none
- `clipboard.exportPng`: {scale?: 1 (pixels per point, up to 64)} → {dataBase64, width, height (px), warnings} the copied objects cropped to their visual bounds, transparent around them (dataBase64 null when the clipboard is empty); warnings as for document.export (an image that couldn't be decoded, type in the fallback font or that doesn't fit its frame)
- `clipboard.exportPdf`: {} → {dataBase64, warnings} a one-page PDF of the copied objects, the page their visual bounds (dataBase64 null when the clipboard is empty); warnings as for document.export
- `clipboard.exportText`: {} → {text} the copied type objects' text, one object per line (null unless every copied object is type, or a group of type)
- `clipboard.flavours`: {} → {flavours: [mime…]} what Copy offers other apps for the current clipboard, best first, by the Clipboard Handling preferences: text/plain (a type-only copy's text, else the SVG markup with copyAsSvg when the images it embeds total at most 1 MiB), image/svg+xml (copyAsSvg), application/pdf (copyAsPdf), image/png (always; PDF and PNG only for objects with an area); the app publishes them on Copy and Cut
- `clipboard.importImage`: {dataBase64 (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, TIFF or BMP bytes), mime?: the type the system clipboard gave (an image/… type; the format is read from the bytes), center?: [x, y]} replace the clipboard with the image, embedded at 100% of its physical size (its resolution, else 72 ppi), centred on `center` (default: the first artboard) → {count, width, height (pt), pixelWidth, pixelHeight}; then run edit.paste. A PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP image whose pixels can't be read (a file cut short) is an error
- `clipboard.importText`: {text, center?: [x, y]} replace the clipboard with point text of `text` (one paragraph per line; trailing line breaks dropped) in the default type style, centred on `center` (default: the first artboard) → {count}; then run edit.paste
- `clipboard.importPdf`: {dataBase64, page?: 1, password? (an encrypted PDF), editingData?: false (true: the data is a .ai or .ait file, read as document.open reads it; when document.open would use its editing data, every object of that data comes in, each layer's objects in stacking order and a hidden layer's objects hidden; else page `page`), center?: [x, y]} replace the clipboard with the objects of one PDF page, or of a .ai file's editing data (with the images, patterns and swatches they use; a .ai saved without PDF compatibility: every object of its editing data, as document.open reads it, with or without editingData), centred on `center` (default: the first artboard) → {count, warnings}; then run edit.paste
- `clipboard.importEmf`: {dataBase64 (EMF or WMF bytes), center?: [x, y]} replace the clipboard with the picture's objects (paths, clipping groups, images, point type; the images they use), centred on `center` (default: the first artboard) → {count, warnings}; records VectorCraft doesn't read are skipped with one warning; then run edit.paste
- `clipboard.importEps`: {dataBase64 (EPS or other PostScript bytes), center?: [x, y]} replace the clipboard with the picture's objects as document.open reads an EPS (editable paths, type and images, with the images, patterns and swatches they use; a hidden layer's objects hidden), centred on `center` (default: the first artboard) → {count, warnings}; then run edit.paste

## color

- `color.harmony`: {color, rule: complementary\|complementary2\|splitComplementary\|leftComplement\|rightComplement\|analogous\|analogous2\|monochromatic\|shades\|triad\|triad2\|triad3\|tetrad\|tetrad2\|tetrad3\|compound\|compound2\|highContrast\|highContrast2\|highContrast3\|pentagram (or its label), steps?: 1..20 (4), variation?: "tintsShades"\|"warmCool"\|"vividMuted", amount?: 0..100 (50; how far the outermost steps go), limitTo?: swatch library id or name; or "document" (the document's swatches): every colour snaps to its nearest colour there (ΔE 2000)} the Color Guide for a base colour → {rule, colors: ["#rrggbb", the base first], grid: [per colour, 2·steps+1 variations from shades/cool/muted to tints/warm/vivid with the colour itself in the centre]}
- `color.loadProfile`: {path: .icc/.icm file of at most 32 MB} register an ICC profile (native only). The desktop app and vectorcraft-cli keep a copy of the file in the `Color Profiles` folder next to the preferences and register it again at every start (not with VECTORCRAFT_NO_PREFS, or in a confined `mcp --automation-*-root` server: there it lasts until the process ends) → {name, kind, remembered: whether a copy was kept}
- `color.removeProfile`: {name} forget a profile color.loadProfile loaded, and the copy kept of it (built-in profiles and the working spaces stay) → {removed: name}
- `color.convert`: {color, to: "rgb"\|"cmyk"\|"gray"\|"lab", intent?} → {model, values, hex, lab: [L,a,b], outOfGamut, deltaE}
- `color.gamutCheck`: {ids?\|id?, colors?: [color]} colours that can't be printed in the working CMYK (selection, or the whole document when nothing is selected) → {checked, outOfGamut: [{hex, deltaE, count}]}
- `color.plates`: {} → {on, plates: [{name, spot, visible, rgb}]}

## colorTheme

- `colorTheme.list`: {} the saved colour themes, in order → {themes: [{name, colors: ["#rrggbb"], keys: [colour keys, exact in each colour's model], rule?}]}
- `colorTheme.save` (Window › Color Themes): {colors: [1–5 colours] \| color + rule (a harmony rule id or label, see color.harmony: its 5-colour theme from that base colour, base first), rule?: the rule it was made with, name?: (default "Theme N"), replace?: a saved theme's name (overwrite that theme in place, e.g. after editing it; it may be renamed), color?: one colour (as colors: [colour])} save a colour theme to the local theme library (the preferences) → {name, count: themes saved}
- `colorTheme.delete` (Window › Color Themes): {name} delete a saved colour theme → {deleted}
- `colorTheme.addToSwatches` (Window › Color Themes): {name} add a saved theme's colours to the Swatches panel as a colour group named after it (as swatch.newGroup), as one undo step → {name: the group's, swatches: [names]}

## command

- `command.batch`: {label?, commands: [{command, params}], artboard?: index (the active artboard; the app passes it: each edit.pasteInPlace, edit.pasteInFront, edit.pasteInBack, object.align, object.distribute, object.distributeSpacing, select.allOnArtboard or symbol.edit step that names none gets it, and each artboard.fitToArt or artboard.fitToSelection step that names no index gets it as index, or the last artboard of a document with fewer)} run several commands as ONE undo step (one in each document they edit: steps may open, switch, close or revert documents); stops at the first error and rolls everything back, documents included

## css

- `css.selection`: {ids?\|id?: [id…] (default: the selection), units?: px\|pt\|mm\|cm\|in (default px; 1 px = 1 pt, as in SVG export), position?: false (position: absolute, left and top from the artboard's top-left), dimensions?: true (width, height), unnamed?: true (unnamed objects too, classes named after their kind; false: named objects only), rasterize?: false (art CSS can't describe as background-image: url(<class>.png), its picture, which css.export writes)} the CSS web pages style the objects with, one rule per object back to front (layers and plain groups stand for what they hold): a rectangle's or ellipse's fill as background-color or linear-/radial-gradient(), stroke as border, corners as border-radius; type's font, color, spacing and alignment; opacity, mix-blend-mode, shadows and glows (box-shadow, text-shadow), blur (filter) → {css, rules: [{id, selector, css, unsupported?: why CSS can't describe the art exactly (without rasterize it is written as its box), image?: the PNG it refers to}], skipped: n unnamed objects left out, warnings}
- `css.generate`: {…options as css.selection} css.selection of every object in the document
- `css.export`: {path?, scope?: selection\|all (default selection; ids? picks the objects), …options as css.selection, id?: object id (default: the selection)} write the CSS (css.selection, or css.generate for all) to path and, with rasterize, each picture its rules refer to next to it (PNG of the object's visual bounds, 1 px per point) → {path, bytes, rules: n, images: [path…], warnings}; no path → {data: the CSS, name: <document>.css, rules, images: [{name, dataBase64}], warnings}; warnings are css.selection's and the pictures' as document.export gives them (an image that couldn't be decoded, type in the fallback font or that doesn't fit its frame), each once

## dataset

- `dataset.new` (Window › Variables): {name, values?: {variable: value}} add a dataset
- `dataset.set` (Window › Variables): {name, newName?, values?: {variable: value}} replace a dataset's values; a variable left out loses its value in this row. `newName` also renames the dataset in the same undo step, refused when another dataset has that name → {name, values, removed}
- `dataset.rename` (Window › Variables): {name, newName} rename a dataset
- `dataset.capture` (Window › Variables): {name?} record what the bound objects hold right now as a new dataset (Data Set 1…), and make it the active one; a frame of threaded text holds its whole story, every frame's text in thread order
- `dataset.update` (Window › Variables): {all?: bool} write what the bound objects hold now into the active dataset, for each variable the dataset holds; a variable whose objects report no value (locked, inside a locked layer or group, on a locked artboard, or gone) keeps the dataset's value. `all: true` also adds every other variable the objects report a value for; `left` names the variables it didn't write → {values, left}
- `dataset.delete` (Window › Variables): {names?} delete datasets (default: the active one)
- `dataset.list`: {} → datasets with their values, the active one, and which rows the art still matches
- `dataset.select` (Window › Variables): {name} apply a dataset in one undo step (none when it leaves the document as it was), deselecting every selected object left hidden or locked (the key object too); a text value bound to a frame of threaded text replaces the whole story → {applied, skipped, overflow?: [id…] (area type and type on a path whose text it rewrote and that no longer fits, the story's last frame for threaded text; left out when all fits), warning? (when no object is bound to a variable that the dataset has a value for, so nothing was applied)}
- `dataset.next` (Window › Variables): {} apply the next dataset, wrapping
- `dataset.prev` (Window › Variables): {} apply the previous dataset, wrapping
- `dataset.import` (Window › Variables): {path? \| data? \| dataBase64? \| csv?} load datasets from CSV, comma- or tab-separated, in UTF-16 with a byte-order mark, UTF-8 or else Windows-1252 (header: name, then variable names; one row per dataset; without the name column every header is a variable and the rows take the first free names Data Set 1, 2…). A header that names no variable defines one, unbound: `#Name` defines a visibility variable, `@Name` defines an image variable, and a header without a prefix defines a text variable. A prefixed header whose name is a variable of another kind, and a `%` header that isn't a variable's exact name (graph data files), are refused, and so are two headers that name one variable and a header that would define a variable while the variables are locked. Rows whose name already exists are replaced → {imported, updated, names, defined}
- `dataset.export` (Window › Variables): {path?} write every dataset as CSV (header: name, then one column per variable: `#Name` for a visibility variable, `@Name` for an image variable, and the name alone for a text or graph variable, as dataset.import reads them). Refused when the document has more than 1000 data sets or 256 variables, the most dataset.import reads, or when a variable's header is another variable's name (a visibility variable `Show` next to a text variable `#Show`) → {path, datasets, warning?}; without path → {csv, datasets, warning?}. `warning` names the variables whose header or values make a document without them refuse the file (a % header, a # header whose values aren't true or false); otherwise it names the variables such a document doesn't read back with their kind (text variables whose names start with # or @, graph variables)

## dimension

- `dimension.create`: {mode?: linear\|angular\|radius\|diameter, a?: [x,y], b?: [x,y], vertex?: [x,y], source?: {kind: segment, edge: {node,subpath,segment}}\|{kind: angle, first: edge, second: edge}\|{kind: circle, node}\|{kind: arc, edge}\|{kind: points, a?, b?, vertex?: {node,subpath,anchor}}, offset?: pt, options?: {units: in\|mm\|pt\|pc\|px\|cm\|m\|ft\|yd, precision: 0-6, scale: drawing:real, hideUnits, lineWeight, lineType: solid\|dashed\|dotted, lineColor: [r,g,b], arrowStyle: filled\|open\|tick\|none, arrowScale: %, extensionWeight, extensionType, extensionColor, extensionOffset, extensionLength, hideExtensions, fontFamily, fontStyle, fontSize, textColor, textPosition: away\|between, textAlignment: left\|center\|right, textOffset}} → {id,label}; linked measurements follow source edits
- `dimension.update`: {ids?: [dimension ids] (default selection), options?: dimension options, offset?: pt} restyle or move existing dimensions → {ids}
- `dimension.applyToAll`: {id?: dimension id (default the first selected dimension)} gives every dimension in the document that dimension's style (its scale too), as one undo step → {ids}

## document

- `document.activate`: {index}
- `document.inspect`: {depth?, childLimit?, ids?: [id…] (list just these nodes, of any level, in this order, under `nodes` in place of `layers`; an unknown id is an error; at most 10000), kind?: panel label such as Layer; Group; Path; Type or Image (exact, ignoring case; list every such node under `nodes`, each with `path`, its ancestor ids with the layer first, searching the subtrees of `ids` when given), limit?: at most this many entries of the list, offset?: entries skipped first (default 0; with `ids`, `kind`, `limit` or `offset` the reply adds `total`, the entries before paging)} → layer tree (each node: id, name, kind, visible, locked, bounds, opacity (0–1) and opacityPct (0–100 %, the scale object.setProps takes) when below 1, blend when not Normal, fill, stroke and strokeWidth of objects and compound paths; a compound path's members have compoundMember: true instead of paint, which is the compound's), artboards, selection, history (a sliced tree reports childCount); without the scope options the reply is the whole layer tree
- `document.node`: {id, summary?: compact summary, depth?: child levels in the summary (default all), childLimit?: children shown per node (default all; a level that shows fewer reports childCount)} → one object
- `document.find`: {name?: substring of the Layers panel name, kind?: panel label such as Group; Path; Type or Image (exact), text?: substring of type content, within?: [x, y, width, height] (an object's geometric bounds inside this rectangle, edges included; document points as document.inspect's bounds; layers never match this or the size filters), intersects?: [x, y, width, height] (bounds touching or overlapping it), minWidth?, maxWidth?, minHeight?, maxHeight?: pt (the bounds' size), minThickness?, maxThickness?: pt (the smaller of the bounds' width and height), minArea?, maxArea?: pt² (the bounds' width × height), minAnchors?, maxAnchors?: anchor points of a path (a compound path's members together; other objects never match), select?: bool (also select the matches, leaving out hidden and locked ones, those inside another match, and a compound path's members unless the compound path is isolated; Select > Reselect repeats it), limit?: max matches listed (default 100, 0 counts only)} → {matches: [{id, name, kind, path}], total, selected (with select: the count selected)}; at least one filter, all of them matching
- `document.json`: {} → complete document model
- `document.setUnits` (File › Document Setup): {units: "Points"\|"Picas"\|"Inches"\|"Millimeters"\|"Centimeters"\|"Pixels"\|"Feet & Inches"\|"Meters"\|"Yards"\|"Feet"} the document's units: every length the UI shows and reads (General)
- `document.switchUnits`: {} the document's units become the next of Points, Picas, Inches, Millimeters, Centimeters, Pixels, Feet & Inches, Meters, Yards and Feet (the order a right-click on a ruler lists them), Feet going back to Points; one undo step → {units}
- `document.rulerOrigin`: {artboard?: index=0 (the active artboard, which artboard rulers measure from)} → {x, y} the rulers' zero point, in absolute document points
- `document.setRulerOrigin`: {x, y, artboard?: index=0} set the rulers' zero point in absolute document points: the document's with global rulers, that artboard's with artboard rulers (it moves with the artboard); art, guides and command coordinates stay in place
- `document.resetRulerOrigin`: {artboard?: index=0} reset the rulers' zero point to that artboard's top-left corner (with artboard rulers, that artboard's own zero point)
- `document.open`: {path \| name \| dataBase64 (a file's path, or its name with its bytes), pages?: "2-3, 5" (PDF/.ai: 1-based, default all; one artboard each) \| page?: n, cropTo?: bounding\|art\|crop (default)\|trim\|bleed\|media (the box each artboard gets; bounding: the art's bounds; trim: the bleed box round it becomes the document's bleed; a PDF-compatible .ai or a PDF saved with its editing data opens on its trim boxes for crop), password?
