- `guide.move`: {index, pos: pt} \| {dx?, dy?: pt, copy?: bool} put ruler guide `index` at `pos`, or move the selected guides (vertical ones by dx, horizontal ones by dy); copy leaves them and selects the moved copies
- `guide.setLayer`: {index?, indexes?: [index…], layer: id} put ruler guide `index` (or guides `indexes`), else the selected guides, on layer `layer` (a layer or sublayer), as dragging their rows onto that layer's row in the Layers panel does → {count}

## help

- `help.discord` (Help): {} → {url} the ArtCraft community Discord
- `help.website` (Help): {} → {url}
- `help.appPage` (Help): {} → {url} this app's page
- `help.github` (Help): {} → {url} source code, issues and releases
- `help.links`: {} → {discord, website, appPage, github}

## image

- `image.info`: {id?} an image object (default: the one selected) → {id, name, linked, link, colorMode: RGB\|Grayscale\|CMYK, pixelWidth, pixelHeight, ppi: [x, y] at its placed size, width, height}

## imageTrace

- `imageTrace.make` (Object › Image Trace): {id?: image or Image Trace group (default: selection), preset?: name (imageTrace.presets), savedPreset?: {settings} (a saved preset's settings, which the journal records so a replay traces with them after the preset is changed, renamed or deleted; used in place of the saved preset `preset` names), enhanced?: false (the preset's enhanced version: with Auto Grouping, and with Shapes in all but High Fidelity Photo; Flat Logo has none and is traced as it is), params?: {mode: "blackAndWhite"\|"grayscale"\|"color"\|"logo", threshold: 0-255, palette: "limited"\|"fullTone"\|"automatic"\|"documentLibrary" (color mode; limited: the `colors` best colours, fullTone: as many as the tones need, more with colorDetail, automatic: flat art with its own colours, else fullTone, documentLibrary: at most `colors` of the library's colours, as they are), colors: 2-256, colorDetail: 0-100 (fullTone, automatic), library: "document" (the document's swatches) or a swatch library id or name (swatch.library.list; documentLibrary), colorGroup?: name (color mode, documentLibrary with library "document": the colors of that document color group instead of every swatch; "" is every swatch), paths: 0-100, corners: 0-100, noise: px, method: "abutting"\|"overlapping", ignoreWhite (leave out white areas), ignoreColor?: "#rrggbb" (with ignoreWhite: the colour left out instead of white), snapCurvesToLines, shapes: false (filled outlines that are a circle, an ellipse, a rectangle or a regular polygon come out as that live shape), autoGrouping: false (each path that has others inside it is grouped with them), gradients: false (grayscale and color: an area whose colour changes evenly along one direction is one path with a linear gradient fill), fills: true, strokes: false (Create: lines up to strokeWidth wide become stroked centre lines; without fills, wider areas become stroked outlines), strokeWidth: px (10), logoColors?: ["#rrggbb"…] (mode logo: flat-colour logos only; colours found automatically or named here, white is always its own colour, at most ten; noise = areas under noise/10 px ignored; edges to a fraction of a pixel, sharp corners restored, lines and circles snapped; enclosed transparent areas are painted white unless ignoreWhite makes them holes; fills/strokes are ignored; gradients and photos are refused)}, view?: as imageTrace.setView (default: the traced object's own, else tracingResult), ids?: [id…] (instead of id)} → {id, paths, anchors, colors, warnings: [text…] (the image is over 1500 px on a side, so it traces more slowly; or it was traced scaled down; Flat Logo names only the size it traced a larger image at)}
- `imageTrace.makeAndExpand` (Object › Image Trace): {…as imageTrace.make} same as imageTrace.make, then expand → {id, paths, anchors, colors}
- `imageTrace.release` (Object › Image Trace): {id?, ids?: [id…] (instead of id)} put the source image back in place of Image Trace groups → {ids}
- `imageTrace.expand` (Object › Image Trace): {id?, ids?: [id…] (instead of id)} keep only the traced paths (drop the source image) → {ids}
- `imageTrace.setView`: {id?\|ids?: Image Trace objects (default: selection), view: "tracingResult"\|"tracingResultWithOutlines"\|"outlines"\|"outlinesWithSourceImage"\|"sourceImage"} how they draw on screen; exports, printing and Expand keep the tracing result → {ids, view}
- `imageTrace.presets`: {} → {presets: [{name, builtIn, params, enhanced: the params of the preset's enhanced version, null if it has none}], revision: grows each time the saved presets change} the built-in presets, then the saved ones (builtIn false, enhanced null); any of these names works as imageTrace.make's `preset`
- `imageTrace.presets.save`: {name?: (default: a new "Tracing Preset N"), newName?: rename the saved preset `name`, preset?: the preset to start from (default: the saved preset `name`, else Default), enhanced?, params?: {settings, as imageTrace.make takes them, over the preset's}} create or change a saved tracing preset, kept with the preferences (built-in ones can't change) → {name, params, created, renamedFrom: the name before `newName` changed it, else null}
- `imageTrace.presets.delete`: {name} delete a saved tracing preset (built-in ones stay) → {deleted: name}

## importMatches

- `importMatches.list`: {kind?: name\|key\|modifiers, query?: text (in the name, target or label; any case)} the matches the user confirmed when importing other apps' files (shortcut sets), kept with the preferences → [{kind, name, target, label?}] (target "": no equivalent)
- `importMatches.set`: {matches: [{kind: name\|key\|modifiers, name: as the file writes it (codes in decimal), target: a command id, tool:<id> or panel:<id> (name), a key as Edit › Keyboard Shortcuts writes it (key), modifiers as it writes them, such as Cmd+Shift (modifiers), "" for no equivalent (no modifiers for kind modifiers), or null to forget the match, label?}]} remember what names and codes in another app's file stand for in VectorCraft, for later imports (at most 20,000 matches; names, targets and labels up to 1,024 bytes) → {count}
- `importMatches.reset`: {kind?: name\|key\|modifiers} forget the import matches, all of them or those of one kind → {removed}

## layer

- `layer.new` (Window › Layers): {name?, top?: bool, below?: bool, color?, template?, locked?, visible?, printable?, preview?, dimImages?} a layer directly above the current layer, at its level (a sublayer beside a current sublayer); `top`: above all layers (Ctrl/Cmd-click the button); `below`: directly below the current layer, at its level (Ctrl/Cmd+Alt-click the button), not with `top`. Options as layer.setProps; refused while an opacity mask is edited (the Layers panel lists only the mask) → {id}
- `layer.newSublayer` (Window › Layers): {parent?: layer id (default: the highlighted row's layer, else the current layer), bottom?: bool, name?, …options as layer.new} a sublayer on top of the parent's contents; `bottom`: under the parent's contents, above a clipping layer's clipping path (Ctrl/Cmd+Alt-click the button); refused while an opacity mask is edited → {id}
- `layer.delete` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} delete rows: layers, sublayers, groups or objects with what they hold (default: the highlighted rows, else the current layer). The last layer stays → {deleted}
- `layer.duplicate` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} copy rows (default: the highlighted rows, else the current layer), each just above its original; a layer's copy is named `<name> copy`. The copies are highlighted → {ids}
- `layer.setCurrent`: {id} click a row: it alone is highlighted, and the layer it is (or the layer it is in) becomes the current layer, where new art goes. Not an undo step → {id, layer}
- `layer.highlight`: {ids, mode?: "set"\|"add"\|"toggle"} highlight Layers panel rows (Shift-click: a range, Ctrl-click: toggle one); the last one's layer becomes current. Not an undo step → {rows}
- `layer.setProps` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id? (default: the highlighted rows, else the current layer), name? (with prefs.identifyObjectsBy xmlId: a valid XML ID, stored as the object name), visible?, locked?, template?, printable?, preview?, dimImages?: 0..100\|false, color?: index 0..26\|"#rrggbb"\|preset name} Layer Options; rows that are not layers take name, visible and locked. Template on also locks the layer and dims its images to 50% (unless given); off unlocks it and stops dimming. One undo step
- `layer.collectInNew` (Window › Layers): {ids?} move rows (default: the highlighted rows, else the selected objects) into a new layer where the topmost of them was (a sublayer when they are in a layer), deselecting every selected object left hidden or locked (the key object too) → {id}
- `layer.selectAll`: {id, add?: bool} the Layers panel's selection column: select the visible, unlocked art of a layer (its sublayers' too), or the object itself; `add` (Shift-click) adds it to the selection, or removes it when it is all selected already → {selected: [..]}
- `layer.move`: {ids\|id, target, place?: "above"\|"below"\|"inside" (default inside), copy?: bool} drag rows in the Layers panel: above or below the target row (beside it, in its layer or group), or inside a layer or group (on top of its contents). Layers go only in layers or at the top level, objects in layers and groups; an object placed beside a top-level layer goes inside it. A row never goes into itself; a locked layer or group takes nothing. `copy` (Alt-drag) moves copies. Every selected object left hidden or locked, such as one moved into a hidden layer or group, is deselected (the key object too). One undo step → {ids}
- `layer.clippingMask.toggle` (Window › Layers): {id?: layer or group (default: the one highlighted layer or group row, else the one selected group, else the current layer)} make: its top object (a path, compound path or text, which loses its paint) clips the rest and moves to the bottom (new art added on top is clipped); release: it stops clipping, the clipping path stays unpainted → {clip}
- `layer.target`: {id, add?: bool (Shift-click: the item joins the selection, or leaves it when all selected; nothing is targeted)} target a layer, group or object as clicking its target circle in the Layers panel does: a layer gets its visible, unlocked art (its sublayers' too) selected and becomes the current layer, and appearance.*, effect.*, transparency.* and the opacity-mask commands without `ids` then act on the layer itself; anything else is selected. Any other selection change ends the targeting (`document.inspect` → target) → {id, selected: [..]}
- `layer.pasteRemembersLayers` (Window › Layers): {on?} (default: toggle) the document option: on, the Paste commands put objects back into the layers they were copied from (by name; a missing one is made on top), off into the current layer; one undo step when it changes (document.inspect → pasteRemembersLayers) → {on}
- `layer.merge` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} (default: the highlighted rows) merge two or more rows into the layer listed (highlighted) last: the other layers' contents and the other rows move into it, keeping their stacking order, and the emptied layers go. Every selected object left hidden or locked, such as one merged into a hidden or locked layer, is deselected (the key object too). One undo step → {id}
- `layer.flatten` (Window › Layers): {id?} (default: the highlighted row's top-level layer, else the current one's) move the art of every other visible top-level layer into this one, keeping the stacking order; hidden layers and their art are deleted, template layers stay. Every selected object left hidden or locked, such as one flattened into a locked layer, is deselected (the key object too). One undo step → {id, discarded}
- `layer.releaseToLayers` (Window › Layers): {id?: layer or group (default: the highlighted row, else the current layer), build?: bool} each object in it goes into a new sublayer of its own, in stacking order (a group's objects go into sublayers of its layer, where the group was). `build`: Release to Layers (Build), each new sublayer holds copies of the objects up to its own, so the layers add up. One undo step → {ids}
- `layer.releaseToLayersBuild` (Window › Layers): {id?} layer.releaseToLayers with build: true → {ids}
- `layer.reverse` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} (default: the highlighted rows) reverse the stacking order of two or more rows that share a layer or group; each keeps the places the others held. One undo step
- `layer.template` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?, on?: bool (default: toggle the first)} make layers templates (locked, dimmed, left out of print and export) or ordinary layers again (layer.setProps template) → {on}
- `layer.hideOthers` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} hide every top-level layer but those holding the highlighted rows (else the current layer), which show. One undo step → {count}
- `layer.showAll` (Window › Layers): {} show every layer and sublayer → {count}
- `layer.outlineOthers` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} turn Preview off (outline view) for every top-level layer but those holding the highlighted rows (else the current layer), which preview → {count}
- `layer.previewAll` (Window › Layers): {} turn Preview on for every layer and sublayer → {count}
- `layer.lockOthers` (Window › Layers): {ids?\|id?} lock every top-level layer but those holding the highlighted rows (else the current layer), which unlock → {count}
- `layer.unlockAll` (Window › Layers): {} unlock every layer and sublayer → {count}
- `layer.locate` (Window › Layers): {id?} (default: the last selected object) highlight the object's row; the Layers panel opens the layers around it and scrolls to it → {id, ancestry: [layer…, id]}

## library

- `library.list`: {} → {libraries: [{id, name, graphics, colors, gradients, patterns, charStyles, paraStyles, groups} (counts)], current: the current library's id or null, folder: the library folder, or null when libraries last for the session only (the web, headless)}
- `library.get`: {library?: id or name (default: the current library)} → {id, name, graphics: [{id, name, width, height, thumbnail: PNG base64}], colors: [{name, hex, color}], gradients: [{name, gradient: {gradient: {kind, stops}, angle}}], patterns: [{name, pattern: its name in the item, thumbnail: PNG base64}], charStyles: [{name, attrs}], paraStyles: [{name, attrs}], groups: [{name, items: [{kind: color\|gradient\|pattern\|charStyle\|paraStyle\|graphic, item: name (graphic: id)}]}] (an item is in one group at most; the others are ungrouped)}
- `library.setCurrent`: {library: id or name} the library the Libraries panel shows, which library commands without a `library` act on → {id}
- `library.create`: {name?: "My Library"} a new empty library (its file in the library folder), made the current one → {id, name}
- `library.rename`: {library?, name} rename a library (default: the current one) → {id, name}
- `library.delete`: {library?} delete a library (default: the current one) and its file; not undoable → null
- `library.add`: {library?: id or name (default: the current library, made when there is none), kind: graphic\|fillColor\|strokeColor\|fillGradient\|strokeGradient\|fillPattern\|strokePattern\|charStyle\|paraStyle, ids?: objects (default: the selection), name?, color?: colour (a colour to add, instead of the selection's), swatch?: a gradient or pattern swatch of the document (fillGradient…strokePattern: added instead of the selection's paint), group?: name, before?: item \| after?: item (an item of the same kind)} add an item from the selection: graphic: a copy of the objects with every resource they use; fillColor/strokeColor: the first selected object's solid fill/stroke colour (else the default fill/stroke); fillGradient/strokeGradient: the first selected object's fill/stroke gradient (else the default's), without its placement or swatch links, named after its swatch, else "Gradient"; fillPattern/strokePattern: the first selected object's fill/stroke pattern (else the default's) with the resources its art uses, named after it; charStyle/paraStyle: the selected text's character/paragraph attributes (the Type tool's range, else the first selected text object). A new item goes into `group` (default: none) and, with `before` or `after`, just before or after that item in the order its kind is shown in, as dropping it among the Libraries panel's items does; an item the library already has (same kind and contents) isn't added again, nor moved → {library, kind, name, id?: graphic id, existing: bool, group?: name or null (a new item's)}
- `library.use`: {library?, kind, item: name (graphic: id or name), center?: [x, y] (graphic; default: the first artboard's centre), to?: fill\|stroke (colors, gradients, patterns; default: the kind's), recorded?: the item as it was when used (recorded in the journal so a replay matches)} graphic: place a copy centred on `center`, its resources pasted along, selected, one undo step; colors and gradients: paint the selection (and the defaults) as paint.setFill/paint.setStroke, a gradient fitting each object; patterns: add the pattern and its swatch to the document (a name the document gives another pattern gets a number; the same pattern is reused) and paint the selection (and the defaults) with it, one undo step; charStyle/paraStyle: add the style to the document (a name taken by different attributes gets a number) and apply it to the selected text, one undo step → {kind, ids?, style?}
- `library.removeItem`: {library?, kind, item: name (graphic: id or name)} delete an item (out of its group too) → null
- `library.createGroup`: {library?, name?: "Group" (a name the library's groups have, ignoring case, gets a number), items?: [{kind, item}] (moved into it out of their groups)} a new group of items, after the others → {library, group: its name}
- `library.renameGroup`: {library?, group: name, name} rename a group (another group's name, ignoring case, is an error) → {library, group: the new name}
- `library.deleteGroup`: {library?, group: name} delete a group: its items stay in the library, ungrouped → {library, ungrouped: how many}
- `library.moveItem`: {library?, kind, item: name (graphic: id or name), group?: name (null or none: out of its group), before?: item \| after?: item (an item of the same kind, named as `item` is)} put an item in a group (out of the one it was in); with `before` or `after`, also move it just before or after that item in the order its kind is shown in, as dragging it in the Libraries panel does → {library, kind, item, group: name or null}
- `library.moveGroup`: {library?, group: name, to: index} move a group to position `to` of the library's groups (0 = first; past the end: last), as dragging its header in the Libraries panel does → {library, group, to: where it went}
- `library.export`: {library?: id or name (default: the current library), path?} write a library (its colours, gradients, patterns, styles, graphics with their art and thumbnails, and groups) as a .vclibrary file (JSON, as libraries are kept) → {path, name}; without path → {data: the file's text, name}
- `library.import`: {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, name?: the library's name (default: the file's)} add the library of a .vclibrary file (as library.export writes) as a new library, made the current one; a name another library has gets a number (`Brand (2)`) → {id, name, graphics, colors, gradients, patterns, charStyles, paraStyles, groups (counts)}

## links

- `links.check`: {ids?: [image or placed document ids] (default: every linked image and placed document)} where each linked file is and whether it changed since it was read → {links: [{name, path, status: ok\|modified\|missing, ids, found?: path (found away from its path: relative to the document, or by name in its folder), preview: true (showing the low-resolution preview, not the file), refused?: why the path is not followed (another computer, a device or a URL: it counts as missing)}], missing, modified}
- `links.update`: {ids?: [image or placed document ids] (default: the linked images whose file was modified or is showing its preview, and placed documents whose file was modified)} read their linked files again; each keeps its bounds. One undo step → {updated: [ids], missing: [ids]}
- `links.updateChanged`: {} read again the active document's linked files that changed on disk since they were last seen (size or modification time), whatever Preferences → File Handling → Update Links says; the desktop app looks every two seconds and follows that preference. Files seen for the first time are only remembered. One undo step → {updated: [ids], ask: [], modified: []}
- `links.relink`: {ids?: [image or placed document ids] (default: the selected ones; with folder and nothing selected, every missing link), path \| folder, copy?: false (with path, for images of a saved document: copy the file into the document's folder and link to the copy; the names Name.png, Name 2.png … Name 99.png are tried in turn until one is free, which gets the copy, or is a file with the same contents, which is used), preserve?: transforms\|bounds\|fileDimensions\|fit\|fill (how the new file takes each image's place this time, as links.placementOptions describes; default: each image's Placement Options; fit keeps the new file's aspect ratio inside the old bounds: Replace)} link images (or placed documents, each showing the same artboard or page of the new file) to the file at path, or each to the file of its link's name in folder; embedded images become linked; each keeps its bounds. One undo step → {relinked: [ids], notFound: [file names], copied?: [{from, to}], kept?: [{from, to}] (with copy)}
- `links.list`: {show?: all (default)\|missing\|modified\|embedded, sort?: name\|kind (file format)\|status (missing, modified, ok, embedded); default: stacking order; top first} every image object and placed document in the layers, as the Links panel lists them → {links: [{id, name, linked, status: ok\|modified\|missing\|embedded, format, pixelWidth, pixelHeight, path?, found?: path (found away from its path), page?, preview?: true (showing the saved preview), note?: said of an image past the decode allowance (shown at reduced size, or too large to draw), document?: true (a placed document: pageWidth and pageHeight in pt instead of pixels)}], missing, modified, embedded}
- `links.goTo`: {id?} select image or placed document `id` (default: the first selected one); the Links panel scrolls it into view → {id, bounds: [x0, y0, x1, y1]}
- `links.embed`: {ids?: [image or placed document ids] (default: the selected linked ones)} break the links (the Links panel's Break Link): images keep the linked files' pixels in the document; a placed document becomes an editable copy of its art, as placing its file without link gives (its symbols, patterns, swatches and images joining the document). An image whose file can't be found and that shows the saved preview, or a placed document whose file can't be read or changed, stays linked: relink or update it first. One undo step → {embedded: [ids], missing: [ids]}
- `links.unembed`: {id?: an embedded image (default: the one selected), path?} write the image's pixels to the file at path (PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP as stored, other images as PNG; a path ending in .png/.jpg/.jpeg/.gif/.webp/.tif/.tiff/.bmp converts them) and link the image to it, keeping its bounds. One undo step → {id, path}; no path → {name, dataBase64} (nothing changes: there is no file to link to)
- `links.info`: {id?} an image's or placed document's Link Info (default: the one selected) → image.info's fields (id, name, linked, link, colorMode, pixelWidth, pixelHeight, width, height) and status: ok\|modified\|missing\|embedded, format, ppi: [x, y] (the file's), effectivePpi: [x, y] (at its placed size), scale: [x%, y%] (of its 100% size), rotation (degrees, counter-clockwise), placement: {preserve, align, clip}; linked images also fileName, location (its folder), page?, fileSize? (bytes), modified?, created? (ms since the Unix epoch), refused? (why the path is not followed); a placed document has document: true, pageWidth and pageHeight (pt) instead of pixels, ppi and colorMode, and its scale is of its artboard's or page's 100% size
- `links.placementOptions`: {ids?: [image or placed document ids] (default: the selected ones), preserve?: transforms\|bounds (default)\|fileDimensions\|fit\|fill, align?: topLeft\|top\|topRight\|left\|center (default)\|right\|bottomLeft\|bottom\|bottomRight (where the new art sits; not for bounds), clip?: false (clip it to the old bounds where it is larger)} how a file read again (links.relink, links.update) takes each image's place: transforms keeps its scale (relative to each file's 100% size), rotation and position; bounds stretches it into the old bounds; fileDimensions puts it at 100%, unrotated; fit and fill scale it proportionally to fit inside or cover the old bounds. Without options → {placement} of the first image; else one undo step → {ids, placement}
- `links.findFiles`: {folder?: an absolute path, ids?: [image or placed document ids] (default: the active document's missing links), maxSeconds?: 60 (1 to 600), stop?: false (true: stop the last search)} with folder: look in it and its subfolders, on separate threads, for files with the names of the linked files (in any letter case), and answer at once with the search's state (searching while it runs); without folder: the last search's state, with the files found so far. Each linked file's files come best first: same (the size and hash saved with the link), size (the saved size, where there is no saved hash or the file is larger than 1 GB), then name. Within each kind, a file whose name has the link's letter case comes first. Then each file's folder names are compared with the saved path's, from the innermost outward up to the first one that differs, and a file with more matching names comes first; files that still tie are listed by path. A search looks for at most 1000 linked files and keeps the first 10 files of each one's name, and up to 10 that are the same file; it ends once every linked file has its same file, and searches only the folders text.findFontFiles may search, with the same maxSeconds, depth and count limits; none on the web → {id (absent while idle), state: idle\|searching\|done\|stopped\|failed, folder?, links: [{name, path, ids, files: [{path, size, match: same\|size\|name, caseDiffers?: true}]}], searched?: {folders, files, read}, skipped?, unreadable?, stopped?: stop\|time\|limit, error?, seconds?}

## livePaint

- `livePaint.make` (Object › Live Paint): {ids?\|id?} build a Live Paint group from the selected paths (paths, compound paths and groups of them; type, images and other objects are refused, nothing changes); with a Live Paint group among them, add the others to it as livePaint.merge does → {id, faces, edges}
- `livePaint.merge` (Object › Live Paint): {ids?\|id?} add the other selected paths (compound paths, groups of paths and Live Paint groups too; type, images and other objects are refused, nothing changes) to the bottom-most Live Paint group, keeping its id, its paint and its own properties (name, lock, visibility, transparency, appearance, opacity mask, graphic style link) → {id, faces, edges}
- `livePaint.release` (Object › Live Paint): {id?, ids?: [id…] (instead of id)} replace Live Paint groups with their original paths → {ids}
- `livePaint.expand` (Object › Live Paint): {id?, ids?: [id…] (instead of id)} turn Live Paint groups into plain groups of their painted faces and edges → {ids}
- `livePaint.gapOptions`: {id?, ids?: [id…] (instead of id; default: the selected Live Paint groups), detect?: bool (Gap Detection: paint stops at gaps up to size wide), size?: "small" (3 pt) \| "medium" (6 pt) \| "large" (12 pt) \| pt (custom, over 0 up to 72) (Paint Stops At), color?: #rrggbb or a layer color's name such as "Red" (Gap Preview Color), closeGaps?: bool (add an unpainted path across each gap up to size wide to the group's paths)} set the Gap Options of Live Paint groups (what is not given stays as it was; a group starts with detect false, small and Red) and make their faces again, keeping their paint → {ids, faces, gaps: [[x0,y0,x1,y1]…] (the gaps up to size wide that no path closes, from a loose end of a path to where it closes to; detect closes them for paint), closed (paths added)}
- `livePaint.fill`: {group?: id \| ids?\|id?: [id…] (paths to make live first, as livePaint.make does), point: [x,y], color?\|swatch?\|gradient?\|none? (default: current fill), tint?: 0..100, keepModel?: bool (both as paint.setFill)} fill the face containing point, skipping hidden faces → {group, face}
- `livePaint.strokeEdge`: {group: id, point: [x,y], color?\|swatch?\|none? (default: current stroke), width?: pt (default: current weight), tolerance?: pt (4), tint?: 0..100, keepModel?: bool (both as paint.setFill)} paint the edge nearest point → {group, edge}
- `livePaint.info`: {group, id?: object id (default: the selection)} → {faces: [{id, fill, bounds}], edges: [{id, stroke, width}], sources, gapOptions: {detect, size: "small"\|"medium"\|"large"\|pt, points, color: #rrggbb} (as livePaint.gapOptions sets them), gaps: [[x0,y0,x1,y1]…] (as livePaint.gapOptions returns them)}

## magicWand

- `magicWand.set` (Window › Magic Wand): {fillColor?, fillTolerance?: 0-255, strokeColor?, strokeTolerance?: 0-255, strokeWeight?, weightTolerance?: pt, opacity?, opacityTolerance?: %, blendingMode?, reset?: bool} → the settings
- `magicWand.options`: {} → the Magic Wand settings

## node

- `node.move`: {id, parent?: id (null = top level), index} put a row at `index` (0 = bottom) of `parent` (layers only at the top level or in layers), deselecting every selected object left hidden or locked (the key object too)

## object

- `object.transform`: {matrix: [a,b,c,d,e,f], copy?: bool, ids?, strokes?: bool, corners?: bool, typeAreas?: bool, anchors?: bool, objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), releaseOutside?: bool} apply an affine to the selection (or ids); anchors (without ids or copy): paths with anchors selected with Direct Selection have only those anchors and their handles transformed, as the Transform tools and object.move/rotate/scale/reflect/shear do (default: the whole objects); strokes/corners: Scale Strokes & Effects / Scale Corners (default: the preferences); typeAreas: area type among them (not type inside a group) reshapes its frame by the matrix and its text reflows at its size, as a bounding-box handle drag does (default: the type transforms too); releaseOutside (without copy or anchors): a Shaper Group's original shape that ends wholly outside the bounds the group's original shapes had before takes shaper.releaseSource in the same undo step, named Remove from Shaper Group, as the Selection tool's moves in Construction mode do
- `object.move` (Object › Transform): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), dx, dy, copy?, objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size)}. With anchors selected with Direct Selection (and no copy), only those anchors and their handles transform, about the centre of their own box.
- `object.rotate` (Object › Transform): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), angle: deg (counter-clockwise), absolute?: bool (angle is the bounding box's new angle, not an amount), origin?: [x,y] (default: the bounding box centre), copy?, objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size)} → {ids}. With anchors selected with Direct Selection (and no copy), only those anchors and their handles transform, about the centre of their own box.
- `object.scale` (Object › Transform): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), sx: %, sy?: %, origin?: [x,y], copy?, strokes?: bool (Scale Strokes & Effects: stroke weights, dashes and effect distances scale; off keeps them, type strokes included), corners?: bool (Scale Corners: live corner radii scale), objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size)} (strokes/corners default to the preferences). With anchors selected with Direct Selection (and no copy), only those anchors and their handles transform, about the centre of their own box.
- `object.reflect` (Object › Transform): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), axis: "vertical"\|"horizontal"\|deg, origin?, copy?, objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size)}. With anchors selected with Direct Selection (and no copy), only those anchors and their handles transform, about the centre of their own box.
- `object.shear` (Object › Transform): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), angle: deg, axis?: "horizontal"\|"vertical", origin?, copy?, objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size)}. With anchors selected with Direct Selection (and no copy), only those anchors and their handles transform, about the centre of their own box.
- `object.transformAgain` (Object › Transform): {copy?, strokes?, corners?, patterns?, typeAreas?: bool (as object.transform's; default: the value the transform it repeats used, else object.transform's default)} the last transform's matrix on the selection (after a perspective move or scale: that plane-space transform, without these params)
- `object.nudge`: {dx: -1\|0\|1, dy: -1\|0\|1, big?: bool (×10), copy?: bool, patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the objects that move whole; default: prefs transformPatternTiles), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size), objects?: bool (Transform Objects, as object.move)} arrow-key nudge by the keyboard increment (the selected anchors, else objects, along the x and y axes turned by the constrainAngle preference; else ruler guides, along the page axes)
- `object.arrange.bringToFront` (Object › Arrange): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.arrange.bringForward` (Object › Arrange): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.arrange.sendBackward` (Object › Arrange): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.arrange.sendToBack` (Object › Arrange): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.arrange.sendToCurrentLayer` (Object › Arrange): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.group` (Object): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} → {id}
- `object.ungroup` (Object): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.lock` (Object › Lock): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection; in isolation mode, only those inside the isolated object)}
- `object.unlockAll` (Object): {scope?: "document"\|"group" (default document, which in isolation mode is the isolated object; group: only the objects inside the groups that hold the selected objects, a selected group holding its own; the in-window menu item chosen with Shift+Alt)} unlock the locked objects and select them → {count}
- `object.hide` (Object › Hide): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection; in isolation mode, only those inside the isolated object)}
- `object.showAll` (Object): {scope?: "document"\|"group" (default document, which in isolation mode is the isolated object; group: only the objects inside the groups that hold the selected objects, a selected group holding its own; the in-window menu item chosen with Shift+Alt)} show the hidden objects and select them → {count}
- `object.compoundPath.make` (Object › Compound Path): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)}
- `object.compoundPath.release` (Object › Compound Path): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} release the selected compound paths into their paths, each with the compound path's paint; a path of several subpaths (an SVG `d` with several `M`s) splits into one path per subpath
- `object.clippingMask.make` (Object › Clipping Mask): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} the topmost selected object (a path, compound path or text, which loses its paint) clips the others: compound holes, even-odd fills and glyph outlines clip as drawn. Paint given to the clipping path later (paint commands with its id) shows: its fill behind the clipped art, its stroke over it → {id} of the clip group
- `object.clippingMask.release` (Object › Clipping Mask): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} the selected clip groups become plain groups; their clipping path (path, compound path or text) stays, with the paint it has (none unless painted after Make)
- `object.clippingMask.editContents` (Object › Clipping Mask): {} select the clipped art of the selected clip groups → {count}
- `object.clippingMask.editMask` (Object › Clipping Mask): {} select the clipping paths of the selected clip groups → {count}
- `object.isolate`: {id?} isolate a group, a layer or a single object (default: the first selected object); type, a hidden or locked object, an object in a hidden or locked group or layer, and a guide path while Lock Guides is on are refused, and so is every object while an opacity mask is edited
- `object.exitIsolation`: {}
- `object.setProps`: {ids?\|id?, name?, visible?, locked? (visible: false or locked: true deselects every selected object hidden or locked after the call, the key object included), opacity?: 0..100, blend?: "Multiply"…, isolate?, knockout?: "on"\|"off"\|"neutral"\|bool (true = on, false = neutral), knockoutShape?: bool, data?: {key: "value" \| null (removes it)} (the object's own data, SVG data-* attributes: {pivot: "100,180"} is data-pivot; document.node → attrs.data)}
- `object.align` (Window › Align): {horizontal?: "left"\|"center"\|"right", vertical?: "top"\|"center"\|"bottom", to?: "selection"\|"artboard"\|"key" (default: the key object when the selection has one, select.key, else the selection; a key guide, select.key {guide}, stays put and the objects line up on its line: a vertical guide sets where left, center and right go and vertical alignment leaves them, a horizontal one the other way round; aligning to the key, the other selected ruler guides move too: a vertical guide to the key's left edge, center or right edge, a horizontal one to its top, center or bottom; aligning to the artboard moves selected guides to its edges too (#3463), and with only guides selected aligning to the selection moves them to their collective bounds), artboard?: index (0-based; with to: "artboard", the artboard to align to; the app passes the active one; default: the artboard under the center of the selection, else the first), bounds?: "preview"\|"geometric" (default: the Use Preview Bounds preference; preview bounds take in strokes), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} With anchor points selected (Direct Selection), the points align instead: to their own bounds, or to the artboard (#1180)
- `object.distribute` (Window › Align): {horizontal?: "left"\|"center"\|"right", vertical?: "top"\|"center"\|"bottom", spacing?: pt (used with a key, select.key: the key object, or the key guide, select.key {guide}, stays put and the lines are placed this far apart, outward from its line, in their order on that line; 0 puts them all on its line; a key guide along the axis leaves everything where it is; not used without a key or with anchor points selected), to?: "selection"\|"artboard"\|"key" (default: the key while there is one, else the selection; "key" without spacing distributes as "selection" does; not used with anchor points selected, or with spacing and a key object; with a key guide, "selection" or "artboard" distributes without it as the key), artboard?: index (0-based; with to: "artboard", the artboard to distribute across; the app passes the active one; default: as object.align's), bounds?: "preview"\|"geometric" (default: the Use Preview Bounds preference; preview bounds take in strokes), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} space the selected objects' left edges, centers or right edges (top edges, centers or bottom edges) in their order on that line, with the selected ruler guides across the axis (vertical ones for horizontal; #1597): a guide has no size, so its edges and center are its line, and guides along the axis stay. Without spacing and a key they are spaced evenly, the first and last stay put, and fewer than three don't move; with spacing and a key, see spacing (two are enough); with to: "artboard", the first one's left (top) edge goes to the artboard's left (top) edge, the last one's right (bottom) edge goes to the artboard's right (bottom) edge, and two are enough. With anchor points selected (Direct Selection), the points are spaced evenly between the first and the last along the axis instead (#1636)
- `object.distributeSpacing` (Window › Align): {axis?: "horizontal"\|"vertical" (default: "horizontal"), spacing?: pt (the key object, select.key, or the key guide, select.key {guide}, stays put and the others are spaced from it; a key guide along the axis leaves everything where it is), to?: "selection"\|"artboard"\|"key" (used without spacing and without anchor points selected, and with a key guide; default: the key while there is one, else the selection; "key" without spacing spaces as "selection" does), artboard?: index (0-based; with to: "artboard", the artboard to space across; the app passes the active one; default: as object.align's), bounds?: "preview"\|"geometric" (default: the Use Preview Bounds preference; preview bounds take in strokes), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the art; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} without spacing, make the gaps between the selected objects equal, in the order of their left (top) edges, with the selected ruler guides across the axis as items with no size (#1597; guides along the axis stay): the first item's left (top) edge and the last one's right (bottom) edge stay put, or with to: "artboard" go to the artboard's left (top) and right (bottom) edges. With anchor points selected (Direct Selection), the points are spaced evenly along the axis instead, or `spacing` apart from the first (#1636)
- `object.setBounds`: {x?, y?, width?, height?, reference?: 0..8 (9-point grid; a number past 8 is read as 8), proportional?, strokes?, corners?, copy?: bool, patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns), typeAreas?: bool (area type: the frame is reshaped and the text reflows at its size), objects?: bool (Transform Objects, as object.move)} (Transform panel; width/height and the reference point follow the bounding box, rotated with rotated objects; x, y are page coordinates; with Use Preview Bounds the values measure the visual bounds; strokes/corners as object.scale; with anchor points selected (Direct Selection) and no copy, the values are the box around those points, square to the page, and only they move or scale, as with object.move (#1824))
- `object.expandShape` (Object › Shape): {} convert live shapes to plain paths
- `object.setLiveShape`: {id?, ids?, radius?: pt (or % of each corner's maximum when `relativeRadii` is true), kind?: "round"\|"invertedRound"\|"chamfer", relativeRadii?: bool (Relative rounding, as in the reference app: store radii as fractions of each corner's maximum so they scale with the shape), corners?: [i…], items?: [{id, corners?: [i…]}] (several objects, each with its own corners, in place of ids and corners: the canvas's corner widgets across a selection), sides?: n, polygonAngle?: degrees (counterclockwise from its first vertex straight up), polygonRadius?: pt (centre to vertex), sideLength?: pt (sets the radius), makeSidesEqual?: true (a polygon scaled unevenly: drops the uneven scale and shear, keeping its centre, angle and mean radius), pieStart?, pieEnd?: degrees (an ellipse's pie, counterclockwise from 3 o'clock; 0 to 360 is the whole ellipse), invertPie?: true (swaps them: the other part of the ellipse)} (Live Corners on any path: radius and kind set its corners (anchors that aren't smooth, where two sides, straight or curved (#1144), meet at an angle): `corners` (anchor indices of the path with its corners uncut, counting every subpath's anchors in order: a rectangle's 0 top-left, 1 top-right, 2 bottom-right, 3 bottom-left; a polygon's from the first vertex clockwise), else the corners with a Direct-Selected anchor, else every corner; each radius is drawn no larger than half the corner's shorter side allows; a path that isn't a live shape keeps its uncut outline so its corners stay editable, and is plain again once none is cut; sides: a polygon's, which keep the radius they shared; the polygon params apply in that order: sides, makeSidesEqual, polygonAngle, then sideLength or else polygonRadius)
- `object.distort`: {corners: [[x,y]×4] (TL, TR, BR, BL), from?: [x0,y0,x1,y1] (default: selection bounds), ids?} projective warp of anchors and handles
- `object.pathfinder.unite` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.minusFront` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.intersect` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.exclude` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.divide` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.trim` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.merge` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.crop` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.outline` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.minusBack` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), precision?: pt 0.001..10, removeRedundantPoints?: bool, removeUnpainted?: bool (default: the Pathfinder Options, see object.pathfinder.options; the values used join the journal entry)} operate on the selected objects (back → front) → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.trap` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), thickness?: pt 0.01..5000 (0.25; how far the lighter colour reaches into the darker one across vertical edges), heightWidth?: % 1..1000 (100; the reach across horizontal edges as a percentage of the vertical one), tintReduction?: % 0..100 (40; the trap paints the lighter colour at this percentage of its tint), trapsWithProcessColor?: bool (false; a swatch (spot) colour's trap is painted with its CMYK process equivalent), reverseTraps?: bool (false; the darker colour spreads into the lighter one, except a rich black)} adds overprinting traps where the selected objects' visible areas of different solid colours meet, grouped directly above the topmost selected object and selected; the objects stay as they are → {ids}
- `object.pathfinder.options` (Window › Pathfinder): {precision?: pt 0.001..10 (0.01; how closely the Pathfinder operations follow the paths: results are refit to within it and pieces thinner than it are dropped), removeRedundantPoints?: bool (false; the results lose the anchors that don't change their shape), removeUnpainted?: bool (false; Divide and Outline leave out the pieces of objects with no fill)} Pathfinder Options, kept in the preferences and used by every later Shape Mode and Pathfinder operation (not Trap); {} reads them → the options
- `object.path.outlineStroke` (Object › Path): {} replace stroked paths with filled outlines → {ids}
- `object.path.offsetPath` (Object › Path): {offset: pt (negative insets), joins?: "miter"\|"round"\|"bevel", miterLimit?: 4} → {ids} (new objects above the originals)
- `object.path.simplify` (Object › Path): {tolerance?: pt (1), level?: 0..100 (the Simplify slider, in place of tolerance: 0 fewest anchor points, 50 is 1 pt, every 10 levels halves or doubles it), auto?: bool (Auto-Simplify: the lowest level whose tolerance is at most 1/400 of the selection's longer side, or 100 when that side is under 12.5 pt), cornerAngle?: deg (30), straightLines?: bool, wholePaths?: bool (false: a path with segments direct-selected, or between direct-selected anchors, simplifies only each run of them between its end anchors, which stay as they are, and keeps the runs selected; true simplifies those paths whole)} → {ids, before, after} anchor counts, and the level used with level or auto
- `object.path.smooth` (Object › Path): {level?: 0..100 (50; the Smooth slider: 0 minimum smoothing, the path staying within 0.1% of its longer side, 100 maximum: smoothed along its length by up to 5% of it and refitted with curves within up to 2.5% of it, corners kept only where the path turns back, never with more anchor points), auto?: bool (Auto-Smooth: level 50), ids?: [id…] (default: the selection)} smooth each path under the objects, compound path members included, as one undo step; without ids, a path with anchor points or segments direct-selected smooths only the sections between them, which stay selected → {ids, before, after} anchor counts, and the level used
- `object.path.addAnchorPoints` (Object › Path): {} → {ids}
- `object.path.divideObjectsBelow` (Object › Path): {} cut the objects below with the selected path (which is removed) → {ids}
- `object.path.splitIntoGrid` (Object › Path): {rows?: 2, columns?: 2, gutter?: pt (12; rowGutter?, columnGutter?: one of the two), totalHeight?, totalWidth?: pt (the grid's size; the selected paths' when left out), height?, width?: pt (each row's and column's size, which then sets the total), addGuides?: false (ruler guides along the row and column edges)} one grid of rectangles over the selected paths replaces them, painted as the topmost one → {ids, guides: count}
- `object.path.cleanUp` (Object › Path): {strayPoints?: true, unpaintedObjects?: true, emptyTextPaths?: true} → {removed}
- `object.compoundShape.make` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection), mode?: "add"\|"subtract"\|"intersect"\|"exclude" (default add; Alt-clicking a Shape Mode button gives its mode)} the two or more selected objects (paths, compound paths, groups, compound shapes, type, blends, envelopes) become the members of one compound shape, each with the shape mode, in their stacking order where the frontmost was: it draws as the area they make from the backmost up (open paths closed; a blend as its steps, an envelope as its distorted art, an object with a warp or other geometry effect as it draws), painted with the appearance and transparency of the topmost member in Add, Intersect or Exclude mode (with every member in Subtract, the backmost), and the members stay editable (Direct Selection, Layers panel, isolation mode) → {id}
- `object.compoundShape.setMode` (Window › Pathfinder): {mode: "add"\|"subtract"\|"intersect"\|"exclude", ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} the members of compound shapes that the selected objects are or are in take the shape mode (what clicking a Shape Mode button does with members selected); the backmost member's mode doesn't change the shape → {ids}
- `object.compoundShape.release` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} the selected compound shapes (and those holding the selected objects) become their members again, as they were before Make Compound Shape, selected → {ids}
- `object.compoundShape.expand` (Window › Pathfinder): {ids?: [id…] (selected first; default: the selection)} the selected compound shapes (and those holding the selected objects) become the path, or compound path, of the area they draw, painted as they are, selected → {ids}
- `object.lock.above` (Object › Lock): {} lock objects in the same layer that are above and overlap the selection, deselecting every selected object left locked or hidden (the key object too); in isolation mode, lock the selected objects and the objects above them inside the isolated object → {count}
- `object.lock.otherLayers` (Object › Lock): {} lock every layer that holds no selected object; in isolation mode, change nothing and return a count of 0 → {count}
- `object.lock.unselected` (Object › Lock): {} lock unselected artwork in one undo step, including unselected members of partly selected groups, clipping groups and compound paths, and guides while Lock Guides is on; hidden art and art on locked layers stay unlocked; a selected layer's art counts as selected; in isolation, only art inside the isolated object is affected; object.unlockAll unlocks locked art document-wide, or inside the isolated object in isolation mode → {count}
- `object.hide.above` (Object › Hide): {} hide objects in the same layer that are above and overlap the selection, deselecting every selected object left hidden or locked (the key object too); in isolation mode, hide the selected objects and the objects above them inside the isolated object → {count}
- `object.hide.otherLayers` (Object › Hide): {} hide every layer that holds no selected object; in isolation mode, change nothing and return a count of 0 → {count}
- `object.hide.unselected` (Object › Hide): {} hide, in one undo step, every object select.all takes that is not selected and holds no selected object; in a group that holds one it hides the other members one by one, and a clip group, compound path or other object that holds one stays as it is; the objects of a selected layer count as selected; while Lock Guides is on, guide paths count as selected and stay as they are; in isolation mode, only art inside the isolated object is affected; object.showAll shows them again → {count}
- `object.transformEach` (Object › Transform): {scaleH?: % (100), scaleV?: % (100), moveH?: pt, moveV?: pt (down = +), rotate?: deg (counter-clockwise), reflectX?: bool (flip left-right, as Reflect X in the Transform Each dialog), reflectY?: bool (flip top-bottom, as Reflect Y), random?: bool, seed?: n, reference?: 0..8 (9-point grid, 4 = centre), copy?: bool, strokes?: bool (Scale Strokes & Effects), corners?: bool (Scale Corners; both default to the preferences), objects?: bool (Transform Objects, default true; false: the art stays and, with patterns, only its pattern tiles transform), patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} transform every selected object about its own reference point → {ids}
- `object.resetBoundingBox` (Object › Transform): {} square the bounding box of the selected objects to the page again after a rotation (their geometry stays; one undo step, none when no box is rotated) → {changed: objects reset}
- `object.rasterize` (Object): {ppi?, background?: transparent\|white\|black\|"#rrggbb", antiAlias?: none\|art\|type (text snapped to pixels)\|bool, padding?\|addAround?: pt (0–1000), colorModel?: "rgb"\|"cmyk"\|"grayscale"\|"bitmap", clippingMask?: bool (a clip group with the art's outline)} replace the selection with an embedded image (PNG; in a CMYK document with its own colour model, a CMYK image of the art's inks, its transparency as its opacity mask); each defaults to document.rasterEffectsSettings; a frame of threaded text that is rasterized leaves its thread with the text it shows → {id (the image, or its clip group), width, height, warning?: "an image couldn't be decoded and is missing from the picture" (as raster exports say)}
- `object.createObjectMosaic` (Object): {columns=10, rows=10 (whole numbers, kept within 1..1000), width?, height? (new size in pt; default the image's), spacingX=0, spacingY=0 (pt between tiles), gray?: bool, deleteRaster?: bool} a group of rectangles coloured by the selected image's average tile colours → {id, tiles}
- `object.cropImage` (Object): {rect?: [x, y, width, height] \| trim?: true (to the pixels that aren't fully transparent, pixel for pixel; an image with none to cut is left as it is, trimmed: false)} crop the selected image (default: to the artboard it sits on) → {id, width, height, trimmed? (with trim), warning? (without trim: the image couldn't be decoded, as object.rasterize says)}
- `object.maskImage`: {} clip the selected image with a rectangle around it (its own outline, rotated with it) and select that clipping path, whose handles then crop it; one undo step → {id: the clip group, path: the clipping path}
- `object.createTrimMarks` (Object): {style?: "roman"\|"japanese" (default: the japaneseCropMarks preference; japanese: double lines at the trim and bleed edges plus centre marks), allArtboards?: bool (default: true when nothing is selected), offset?: pt (9, roman), length?: pt (18), bleed?: pt (8.5, japanese), weight?: pt (0.3)} trim marks stroked in [Registration] (printing on every plate) around the selection, or around every artboard, one group each, as one undo step → {id: the first group, ids}
- `object.shape.convertToShape` (Object › Shape): {} turn paths that are rectangles (any rotation) or axis-aligned ellipses into live shapes → {converted}
- `object.blend.make` (Object › Blend): {ids?, steps?: n \| distance?: pt \| smooth?: bool, orientation?: page\|path (default: the Blend Options set with nothing selected, else smooth colour and page), starts?: [anchor index \| null, …] (per object of `ids`, else of the selection in paint order: the anchor of its first subpath the blend starts from; an open path's last anchor runs it the other way), spacing?: smooth\|steps\|distance (with value), value?: n, easingType?: easeIn\|easeOut\|easeInOut\|easeOutIn, ramp?: pct (0..100), colorShift?: pct (-100..100; 0: even)} blend the selected objects (paint order) into a live blend; a blend among them takes the others in as more key objects (keeping its options, name and transparency) instead of nesting → {id}
- `object.blend.release` (Object › Blend): {} release the selected blends: the key objects come back and the spine stays as a path with no fill or stroke, below them; a blend with a name, opacity, blend mode, isolation, opacity mask or appearance of its own comes back as a group keeping them (and its knockout) → {ids (the keys), spines: [ids], groups: [ids]}
- `object.blend.options` (Object › Blend): {spacing?: smooth\|steps\|distance, value?: n, steps?: n, distance?: pt, orientation?: page\|path, smooth?: bool, easingType?: easeIn\|easeOut\|easeInOut\|easeOutIn, ramp?: pct, colorShift?: pct} set the options of the selected blends; easingType chooses the distribution, ramp sets its strength (0..100; 0 = even), colorShift (-100..100; 0 = even) shifts colours independently of shape/position easing (omitted ones keep their value; out of range is an error); with no blend selected, the options new blends start with (spacing and orientation only; a tool setting, not an undo step) → {defaults?: true}
- `object.blend.expand` (Object › Blend): {} replace the selected blends by groups of their keys and steps, each keeping the blend's id, name, transparency (knockout and isolation included), opacity mask and appearance → {ids}
- `object.blend.replaceSpine` (Object › Blend): {} use the selected path as the spine of the selected blend (the path is consumed)
- `object.blend.reverseSpine` (Object › Blend): {} reverse the order of the key objects along the spine
- `object.blend.info`: {} the options of the first selected blend, else those new blends start with → {target: blend\|defaults, id?, spacing: smooth\|steps\|distance, steps, distance, orientation: page\|path, easingType?: easeIn\|easeOut\|easeInOut\|easeOutIn, ramp?: pct, colorShift?: pct, keys?: [ids], starts?: [anchor\|null], spine?: {anchors: [{x, y, in: [x, y], out: [x, y]}], closed, keyAnchors: [anchor each key sits on] \| null (keys spread evenly: a spine from Replace Spine), explicit: false for the straight lines between the key centres}}
- `object.blend.spine.moveAnchor`: {id? (default: the selected blend), anchor: index, x, y, handle?: in\|out, independent?: bool} move a point of the blend's spine (its handles along), or with `handle` place that handle's end at (x, y) (a smooth point keeps the other handle in line unless `independent`). A key object sitting on the point moves with it. The first edit turns the straight spine into a path
- `object.blend.spine.convertAnchor`: {id? (default: the selected blend), anchor: index, to: smooth\|corner, x?, y?} convert a blend spine point: dragging a corner creates symmetric smooth handles, clicking a point with handles retracts both. Without x/y, smooth uses the neighbouring spine points
- `object.blend.spine.reshapeSegment`: {id? (default: the selected blend), segment: index, t: 0..1, dx, dy, perpendicular?: bool} bend a segment of the blend's spine so its point at t moves by (dx, dy), as path.reshapeSegment bends a path's (perpendicular too): a straight spine turns into a curve (#1111)
- `object.blend.spine.addAnchor`: {id? (default: the selected blend), x, y} add a point to the blend's spine where it passes nearest (x, y) → {anchor}
- `object.blend.spine.removeAnchor`: {id? (default: the selected blend), anchor: index} delete a point of the blend's spine that no key object sits on
- `object.blend.reverseFrontToBack` (Object › Blend): {} reverse the stacking order of the selected blends
- `object.envelope.makeWithWarp` (Object › Envelope Distort): {ids?, style?: arc\|arcLower\|arcUpper\|arch\|bulge\|shellLower\|shellUpper\|flag\|wave\|fish\|rise\|fisheye\|inflate\|squeeze\|twist (arc), bend?: % -100..100 (50), h?: horizontal distortion % -100..100 (0), v?: vertical distortion % (0), horizontal?: bool (true) \| orientation?: horizontal\|vertical, horizontalDistortion?: as h, verticalDistortion?: as v} envelope the selected objects with a warp (Envelope Options from object.envelope.options with nothing selected) → {id}
- `object.envelope.makeWithMesh` (Object › Envelope Distort): {ids?, rows?: 1..50 (4), cols?: 1..50 (4), columns?: as cols} envelope the selected objects with a mesh of rows × cols patches → {id}
- `object.envelope.makeWithTopObject` (Object › Envelope Distort): {ids?} the topmost selected path becomes the envelope of the others → {id}
- `object.envelope.resetWithWarp` (Object › Envelope Distort): {style?, bend?, h?, v?, horizontal? \| orientation? (as makeWithWarp; unset: the envelope's current warp, else arc 50 %), horizontalDistortion?: as h, verticalDistortion?: as v} make the selected envelopes warp envelopes, keeping their content
- `object.envelope.resetWithMesh` (Object › Envelope Distort): {rows?: 1..50, cols?: 1..50 (unset: a mesh envelope's own, else 4), maintainShape?: bool (true: the mesh follows the current envelope shape; false: a flat grid over the content), columns?: as cols} make the selected envelopes mesh envelopes, keeping their content
- `object.envelope.info`: {} the selected envelope's settings, or with none selected the defaults new envelopes get → {id: n\|null, type: warp\|mesh\|topObject, style, bend, h, v, horizontal, rows, cols, fidelity, antiAlias, preserveShape: clippingMask\|transparency, distortAppearance, distortLinearGradients, distortPatternFills, editing}
- `object.envelope.release` (Object › Envelope Distort): {} release the selected envelopes into their content and their shape (a grey gradient mesh for warp and mesh envelopes, the path for a top-object envelope; the content keeps the envelope's opacity, blend mode and opacity mask) → {ids}
- `object.envelope.options` (Object › Envelope Distort): {fidelity?: 0..100, antiAlias?: bool, preserveShape?: clippingMask\|transparency, distortAppearance?: bool, distortLinearGradients?: bool, distortPatternFills?: bool (the last two work with distortAppearance), style?, bend?, h?, v?, horizontal? (warp envelopes), horizontalDistortion?: as h, verticalDistortion?: as v, orientation?: horizontal\|vertical} set the selected envelopes' options; with no envelope selected, the options new envelopes get (see object.envelope.info)
- `object.envelope.expand` (Object › Envelope Distort): {} replace the selected envelopes by groups of their distorted content (type outlined), keeping name, opacity, blend mode, isolation, knockout and opacity mask → {ids}
- `object.envelope.editContents` (Object › Envelope Distort): {editing?: bool} toggle Edit Contents / Edit Envelope → {editing}
- `object.envelope.setMeshPoint`: {id, index, x, y, handle?: 0 right\|1 left\|2 down\|3 up} move a point of a mesh envelope (its handles follow), or with `handle` place that handle end at (x, y); object.mesh.movePoint, addLine and deletePoint edit mesh envelopes too
- `object.mesh.create` (Object): {ids?, rows?: n (4), cols?: n (4), appearance?: flat\|center\|edge, highlight?: % (100), at?: [x,y], tolerance?: pt (0), columns?: as cols} convert filled paths into gradient meshes in their fill colour (a gradient fill: the colour it paints at each point), lightened per `appearance` (with `at`: 1×1 mesh plus lines through that point, or through the nearest point of the edge when `at` is outside within `tolerance`); an embedded image becomes a mesh over its rectangle (its transform kept) whose points take the average colour and opacity of the picture around them (a linked image is refused: embed it first) → {ids, index?}
- `object.mesh.setPointColor`: {id, index \| points: [index…] \| items: [{id, index \| points}…], color?, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), opacity?: 0..1} colour and/or opacity of gradient mesh points (one undo step; an index out of range changes nothing)
- `object.mesh.movePoint`: {id, index, x, y, handle?: 0 right\|1 left\|2 down\|3 up} move a point of a gradient mesh or mesh envelope (its handles follow), or with `handle` place that handle end at (x, y)
- `object.mesh.movePoints`: {id, points: [index…] \| items: [{id, index \| points}…], dx, dy} move points of gradient meshes or mesh envelopes by (dx, dy), their handles with them (one undo step; an index out of range changes nothing)
- `object.mesh.addLine`: {id, x, y, color?, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), tolerance?: pt (0)} add a row and a column through (x, y) to a gradient mesh (new point in `color`) or a mesh envelope; a point outside within `tolerance` of the edge adds them through the nearest point of the edge → {index}
- `object.mesh.deletePoint`: {id, index} delete the mesh lines through a point of a gradient mesh or mesh envelope
- `object.mesh.expand` (Object): {} replace the selected meshes by groups of flat-coloured pieces → {ids}
- `object.convertDocumentColorMode`: {…as file.documentColorMode} same as file.documentColorMode: {mode: "cmyk"\|"rgb", convert?: true, intent?, grays?} → {changed} (an alias kept for older scripts; the command palette leaves it out)
- `object.textWrap.make` (Object › Text Wrap): {offset?: pt (6), invert?: bool, ids?} area type below the selected objects (same layer) wraps around them → {ids}
- `object.textWrap.release` (Object › Text Wrap): {ids?} → {ids}
- `object.textWrap.options` (Object › Text Wrap): {offset?: pt, invert?: bool, ids?} set the options of the selected wrap objects; no options → the current {offset, invert}
- `object.makePixelPerfect` (Object): {ids?\|id?} snap each object's edges to the pixel grid (odd stroke weights on half pixels) and round stroke weights
- `object.expandBrush`: {ids?\|id?} replace brushed paths (in the selection) with groups of the brush art → {ids}
- `object.pattern.make` (Object › Pattern): {ids?, name? (trimmed; blank: New Pattern N), tileType?: grid\|brickByRow\|brickByColumn\|hexByColumn\|hexByRow, brickOffset?: 0..1 (0.5), width?, height?, edit?: bool (true)} make a pattern swatch from the selection and enter pattern editing mode; refused while an opacity mask is edited → {name}
- `object.pattern.edit` (Object › Pattern): {name?} edit a pattern (default: the selected object's fill pattern) in pattern editing mode; refused while an opacity mask is edited
- `object.pattern.done`: {} leave pattern editing mode, saving the tile art into the pattern
- `object.pattern.cancel`: {} leave pattern editing mode, discarding changes
- `object.pattern.saveCopy`: {name? (trimmed; blank: <pattern> copy)} while editing, save the current tile as a new pattern swatch → {name}
- `object.repeat.radial` (Object › Repeat): {ids?, instances?: n (8), radius?: pt} radial repeat of the selection (or switch the selected repeat to radial) → {id}
- `object.repeat.grid` (Object › Repeat): {ids?, hSpacing?: pt, vSpacing?: pt (¼ of the art size), rows?: n (3), cols?: n (3)} grid repeat of the selection → {id}
- `object.repeat.mirror` (Object › Repeat): {ids?, angle?: deg (90 = vertical axis), offset?: pt (10)} mirror repeat of the selection → {id}
- `object.repeat.release` (Object › Repeat): {} release the selected repeats back to their source art → {ids}
- `object.repeat.options` (Object › Repeat): {instances?, radius?, reverseOverlap?, startAngle?, endAngle?, center?: [x,y] \| hSpacing?, vSpacing?, rows?, cols?, gridType?, flipRows?, flipCols? \| angle?} set the options of the selected repeats
- `object.repeat.expand`: {} replace the selected repeats by groups of their instances → {ids}
- `object.intertwine.make` (Object › Intertwine): {ids?: two or more objects (default: the selection)} put the objects in an Intertwine group and choose the Intertwine tool, which arranges its overlap areas (object.intertwine.arrange) → {id}
- `object.intertwine.edit` (Object › Intertwine): {} choose the Intertwine tool for the selected Intertwine group (Esc or Enter goes back to the tool before)
- `object.intertwine.release` (Object › Intertwine): {} give the objects of the selected Intertwine groups back as they were made, dropping the arranged areas → {ids}
- `object.intertwine.arrange`: {id?: Intertwine group (default: the selected one), at?: [x,y] (the overlap area under this point, as a click picks it) \| area?: [[x,y]…] (a closed polygon drawn around overlap areas: every area whose inside it encloses), order?: toggle\|front\|forward\|backward\|back (toggle: the object drawn right below the top comes to the front, so two objects swap; forward and backward move an object one place up or down among the objects in that area; the others keep their order there), member?: id (front, forward, backward and back: the object to move; default: front and forward move the object right below the top, back and backward the top one)} → {count: areas changed (an area already in that order is not changed, and with none changed there is no undo step), top: [id of the object now on top in each changed area]}
- `object.objectsOnPath.attach` (Object › Objects on Path): {path?: id of the path to attach to (without it: choose the Objects on Path tool, whose click on a path attaches the selection to it), ids?: the objects to attach (default: the selection; the path is left out of it), pivot?: 0-8 (the reference point of each object that attaches to the path, row by row from the top left; default: the Objects on Path tool's Pivot, 4, the center, until its options change it), rotate?: degrees counter-clockwise from upright on the path (default: the tool's Rotate, 0 until its options change it)} put the objects and the path in an Objects on Path group: the path at the bottom, the objects spread evenly along it from its first anchor (the last one at the end of an open path) in their order on the canvas, column by column from left to right and top to bottom in each column, each turned to follow the path; editing the path moves them → {id}
- `object.objectsOnPath.options`: {ids?: Objects on Path groups or objects in them (default: the selection), pivot?: 0-8 (the reference point of each object that attaches to the path, row by row from the top left; 4: the center), rotate?: degrees counter-clockwise from upright on the path} set how the objects of the groups attach → {ids}
- `object.objectsOnPath.expand` (Object › Objects on Path): {ids?: Objects on Path groups or objects in them (default: the selection)} make the groups plain groups of the path and the objects where they are → {ids}
- `object.liquify`: {tool: warp\|twirl\|pucker\|bloat\|scallop\|crystallize\|wrinkle, points: [[x,y,pressure?]…] (the brush stroke; pen pressure 0..1, default 1), diameter?\|width?, height? (pt, default 100), angle?, intensity? (0..1 or %), usePressure?: bool (each point's pressure is the intensity there), detail? (1..10), simplify?: 0..100, simplifyOn?: bool (default true; both for warp, twirl, pucker, bloat), rate? (twirl °), complexity? (0..15), horizontal?, vertical? (wrinkle, 0..1 or %), affectAnchors?, affectIn?, affectOut?: bool (scallop, crystallize, wrinkle; default true), ids?\|id? (default: selection, else every path the brush touches)}
- `object.puppetWarp`: {id?\|ids? (default: selection), pins: [[x,y]…] (current pin positions), moved: [[x,y]…] (targets, same length), angles?: [deg\|null…] (same length: the turn the art takes around each pin, as Alt-dragging around a pin does; null leaves it free), expand?: pt, rest?: bool, auto?: bool (as object.puppetWarp.pins reports; ignored)} as-rigid-as-possible mesh warp of anchors and handles. rest: true = the Puppet Warp tool's pins: `pins` are points on the shape the pins started from (object.puppetWarp.pins), each must be on its mesh, the warp replaces the previous one instead of adding to it, and the pins are kept with the document for Undo (an empty list removes them all, leaving the art as it is)
- `object.puppetWarp.pins`: {id?\|ids? (default: selection), expand?: pt} → {ids, pins, moved, angles, expand, rest: true, auto} the Puppet Warp pins on the art: pins = where each sits on the shape they started from, moved = where it is now, angles = the turn it holds (deg, null = free); auto: none placed yet (the tool's automatic pins: the centre and the end of each limb). Change moved/angles (or add/remove pins) and pass it back to object.puppetWarp
- `object.setOverprint`: {fill?: bool, stroke?: bool, item?: appearance item index\|null, ids?\|id?, target?: "object"\|"contents"} make fills and/or strokes overprint (Overprint Preview, Separations Preview) or knock out: item's, else every fill or stroke of the painted objects (groups: their contents; type: its characters too). Without item and ids, the Appearance panel's active item stands in when it is of that kind → {changed: objects}
- `object.flattenTransparency` (Object): {preset?: "high"\|"medium"\|"low" or a saved preset's name (see flattener.presets.list; default medium), balance?: 0..100 (raster/vector balance; 0 rasterizes everything), lineArtPpi?: 1..2400, gradientPpi?: 1..2400 (areas only gradients and meshes reach), textToOutlines?, strokesToOutlines?, clipComplexRegions? (clip images to the region outlines, else rectangles), antiAlias?, preserveAlpha? (composite over nothing instead of white), preserveOverprints? (areas showing one paint keep its colour and overprint; false clears overprints), options?: {the same keys}, ids?\|id?} overlapping transparent objects become one group of flat-colour regions (CMYK colours composited ink by ink in CMYK documents), plus an image where gradients, patterns, images, masks or raster effects reach; objects without transparency stay; a frame of threaded text that is outlined or flattened leaves its thread with the text it shows → {ids, rasterized: images made, vector: regions made, options, warning? (an image in the art couldn't be decoded, as object.rasterize says)}
