- `object.expand` (Object): {object?: true (type → outlines, live shapes → paths, effects baked, blends → groups of their steps, repeats → groups of their instances, Intertwine groups → groups of their objects and of the parts drawn over the others (clip groups), envelopes → their distorted content, placed documents → editable copies of their art, as placing the file without link gives, its symbols, patterns, swatches and images joining the document), fill?: true (gradient fills → `gradient` art, pattern fills → their tiles in a clip group shaped like the object), stroke?: true (strokes → filled outlines, the stroked paths that baking effects leaves included), gradient?: "objects" (default: `steps` solid strips, concentric ellipses for radial gradients) \| "mesh" (a gradient mesh); both in a clip group shaped like the object (freeform gradients always become a mesh shaped like it), steps?: 1..1000 (255)} one undo step → {ids}
- `object.expand.info`: {} what each Expand option would change in the selection → {object, fill, stroke, gradient, appearance} (true: something to expand; gradient: a gradient fill that Fill expands, which Expand Gradient To applies to; appearance: appearance attributes Expand Appearance expands first, for which Object › Expand… is dimmed in the menus)
- `object.slice.make` (Object › Slice): {ids?} make each selected object (or ids) an object slice: its slice follows the object's bounds → {ids: the new object slices}
- `object.slice.release` (Object › Slice): {} release the selected slices: an object slice leaves its object as it was, a user slice becomes an unpainted rectangle; the objects end up selected → {ids}
- `object.slice.fromGuides` (Object › Slice): {} replace the user slices with the grid the ruler guides cut the artboards into (two vertical and two horizontal guides give 9) → {ids}
- `object.slice.fromSelection` (Object › Slice): {ids?\|id?} a user slice over the visual bounds of the selected objects (or ids), selected → {id}
- `object.slice.duplicate` (Object › Slice): {dx?: pt (default 10), dy?: pt (default 10)} copy the selected slices as user slices with their options, offset by dx, dy; the copies are selected → {ids}
- `object.slice.combine` (Object › Slice): {} replace the two or more selected slices with one user slice over their bounds (the first one's options; object slices are released) → {id}
- `object.slice.divide` (Object › Slice): {rows?: slices down, rowHeight?: pt per slice, columns?: slices across, columnWidth?: pt per slice} cut each selected slice into a grid of user slices (evenly by count, or by size with a smaller last one; an object slice is released); the pieces are selected → {ids}
- `object.slice.deleteAll` (Object › Slice): {} delete every user slice and release every object slice → {count}
- `object.slice.options` (Object › Slice): {kind?: "image"\|"noImage"\|"htmlText" (object slices of type only), name?, url?, target?, message?, alt?: (image), text?: cell text; HTML allowed (noImage), background?: ""\|"matte"\|"#rrggbb", hAlign?: "default"\|"left"\|"center"\|"right", vAlign?: "default"\|"top"\|"middle"\|"baseline"\|"bottom"} set the selected slices' options as one undo step; no options: read them → the first slice's {kind, name, url, target, message, alt, text, background, hAlign, vAlign, htmlText: whether HTML Text applies}
- `object.slice.clipToArtboard` (Object › Slice): {on?: bool} toggle (or set) Clip to Artboard: on, slices are clipped to the artboards and auto slices fill them; off, auto slices cover the art and the slices → {on}
- `object.slice.create`: {x, y, width, height} a user slice over that rectangle, selected (the Slice tool's drag) → {id}
- `object.slice.setRect`: {id: user slice, x, y, width, height} move or resize a user slice (an object slice follows its object) → {id}
- `object.slice.move`: {slices?: [id…] (default: the selected slices), dx, dy} move the slices: user slices move, object slices move their objects → {ids}
- `object.slice.delete`: {slices?: [id…] (default: the selected slices)} delete the user slices and release the object slices (their objects stay) → {count}
- `object.slice.select`: {slices?: [id…] (user slice ids or ids of objects with an object slice; none: deselect the slices), toggle?: bool (Shift: add the unselected ones, drop the selected ones)} select slices as the Slice Selection tool does; the objects are deselected → {selected: [id…]}
- `object.vectorHalftone` (Object): {shape?: "circle"\|"ellipse"\|"square"\|"diamond"\|"line" (circle), frequency?: lines per inch 1–300 (20), angle?: deg (45; CMYK: the black screen's, cyan −30°, magenta +30°, yellow −45° from it), mode?: "mono"\|"cmyk" (mono), color?: mono dot colour ("#000000"), invert?: bool (dots for light tones), clip?: true (clip the dots to the art's outline), keepOriginal?: false} the selection as vector halftone dots sized by its tone, a group of one path per screen (CMYK screens multiply), as one undo step → {id, dots, warning? (an image in the art couldn't be decoded, as object.rasterize says)}

## paint

- `paint.setFill`: {color?: "#rrggbb"\|[r,g,b]\|{c,m,y,k}\|{gray}\|{l,a,b} (CIE Lab), none?: true, swatch?: name (a global or spot colour stays linked, so swatch edits recolour it; a tint swatch links to its base at its tint; a gradient swatch is recorded as the gradient's swatch and fits each object, keeping its aspect; the built-in "[Registration]" prints on every plate), tint?: 0..100 (% of a global or spot `swatch`; default 100, or a tint swatch's own), gradient?: {kind?: linear\|radial\|freeform, method?: classic\|perceptual (as paint.editGradient; default classic), stops?: [{offset 0..1, color, opacity? 0..1 (or 0..100), midpoint? 0.13..0.87, swatch?: global or spot colour (or tint) swatch the stop links to (its colour comes from the swatch), tint?: 0..100}] (at least 2; default white→black), angle?: deg, start?: [x,y], end?: [x,y] (the vector in document coordinates, both or neither; type objects keep it in text space), aspect?: % (radial; without start/end the gradient is placed on each object's bounds), focal?: [x,y] (radial, with start/end: the focal point, where the first stop sits), dither?: bool (linear and radial fills: drawn with fine noise that hides banding, and as pixels in SVG and PDF exports; strokes ignore it), swatch?: linked gradient swatch name}, item?: appearance item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item if it is a fill, else the top fill), ids?\|id?, focus?: true (false keeps the active proxy), keepModel?: false (in a CMYK document, RGB colours and gradient stops are stored as CMYK unless true; Gray stays Gray), target?: "object"\|"contents", span?: "object" (default: each target fits a gradient to its own bounds)\|"selection" (a linear or radial `gradient` without start/end is placed once on the union of the targets' bounds, so it runs across them)} sets the selection's fill and the default (new art fits a gradient to itself); a solid colour recolours only the gradient mesh point last clicked with the Mesh tool or Direct Selection; while the Type tool has characters selected (and no ids or item are given), the paint goes to those characters only; without `item`, a grayscale or 1-bit image takes a solid color as the color it is drawn in (white pixels transparent; `none` takes it off; an image with an alpha channel takes only a spot color, black or white) → {objects: how many objects took the paint (the objects inside a group, unless an appearance item of the group's own is targeted; 0 when only the default changed)}
- `paint.setStroke`: {…as paint.setFill, item?: the stroke item to set} same as paint.setFill, for the stroke
- `paint.swap`: {ids?\|id?} swap the fill and stroke of the selection (type too) and of the defaults
- `paint.default`: {ids?\|id?} white fill and 1 pt black stroke for the selection and the defaults; type gets black fill and no stroke
- `paint.toggleActive`: {fill?: bool (true: bring the Fill proxy forward, false: the Stroke proxy; omitted: swap which is in front)} → {fillActive}
- `paint.none`: {} set the active proxy (fill or stroke) to None → {objects}
- `paint.editGradient` (Window › Gradient): {stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), kind?: linear\|radial\|freeform (freeform places points on each object, coloured along the stops), mode?: points\|lines (freeform: how the Gradient tool adds points), method?: classic\|perceptual (how the colours between the stops mix: in display RGB, or perceptually (OKLab) so the steps look even, which SVG and PDF exports draw as an image, except that PDF writes one with spot color stops in its inks when every other stop is paper white, CMYK or gray; freeform gradients mix their own way), stops?: [{offset 0..1, color? (needed without swatch), opacity? 0..1 (or 0..100), midpoint? 0.13..0.87, swatch?: colour swatch name (a global or spot colour or tint swatch links the stop, so swatch edits recolour it and a spot stop prints on its plate; a process colour just gives its colour), tint?: 0..100 (% of the linked swatch; default 100, or a tint swatch's own)}] (at least 2; a freeform gradient's points are recoloured along them), keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill; a stop color the gradient already has keeps its model), angle?: deg, aspect?: %, reverse?: bool, dither?: bool (fills only: a linear or radial gradient fill is drawn with fine noise that hides banding, and as pixels in SVG and PDF exports), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), ids?\|id?, strokeMode?: within\|along\|across (strokes only: the gradient lies on the page and shows through the stroke, runs from the start of each subpath to its end, or runs from the stroke's left edge to its right all along it; with nothing selected, for the next object drawn; type characters' own strokes always paint within), target?: "object"\|"contents"} edit the gradient in place (keeps its placement); solid/none paints become the default gradient
- `paint.setGradientGeom`: {start?: [x,y], end?: [x,y] (document coordinates; both or neither: omitted, the vector stays), aspect?: % (radial: the extent ellipse's height / width; default: kept), focal?: [x,y] (document coordinates) \| null (radial: the focal point, where the first stop sits, pulled inside the extent ellipse; null centres it; default: it keeps its place in the ellipse), ids?\|id?, stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (alias: index; omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), target?: "object"\|"contents", index?: appearance item index (as item)} set the gradient vector, aspect ratio and focal point (solid paints become the default gradient; type objects set it on their runs, in text space)
- `paint.sampleColor`: {color, stroke?: bool (default: whichever proxy is active), ids?, stop?: index, clearStroke?: bool} sample a color into the active fill/stroke (and the selection; `ids: []` changes only the paint default); `clearStroke: true` requires `stroke: false`, `ids: []` and no `stop`, and clears the default stroke after sampling an image into the fill; with `stop`, recolor that stop of the gradient behind the proxy instead (paint.editGradient); in a CMYK document an RGB color is stored as CMYK through the color settings, as paint.setFill stores it
- `paint.invert`: {stroke?: bool (default: the active proxy), ids?\|id?, item?: appearance item index\|null, target?: "object"\|"contents"} invert the active proxy's colours of the selection (or ids; groups recolour their contents) keeping each colour's model, as edit.colors.invert does; the Appearance panel's active fill/stroke item (appearance.setActiveItem) is inverted instead when it is of that kind and no ids are given; with nothing selected, invert the default → {changed}
- `paint.complement`: {stroke?: bool (default: the active proxy), ids?\|id?, item?: appearance item index\|null, target?: "object"\|"contents"} replace the active proxy's colours by their complements ((highest + lowest) − each component, over RGB or CMY; grey unchanged) keeping each colour's model; honours the Appearance panel's active item as paint.invert does; with nothing selected, the default → {changed}
- `paint.lastColor`: {stroke?: bool (default: the active proxy), ids?\|id?, item?: appearance item index\|null, target?: "object"\|"contents"} apply the last solid colour used (paint.recent lastColor) to the active proxy of the selection and the default → {objects} (as paint.setFill)
- `paint.lastGradient`: {stroke?: bool (default: the active proxy), ids?\|id?, item?: appearance item index\|null, target?: "object"\|"contents"} apply the last gradient used (paint.recent lastGradient), fitted to each object, to the active proxy of the selection and the default → {objects} (as paint.setFill)
- `paint.recent`: {} → {colors: [{hex, color}] newest first (fed by every paint command and the eyedropper), lastColor: {hex, color}, lastGradient: gradient paint}
- `paint.proxies`: {} → {fill, stroke: the paints the Fill/Stroke proxies show (the Appearance panel's active item for the proxy of its kind, else the first selected object's, a group's first painted object's, type: its first run's; else the defaults for new art), fillActive, fillMixed, strokeMixed: the selected objects' (or a selected group's contents') fills (strokes) differ, shown as a "?" proxy}
- `paint.freeform.addPoint`: {at: [x,y] (document coordinates), color? (default: the gradient's colour there), keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), opacity?: 0..1 (or 0..100), spread?: 0..1 (or 0..100 %; the radius of pure colour around the point, a fraction of half the object's larger side; default 0), line?: true (join it to the selected point: extends the line ending there, else starts one), ids?\|id?, stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), target?: "object"\|"contents"} add a point to the freeform gradient (an unplaced one keeps its automatic points first) and select it → {index}
- `paint.freeform.setPoint`: {index? (default: the selected point), at?: [x,y] (document coordinates), color?, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), opacity?: 0..1 (or 0..100), spread?: 0..1 (or 0..100 %), ids?\|id?, stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), target?: "object"\|"contents"} move or recolour a point of the freeform gradient → {index}
- `paint.freeform.deletePoint`: {index? (default: the selected point), ids?\|id?, stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), target?: "object"\|"contents"} remove a point (never the last one); lines through it close up around it and lines left with one point go → {index}
- `paint.freeform.addLine`: {points: [index, …] (at least two), ids?\|id?, stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), target?: "object"\|"contents"} join points with a smooth line through them, in order → {line}
- `paint.freeform.splitLine`: {line: line index, segment: the segment from the line's point `segment` to the next, t?: 0..1 along it (default 0.5), ids?\|id?, stroke?: bool (default: the targeted item's kind, else the active proxy), item?: fill/stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item when it is of the edited kind), target?: "object"\|"contents"} add a point on a line there (colour, opacity and spread between the segment's ends) and select it → {index}
- `paint.freeform.selectPoint`: {index: point index \| null to clear} select a point of the freeform gradient behind the active proxy (the first selected object's): the point the Gradient tool, the Gradient and Color panels and Delete act on → {index}
- `paint.freeform.get`: {stroke?: bool (default: the active proxy)} the freeform gradient behind the proxy of the first selected object → {mode: points\|lines, points: [{at: [x,y] (document coordinates), color, opacity, spread}], lines: [[index, …]], selected: index\|null}

## paraStyle

- `paraStyle.list`: {} → {styles: [{name, attrs, uses}]}
- `paraStyle.new` (Window › Type › Paragraph Styles): {name? (trimmed, as rename trims `to`), attrs?: {…} (default: selected text), id?, start?: byte, end?: byte} → {name}; a nonempty character range samples its first selected character; start alone keeps insertion affinity; paragraph styles sample start
- `paraStyle.apply`: {name, clearOverrides?, ids?\|id?, start?, end? (id with start and end: a Type tool range; character styles style it, paragraph styles apply to the paragraphs it touches)} → {count}
- `paraStyle.redefine` (Window › Type › Paragraph Styles): {name, id?, start?: byte, end?: byte} from selected text; nonempty character ranges sample their first selected character, start alone keeps insertion affinity, paragraph styles sample start; users keep their overrides
- `paraStyle.setAttrs`: {name, attrs: {…}} replace the style's attributes; users keep their overrides
- `paraStyle.duplicate` (Window › Type › Paragraph Styles): {name} → {name}
- `paraStyle.rename`: {name, to}
- `paraStyle.delete` (Window › Type › Paragraph Styles): {name} \| {names: [name…]} delete styles in one undo step (text keeps its look; never the Normal style)
- `paraStyle.unused` (Window › Type › Paragraph Styles): {} → {names: [the styles no text uses, in panel order; never the Normal style]} (the panel highlights them)

## path

- `path.create`: {anchors: [{x, y, in?: [x,y], out?: [x,y]}], closed?: bool, d?: SVG path data} → {id}
- `path.appendAnchor`: {id, x, y, in?: [x,y], out?: [x,y], subpath?: usize (the open subpath to extend, 0 included; an invalid or closed one is an error; default: the last open subpath)} append to the end of an open subpath
- `path.close`: {id, subpath?: usize (the open subpath to close, 0 included; default: the last open one), in?: [x,y] (handle into the first anchor), independent?: bool}
- `path.moveAnchors`: {dx, dy, patterns?: bool (Transform Patterns: pattern fills and strokes transform with the objects that move whole; default: prefs transformPatternTiles)} move direct-selected anchors (whole paths if fully selected)
- `path.setHandle`: {id, subpath?, anchor, which: "in"\|"out", x, y, independent?: bool}
- `path.moveAnchor`: {id, subpath?, anchor, x, y} move one anchor to (x, y), its handles with it
- `path.setAnchors`: {id, subpaths: [{anchors: [{x,y,in?,out?}], closed}]} replace a path's geometry
- `path.reverse` (Object › Path): {ids?\|id?, subpath?: usize (reverse only this subpath of each path, 0 included; default: every subpath), reversed?: bool (true: every subpath runs counter-clockwise on screen, false: clockwise, the Attributes panel's Reverse Path Direction On / Off; omitted: flip each path; with subpath, the direction is not read: that subpath is flipped)} the paths in ids or the selection (groups and compound paths: the paths inside), as one undo step → {changed: paths}
- `path.join` (Object › Path): {ids?: [path, ...] (default: the selected paths; a compound path ID expands to its open member paths), subpaths?: [usize, usize] (which subpaths to join), ends?: ["first"\|"last", "first"\|"last"] (which end of each path to join, for two paths/subpaths; default without ids: direct-selected ends if any, else the nearest pair; default with ids: the nearest pair), to?: "corner"\|"smooth" (the joined anchor: corner retracts both handles, smooth pulls handles in line with the neighbours)} join two or more open paths/subpaths into one (the first keeps its id, selected), or close a single open path; without ids, direct-selected anchors lying on one another along a path merge into one anchor instead
- `path.average` (Object › Path): {axis?: "horizontal"\|"vertical"\|"both"}
- `path.averageJoin` (Object › Path): {to: "corner"\|"smooth" (the joined anchor: corner retracts both handles, smooth pulls handles in line with the neighbours), ids?: [path, ...] (default: the selection; a compound path ID expands to its open member paths)} move two open path ends to their midpoint and join them there, as one undo step: the two direct-selected anchors when they are open path ends, else the nearest ends of the two open paths in ids or the selection (or both ends of one open path, closing it) → the joined path stays selected
- `path.convertAnchors`: {to: "corner"\|"smooth"} convert the direct-selected anchors (all anchors of fully selected paths, every point of a selected blend's spine) as one undo step: corner retracts both handles, smooth pulls handles in line with the neighbours (smooth anchors keep theirs)
- `path.deleteAnchors`: {} delete the direct-selected anchors and their segments, opening closed paths there, and delete objects selected whole alongside them; what remains of a path stays selected, so a second Delete removes it (the Delete key)
- `path.reshape`: {id, x, y, dx, dy, tol=3, radius?} Reshape tool: grab the path at (x, y) (adding an anchor there unless one is within tol) and drag it by (dx, dy); nearby anchors follow with a smooth falloff over radius, measured along the path (default: half the subpath's size)
- `path.insertAnchor`: {id, subpath?, segment, t: 0..1}
- `path.cutAtAnchors`: {} cut the paths at their direct-selected anchors (one undo step): a closed path opens there, an open one becomes one path per piece; one of each cut's two coincident anchors stays selected → {ids}
- `path.freehand`: {points: [[x,y]…] or [[x,y,pressure]…] or [[x,y,pressure,tiltX,tiltY,rotation]…] (pen pressure 0..1: the stroke keeps it, for Calligraphic and Scatter brushes with Pressure variation; a point without one presses fully. Pen tilt in degrees, each −90..90, x positive leaning right and y leaning down the page, and barrel rotation in degrees clockwise: the stroke keeps how far the pen leaned, which way and how far its barrel turned, for brushes with Tilt, Bearing and Rotation variation; null for what a point lacks), fidelity?: pt (1.5), fit?: bool (true; false: the points are joined by straight segments, as the Pencil previews a stroke with Live curve fitting off), closed?, style?: "pencil"\|"brush", fill?: bool, keepSelected?: bool (true: the drawn or continued path is selected; false: it is not, and the rest of the selection stays), extend?: {id, end: "start"\|"end"}, connect?: {id, end: "start"\|"end"}, redraw?: {id, within?: pt (12)}} fit a freehand stroke → {id}. extend continues the path from that end; with connect, the stroke ends on that end of another path's last open subpath and joins it on, in one path with the extended path's appearance (the other path is removed when nothing of it is left); redraw (Edit Selected Paths) replaces the section of the path between where the stroke starts and ends, each within `within` of one subpath (the shorter way round a closed one), and draws a new path when the stroke doesn't end on it
- `path.curvature`: {points: [{x, y, corner?}], closed?, id?} create (or with id: replace) a path curving through the points → {id}
- `path.curvatureEdit`: {id, subpath?, op: "move"\|"insert"\|"extend"\|"close", anchor?, segment?, t?, end?: "start"\|"end", from?: "keep"\|"smooth"\|"corner", corner?, x?, y?} edit a path as the Curvature tool does, its shape kept elsewhere; the anchor edited becomes the direct-selected one → {anchor}. move: anchor to x, y, re-curving only its two segments (a smooth anchor takes the Curvature tool's handles, along the circle through it and its neighbours); insert: a smooth anchor at t on segment, the shape unchanged (with x, y: then moved there); extend: an open subpath goes on from its end (default) or start to a new end at x, y, curving into it unless corner is true; close: an open subpath closes from that end. The old end bends into the new segment as from says: keep its curve (default), smooth through its neighbours, or a corner
- `path.removeAnchor`: {id, subpath?, anchor, curvature?} remove one anchor, re-fitting the curve (with curvature: true, the curve where it was follows the remaining anchors, as the Curvature tool curves its points)
- `path.removeAnchors` (Object › Path): {} remove the direct-selected anchors without opening their paths, refitting the curve round each; the anchor after each removed one is selected, so running it again goes on along the path → {removedObjects}
- `path.convertAnchor`: {id, subpath?, anchor, to: "corner"\|"smooth", x?, y?} (smooth with x/y: out handle there, in handle mirrored; without: as the Curvature tool curves a point, along the circle through it and its neighbours, or at an open end one handle into the curve)
- `path.reshapeSegment`: {id, subpath?, segment, t, dx, dy, perpendicular?: bool} bend a segment so the point at t moves by (dx, dy); with perpendicular (Shift), both handles stand at right angles to the line between its anchors at one length, and the point at t lies as far from that line as the moved point does
- `path.split`: {id, subpath?, segment, t} or {id, subpath?, anchor}: closed paths open there, open paths become two → {ids}
- `path.knife`: {points: [[x,y]…]} cut closed shapes (selected, or all when nothing is selected) along the polyline → {ids}
- `path.eraseRegion`: {points: [[x,y]…] or [[x,y,pressure]…] (pen pressure 0..1), size?: pt (10), sizeMode?: "fixed" (default) \| "pressure" (Eraser Tool Options' Size set to Pressure: the tip is size − sizeVariation across where the pen presses lightest and size + sizeVariation where it presses hardest, as path.blob), sizeVariation?: pt (0), angle?: ° (0), roundness?: % (100, kept within 1–100)} erase the stroke of an elliptical tip from paths (selected, or all) → {ids}. The tip is `size` across its long axis, which runs at `angle` counter-clockwise from horizontal; its short axis is `roundness` percent of the long one
- `path.eraseSegments`: {points: [[x,y]…], width?: pt (8)} erase the parts of selected paths under the drag → {ids}
- `path.smoothRegion`: {points: [[x,y]…], radius?: pt (12), fidelity?: pt (2.5)} smooth selected paths near the drag → {ids, before, after}
- `path.joinScrub`: {points: [[x,y]…], tolerance?: pt (12)} join the open-path endpoints scrubbed over → {id}
- `path.blob`: {points: [[x,y]…] or [[x,y,pressure]…] (pen pressure 0..1), size?: pt (10), sizeMode?: "fixed" (default) \| "pressure" (Blob Brush Tool Options' Size set to Pressure: the brush is size − sizeVariation wide at the lightest pen pressure and size + sizeVariation at the heaviest, at least 0.1 pt; a stroke without pressure keeps the size), sizeVariation?: pt (0), angle?: ° (0, kept within −180–180: the tip's long axis counter-clockwise from horizontal, kept on the page along the stroke), roundness?: % (100, kept within 0–100: the tip's short axis in percent of its long one, 100 round; 0 is drawn as 0.1), fidelity?: pt (0.01–100; the brush follows the stroke fitted at this tolerance as path.freehand fits a Pencil stroke, and a higher value gives a smoother shape; without it the brush follows every point), merge?: true, mergeOnlySelection?: bool (false: merge with any touching same-color blob; true: only with selected ones), keepSelected?: bool (false: the drawn or merged path is not selected, and the rest of the selection stays; true: it is selected)} filled brush shape, merged with the topmost touching same-color blob and the touching ones next to it in stacking order in its group or layer → {id}
- `path.setFillRule`: {rule: "nonZero"\|"evenOdd", ids?\|id?} the fill rule of the paths and compound paths in ids or the selection (groups: those inside), as one undo step → {changed}
- `path.mirrorCut`: {axis?: "vertical"\|"horizontal"\|"free" (vertical), from?: [x,y], to?: [x,y] (free: the axis through both; vertical/horizontal: the axis through `from`, default the selection's centre), keep?: "left"\|"right"\|"top"\|"bottom" (the side kept; default left, or top for a horizontal-ish axis)} cut the selected paths and compound paths at the axis, reflect the kept half across it and join both halves into one closed shape (open paths are joined where they meet the axis); art wholly on the other side is deleted, as one undo step → {ids, removed}
- `path.lineCut`: {from: [x,y], to: [x,y]} split the selected paths and compound paths along the line through both points, extended across the art: filled shapes become their pieces on each side (holes kept), open paths split where they cross; as one undo step → {ids, removed: 0}
- `path.rectCut`: {rect: [x, y, width, height]} or {from: [x,y], to: [x,y]} (opposite corners) crop the selected paths and compound paths to the rectangle as real geometry (not a clipping mask): filled shapes are intersected with it, open paths keep their parts inside, art wholly outside is deleted; as one undo step → {ids, removed}

## pattern

- `pattern.options` (Window › Pattern Options): {name? (default: the pattern being edited), newName? (trimmed; blank: the name is kept), tileType?, brickOffset?, width?, height?, hSpacing?, vSpacing?, sizeTileToArt?: bool, overlap?: {h: left\|right, v: top\|bottom}, copies?: 3\|5\|7\|9, dimCopies?: %, showTileEdge?: bool, showSwatchBounds?: bool (outline the part of the tiling the swatch repeats, dashed)}
- `pattern.delete`: {name} delete a pattern swatch (objects painted with it lose that paint)
- `pattern.list`: {} → {patterns: [{name, tileType, width, height, ...}], editing?}
- `pattern.transform`: {ids?\|id?, matrix: [a,b,c,d,e,f]} transform only the pattern placement of the objects' pattern paints (Transform Patterns)

## pdf

- `pdf.preset.list`: {} → {presets: [{name, builtIn, supported (the writer produces its standard), description, settings (as document.pdfSettings)}]} the built-in presets (VectorCraft Default first), then the saved ones; any of these names works as `preset` wherever PDF options are taken
- `pdf.preset.save`: {name?: (default: a new "PDF Preset N"), newName?: rename it, description?, preset?: the preset to start from (default: the saved preset `name`, else VectorCraft Default), …options of document.exportPdf} create or change a saved preset (built-in presets are read-only; passwords are never stored) → {name, created, settings}
- `pdf.preset.delete`: {name} delete a saved preset (built-in ones stay) → {deleted: name}
- `pdf.preset.export`: {names?: [preset names, built-in ones too] (default: every saved preset), path?} write the presets as a .vcpdfpresets file (JSON) → {path, count}; without path → {data: the file's text, count}
- `pdf.preset.import`: {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, replace?: false (replace saved presets of the same names; else imported ones whose name is taken get a number)} add the presets of a .vcpdfpresets file (as pdf.preset.export writes) to the saved ones → {imported: [names]}

## perspective

- `perspective.grid.set`: {kind?: 1\|2\|3, origin?: [x,y], horizon?, vpLeft?, vpRight?, vpVertical?: [x,y], distance?, cell?, extent?, height?, visible?, plane?, leftOffset?, rightOffset?, groundOffset?: pt (planes moved along their normals), reproject?: bool, …the other fields of the grid as its reply lists them} merge into the document's grid. Objects in perspective stay where they are, as in the reference app; with reproject they move with the grid (each keeps its place on its plane)
- `perspective.grid.preset`: {kind: 1\|2\|3 (its normal view) \| name: a preset (see perspective.presets.list)} reset the grid to the preset fitted to the first artboard (and show it; attached objects stay attached)
- `perspective.grid.show`: {visible?: bool} (no param toggles). View state: not an undo step
- `perspective.plane.set`: {plane: left\|right\|ground\|none} Plane Switching widget. View state: not an undo step
- `perspective.attach`: {ids?\|id?, plane?: left\|right\|ground (default: the active plane)} project the objects onto the plane
- `perspective.release`: {ids?\|id?} detach from the grid (geometry unchanged)
- `perspective.move`: {ids?\|id?, from: [x,y], to: [x,y], plane?, copy?: bool (move copies, as Alt-dragging does), perpendicular?: bool (move along the plane's normal instead, as pressing 5 while dragging does), snap?: bool (default: View › Perspective Grid › Snap to Grid), constrain?: bool (default false; constrain each in-plane move to its plane's nearest axis or 45° diagonal)} slide objects within their own planes (unattached objects attach to `plane`/the active plane first); snapping pulls the nearer edge of the first plane's joint bounds toward a gridline within a quarter cell while preserving the constrained direction; Transform Again repeats it
- `perspective.draw`: {command: shape.* id, params, plane?, snap?: bool (default: Snap to Grid), at?: [x, y] (a point of the plane the shape lies on, as the tool gives it)} run a shape command and attach the result to the plane (snapping its corners to gridlines within a quarter cell)
- `perspective.transform`: {ids?\|id?, matrix: [a,b,c,d,e,f] (affine map in plane coordinates, points: u along the plane, v up or away), depth?: pt to move along the plane's normal (default 0), copy?: bool, plane?} transform objects within their own planes (Perspective Selection tool handles; unattached objects attach to `plane`/the active plane first); Transform Again repeats it → {ids}
- `perspective.nudge`: {dx, dy: arrow direction (-1, 0 or 1), big?: bool (×10), copy?: bool} move the selection in perspective by the keyboard increment, as the arrow keys do with the Perspective Selection tool → {ids}
- `perspective.plane.move`: {plane?: left\|right\|ground (default: the active plane), offset?: pt (where along its normal; 0 is its place in the grid's definition) \| by?: pt, objects?: none\|move\|copy (default none: the objects on the plane stay; move: they move with it, as Shift-dragging a plane widget does; copy: copies of them do, as Alt-dragging does)} → {plane, offset, ids}
- `perspective.plane.matchObject`: {id? (default: the first selected object)} move the plane the object is attached to onto the object and make it the active plane → {plane, offset}
- `perspective.editText`: {id? (default: the selected type)} Object › Perspective › Edit Text: show type in perspective flat where it is drawn, in isolation mode, to edit it (text.* commands, the Type tool); exiting isolation (object.exitIsolation, Esc) projects it again; refused while an opacity mask is edited → {id}
- `perspective.grid.get`: {} → {grid: the model (as perspective.grid.set takes it), define: the Define Grid fields (as perspective.grid.define takes them), station: {x, y, distance} (the viewer: centre of vision on the horizon, distance to the picture plane, pt), defined: false while the document uses the default grid}
- `perspective.grid.define`: {name?: preset name ("" = custom), kind?: 1\|2\|3, units?: unit name (points, pixels, inches, centimeters, millimeters…), scale?: [artboard, real world], gridline?: gridline every, angle?: viewing angle (0–90°), distance?: viewing distance, horizonHeight?: above the ground level, thirdVp?: [x right, y up] from the centre of vision (3-point), leftColor?, rightColor?, groundColor?: "#rrggbb", opacity?: 0–100} lengths are real-world lengths in `units` at `scale`; missing fields keep the grid's (see perspective.grid.get define). Only what changes changes: a new type, angle or distance moves the vanishing points around the station point. One undo step → the new define fields
- `perspective.presets.list`: {} → {presets: [{builtIn, name, kind, …the perspective.grid.define fields}]} the built-in views (fitted to the first artboard, protected) first, then the saved presets
- `perspective.presets.save`: {name?: (default: a new "Perspective Preset N"), newName?: rename it, preset?: the preset to start from (default: the saved preset `name`, else the document's grid), …the fields of perspective.grid.define (over it)} create or change a saved preset (Save Grid as Preset: just `name`); built-in ones are protected → {name, created, definition}
- `perspective.presets.delete`: {name} delete a saved preset (built-in ones stay) → {deleted: name}
- `perspective.presets.export`: {names?: [preset names, built-in ones too] (default: every saved preset), path?} write the presets as a .vcperspective file (JSON) → {path, count}; without path → {data: the file's text, count}
- `perspective.presets.import`: {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, replace?: false (replace saved presets of the same names; else imported ones whose name is taken get a number)} add the presets of a .vcperspective file (as perspective.presets.export writes) to the saved ones → {imported: [names]}
- `perspective.widget.options`: {show?: bool, position?: topLeft\|topRight\|bottomLeft\|bottomRight} the Plane Switching Widget: shown or not, and the corner of the document window it stays in (kept with the preferences; no params reads them) → {show, position}
- `perspective.grid.lock`: {on?: bool} (no param toggles) View › Perspective Grid › Lock Grid: the grid's widgets can't be dragged (the Plane Switching Widget still works). View state: not an undo step → {on}
- `perspective.grid.lockStation`: {on?: bool} (no param toggles) dragging one vanishing point moves the other around the station point (the viewing angle turns, the viewer stays). View state: not an undo step → {on}
- `perspective.grid.snap`: {on?: bool} (no param toggles) art drawn or moved in perspective lands on gridlines within a quarter cell (on by default). View state: not an undo step → {on}
- `perspective.grid.rulers`: {on?: bool} (no param toggles) a ruler up the line where the planes meet, in the grid's units at its scale. View state: not an undo step → {on}

## plugin

- `plugin.list`: {} → {plugins: [{id, name, version, kind: "filter"\|"effect"\|"exporter", author, description, params (doc), paramsSchema, effect? (the effect id of a live effect: plugin.<id>), format? and extensions? (an exporter's format id, plugin.<id>, for document.export, and its file extensions), budget?: "heavy" (a filter that may compute for longer, see plugin.install's allowHeavy), size, source}], folder: the Additional Plug-ins Folder load report {folder, loaded, failed: [{file, error}]} \| null}
- `plugin.info`: {id} → plugin.list's entry plus {manifest, defaults, lastError (a live effect's last failure, null)}
- `plugin.run`: {id, params?: {…} (the plug-in's parameters; or give them as top-level keys; missing ones take their defaults, see plugin.info), ids?: [..] (default: the selection), …the plug-in's parameters as top-level keys} run an object filter on the selected paths and compound paths (inside selected groups and layers too; hidden, locked, guide and clipping paths are left out): it may change, delete or add objects, as one undo step; the result is selected. With nothing selected it gets no objects (generators add theirs to the current layer) → {plugin, ids}
- `plugin.install`: {path: a .wasm file (desktop) \| dataBase64: the module (+ name?: shown as its source), replace?: bool (true: a plug-in with the same id is replaced), name?: file name (with dataBase64), allowHeavy?: bool (true once the user agreed to a heavy budget: a filter whose manifest asks for one, budget "heavy" in plugin.inspect, installs only with it)} validate and install a WebAssembly plug-in for this session → its plugin.list entry
- `plugin.inspect`: {path: a .wasm file (desktop) \| dataBase64: the module, name?: the file name shown as its source (with dataBase64)} validate a WebAssembly plug-in without installing it → the plugin.list entry it would have
- `plugin.remove`: {id} uninstall a plug-in (its live effects then leave the geometry as it is) → {removed}
- `plugin.reload` (Object › Plug-ins): {path?: a folder (default: the Additional Plug-ins Folder preference)} install every *.wasm in it (desktop; a bad module is reported and skipped) → {folder, loaded: [id], failed: [{file, error}]}

## prefs

- `prefs.get`: {key?} → the value of one preference (or preference group: eyedropper), or {prefs: {…all…}}
- `prefs.set`: {key, value} or {values: {key: value, …}} set preferences (validated; see prefs.list; lengths in pt or strings with a unit; unitsGeneral also sets the active document's units, one undo step; the eyedropper group takes a partial object, as eyedropper.setOptions)
- `prefs.reset`: {category?} reset one category (or everything) to defaults
- `prefs.list`: {} → [{key, category, section, label, kind, value, …}] every preference

## print

- `print.setup`: {preset?: name ([Default] or a saved print preset, any case: print.presets.list; its settings replace the document's, as choosing it in the Print dialog does), settings?: {copies: 1 (1–999), collate: true, reverse, artboards: all\|range\|ignore (all the art as one page), range: "1-3, 5" (1-based, with artboards: range), skipBlank (leave out artboards with no art that prints), media: letter\|legal\|tabloid\|a3\|a4\|a5\|a6\|b4\|b5\|b4 (jis)\|b5 (jis)\|b6 (jis)\|japanese postcard\|japanese double postcard\|japanese envelope #3\|japanese envelope #4\|kaku #2\|kaku #3\|yonroku-ban\|custom (a paper is named by its id or its dialog label, any case and spacing, so `B5 (JIS)` works; b4 and b5 stay the ISO 216 sizes, 250 × 353 and 176 × 250 mm, and the JIS sizes are b4 (jis) 257 × 364 mm, b5 (jis) 182 × 257 mm and b6 (jis) 128 × 182 mm), width, height (custom paper, 72–14400 pt), orientation: portrait\|landscape\|portraitFlipped\|landscapeFlipped, autoRotate: true (turn the paper to each artboard; orientation ignored), transverse (the page a quarter turn on the paper), printLayers: visiblePrintable\|visible\|all (template layers never print), placement: {origin: topLeft\|top\|topRight\|left\|center\|right\|bottomLeft\|bottom\|bottomRight (the point of the printed area, the artboard with its bleed and marks, on the same point of the imageable area), x, y (pt, right and down)}, scaling: none\|fit\|custom\|tileFull\|tileImageable, scale: {width: 100, height: 100} (% with custom and tiling), overlap: 0 (pt between tiles), tileRange: "" (1-based tiles across then down; empty: all), margin: 0 (pt the device can't print around the paper: the imageable area is inside it), marks: {trim, registration, colorBars, pageInfo, kind: roman\|japanese, weight: 0.25, offset: 6} (as document.exportPdf, at the paper's scale; page information adds the tile and the ink), bleed: {useDocument: true (the document's bleed, document.setup), top, bottom, left, right} (pt), output: {mode: composite\|separations (one grey page per ink that prints), emulsion: up\|down (down mirrors), image: positive\|negative, spotsToProcess, inks: [{name: "Cyan"\|…\|a spot swatch, print: true, frequency (lpi, default 60), angle (default C 15, M 75, Y 0, K 45, spots 45)}]}, graphics: {autoFlatness: true, flatness: 1 (0.2–100), fonts: none\|subset\|complete}, color: {intent: perceptual\|relativeColorimetric\|saturation\|absoluteColorimetric, preserveNumbers: true (CMYK colours keep their values), profile: "" (the printer profile, an RGB or CMYK one: composite PDF colours are converted to it with the intent, separations separate with a CMYK one; empty: as they are)}, advanced: {printAsBitmap (composite pages print as one image each, at the document's raster effects resolution), overprints: preserve\|discard\|simulate (composite output; preserve writes them to overprint (/OP), simulate prints them as Overprint Preview shows them; separations always keep them), flattenerPreset: "" (a flattener preset, built-in or saved: transparency is flattened before printing; empty: it prints live; PostScript defaults to medium)}, tileOrigin: {placed: false, x, y} (where the Print Tiling tool put the pages: print.tiling.set)}} store the print settings with the document, over the ones it has or the preset's (null keeps a value), as one undo step; no settings or preset → {settings} (the current ones, defaults if never set up)
- `print.preview`: {preset?: name (as print.setup's: its settings instead of the document's), settings?: {…settings of print.setup} (over the document's or the preset's)} → {pages (copies included), sheets: [{artboard (0-based; null with artboards ignored), tile? (1-based), ink? (separations), width, height (pt, the page), orientation, scale: [width %, height %], transform: [a, b, c, d, e, f] (document space → the page, pt, y down), area: [x0, y0, x1, y1] (the document region the page prints), trim: [x0, y0, x1, y1] (the artboard on the page)}] (one copy, in order), tiles: [{artboard, columns, rows, printed: [1-based tiles], tiles: [[x0, y0, x1, y1]…] (document space)}], inks: [{name, spot, print, frequency, angle}] (separations), warnings (art larger than the imageable area, type that prints in the fallback font, type that doesn't fit its frame, options not applied yet…), settings} without printing
- `print.presets.list`: {} → {presets: [{name, builtIn, settings (as print.setup's), changed: [{option, value}] (how it differs from [Default])}]} [Default] (the default print settings, protected) first, then the saved presets
- `print.presets.save`: {name?: (default: a new "Print Preset N"), newName?: rename it, preset?: the preset to start from (default: the saved preset `name`, else [Default]), settings?: {…settings of print.setup} (over it)} create or change a saved preset ([Default] is protected) → {name, created, settings}
- `print.presets.delete`: {name} delete a saved preset ([Default] stays) → {deleted: name}
- `print.presets.export`: {names?: [preset names, [Default] too] (default: every saved preset), path?} write the presets as a .vcprintpresets file (JSON) → {path, count}; without path → {data: the file's text, count}
- `print.presets.import`: {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, replace?: false (replace saved presets of the same names; else imported ones whose name is taken get a number)} add the presets of a .vcprintpresets file (as print.presets.export writes) to the saved ones → {imported: [names]}
- `print.tiling.set`: {origin: [x, y] (document point: the top-left corner of the first page's imageable area), artboard? (0-based: the artboard it is measured from; default the printed artboard containing origin, its edges included, else the first; ignored with artboards: ignore)} \| {reset: true} place the printed pages as the Print Tiling tool does, saved with the print settings (tileOrigin: {placed, x, y}, from the artboard's top-left corner; it replaces the placement, and tiles start there), as one undo step; reset → the placement places them again → {tileOrigin}
- `print.tiling`: {settings?: {…settings of print.setup} (over the document's)} the pages as View → Show Print Tiling draws them, from print.preview's layout → {tileOrigin, pages: [{artboard (0-based; null with artboards ignored), number (1-based tile across then down, or page), printed (tiles outside tileRange don't), page: [x0, y0, x1, y1] (the paper's edge), imageable: [x0, y0, x1, y1] (inside the unprintable margin)}] (document space; every tile when tiling, else each page once)}

## recolor

- `recolor.colors`: {ids?: object ids (default: the selection, or with nothing selected the objects select.all takes, without the hidden and locked objects inside them)} → {colors: [{key: colour identity ("rgb 255 0 0", "cmyk 0 100 100 0", "gray 40", "lab 55 60 -40"), hex, count, swatch?: the global swatch it is linked to}]} unique colours used by the objects (fills, strokes, gradient stops, text, mesh points and the tiles of pattern fills and strokes), most used first
- `recolor.apply` (Edit › Edit Colors): {map: rows [{from: [colour keys (recolor.colors) or "#rrggbb" (every colour shown as that hex)], to: colour (hex, key, {c,m,y,k} or {l,a,b}) \| null, exclude?: false (the row's colours are kept), tint?: 100 (percent; with limitTo, art linked to the global or spot swatch the new color is takes this share of its tint)}] \| {"key or #rrggbb": colour, …} (one row per colour), method?: "exact" (default)\|"preserveTints"\|"scaleTints"\|"tintsShades"\|"hueShift" (how a row's colours take its new colour, relative to the row's darkest, average or most saturated colour), limitTo?: swatch library id or name; or "document" (the document's swatches): new colours snap to its nearest colour, group?: colour group to rewrite, groupColors?: [colour] (what `group` is rewritten with; default: the rows' new colours, in order, excluded rows left out), rename?: the group's new name, includeImages?: true, includePatterns?: true, globalAdjust?: {saturation?: 0, brightness?: 0, temperature?: 0, luminosity?: 0} (Global Adjust: percent from -100 to 100, 0 changes nothing; every colour a row that isn't excluded recolours, and the group's new colours, take the same change after the row's new colour: saturation scales HSB saturation by 1 + saturation/100, brightness moves HSB brightness that share of the way to full or to black, temperature adds 0.2 × temperature/100 to red and takes it from blue, luminosity moves Lab lightness that share of the way to 100 or 0 keeping a and b; with limitTo each row's new colour takes the change before it snaps to the library)} recolour the selection (gradients, text, meshes, image pixels and pattern tiles included; each new colour keeps the model of the one it replaces (model?: "keep" default \| "new" (the new colour's own model, so a CMYK colour replacing RGB art gives CMYK art) \| "document" (the document's colour mode)), except a library swatch's with limitTo: art takes the swatch itself, linked to a global or spot colour at its tints, and the swatch joins the document's swatches; art linked to a global swatch of `group` that is rewritten with its row's new color stays linked to it, at its tint, unless the row converts the color into the art's model; other recolored art, and art in a row linked to a global swatch of `group` that is rewritten with another color, keeps its row's new color and loses its swatch link) and the group, as one undo step → {changed, swatches: [names added to the document's swatches], relinked?: with group, the other paints linked to its global swatches that took their new colors, note?: set when a colour kept a model other than its new colour's (see model)}
- `recolor.reduce`: {colors?: n (rows; default: one per colour, with the tints of a global swatch in its row), method?: as recolor.apply; default scaleTints (picks each row's colour), preserve?: {white?: true, black?: true, grays?: false} (left out of the rows), limitTo?: as recolor.apply (new colours snap to it), ids?: as recolor.colors} group the colors of the objects (default: as recolor.colors) into rows of similar colours (k-means in Lab, weighted by use) → {map: [{from: [keys, the row's colour first], to: key}], preserved: [keys], method}
- `recolor.randomize`: {map: rows as recolor.apply, order?: true (shuffle the new colours among the rows that aren't excluded), saturationBrightness?: false (random saturation and brightness, hue kept), seed?: 0} → {map: the rows with their new colours as keys}

## script

- `script.check`: {path? \| data?: the script's text \| dataBase64?, name?: the file's name (default: the path's; with a path, only the reply's name), includes?: false (with a path: also check the files its #include lines name, inside the script's folder; not on the web)} check a script written for the reference app (.jsx, .jsxinc, .js, or a compiled .jsxbin) without running any of it → {name, kind: script\|compiled\|module\|notText\|tooLarge, needs: [language\|dom\|files\|menuCommands\|dialogs\|hostOnly], lines, targets: [{value, app, instance?, version?, locale?, line, file?}], directives: [{name, value, line, form: "#"\|"//@", file?}], includes: [{value, line, status: checked\|missing\|notFollowed\|cycle\|notRead\|limit\|unreadable, path?, file?}], features: [{id, count, lines}], dom: {members: [{path, count}], types: [{name, count}]}, menuCommands: {strings: [{value, count, lines}], dynamic}, outside: [{id: filesWritten\|filesOpened\|network\|nativeCode\|otherApps\|environment\|unseenCode\|preferences, count, lines, modes?}], problems: [{code, line?, file?, detail?}], truncated} (file: the included file it is in; lines: at most 20 of the script's own, while counts include the included files; at most 8 MiB a file and 32 MiB in all, 64 included files 16 deep, 1,024 paths looked up for them, 1,000 entries a list, text echoed cut to 120 characters)

## select

- `select.all` (Select): {} → {count}; while the Type tool edits text, all of that text instead → {editing, start, end} (byte offsets)
- `select.allOnArtboard` (Select): {artboard?: index (0-based; the app passes the active one; default: the first)}
- `select.none` (Select): {}
- `select.reselect` (Select): {}
- `select.inverse` (Select): {}
- `select.nextAbove` (Select): {}
- `select.nextBelow` (Select): {}
- `select.set`: {ids: [id…]} all ids must be non-negative integers naming existing objects → {count, ids} (resulting selection)
- `select.add`: {ids: [id…]} all ids must be non-negative integers naming existing objects → {count, ids} (resulting selection)
- `select.toggle`: {id} or {ids: [id…]}: each selected one leaves the selection, the others join it; all ids must be non-negative integers naming existing objects → {count, ids} (resulting selection)
- `select.key`: {id?: a selected top-level object (not a layer, a compound-path member or an object inside another selected object); one of two or more or the only one with ruler guides selected (the Selection tool: a click on one object of a selection of several, or of one with guides; on the key again: no id), guide?: index (a selected ruler guide, guide.list, with one or more objects selected: the Selection tool's click on a selected guide; Align lines the objects up on it and the guide stays put)} the key object Align aligns to and Distribute Spacing spaces from; without `id` or `guide` it cancels the key, as does a selection that holds only the key and objects inside it (one object, or a group key and its own members) and no ruler guide, or a key guide with no object selected or taken out of the selection; any other id or guide is an error that leaves the key as it was → {key, guide?} (the resulting key object, or null; guide: the key guide, left out without one)
- `select.anchors`: {id, anchors: [[subpath, anchor]…], mode: "set"\|"add"\|"toggle"}
- `select.segments`: {id, segments: [[subpath, segment]…], mode?: "set"\|"add"\|"toggle"} select path segments; their endpoints move with them, Delete removes only their connections
- `select.anchorsMany`: {items: [{id, anchors}], mode?: "set"\|"add"\|"toggle"\|"subtract" (set by default; add: true is mode add), add?: bool (an older way to ask for mode add)}: toggle selects the anchors not selected and deselects the others, subtract deselects them; a path left with none leaves the selection
- `select.color`: {fill?: colour, stroke?: colour (as paint.setFill's color; at least one)} select the objects whose fill (and stroke) reads as that colour in #rrggbb, as document.inspect reports colours, whatever its swatch or tint; with both, the objects that match both. No object needs to be selected first; Select > Reselect repeats it → {count}
- `select.same.fillColor` (Select › Same): {} the objects filled with the first selected object's fill colour (any tint of its global or spot swatch, the same tint with prefs selectSameTintPercent); a member path of a compound path selected first compares as its compound path, except in isolation mode inside that compound path; from a Live Paint face, the faces of its Live Paint group only, and from any other object, no Live Paint face
- `select.same.strokeColor` (Select › Same): {} the objects stroked with the first selected object's stroke colour (tints and compound paths as select.same.fillColor)
- `select.same.strokeWeight` (Select › Same): {}
- `select.same.fillAndStroke` (Select › Same): {} the objects with the first selected object's fill and stroke colours (tints, compound paths and Live Paint faces as select.same.fillColor)
- `select.same.opacity` (Select › Same): {}
- `select.same.blendingMode` (Select › Same): {}
- `select.same.appearance` (Select › Same): {}
- `select.same.shapeType` (Select › Same): {}
- `select.object.allOnSameLayers` (Select › Object): {}
- `select.object.clippingMasks` (Select › Object): {}
- `select.object.textObjects` (Select › Object): {}
- `select.object.strayPoints` (Select › Object): {}
- `select.object.openPaths` (Select › Object): {}
- `select.same.graphicStyle` (Select › Same): {} the objects linked to the graphic style the first selected object is linked to (compound paths as select.same.fillColor)
- `select.same.appearanceAttribute` (Select › Same): {item?: appearance item index} the objects sharing an appearance attribute of the first selected object: its fill or stroke `item` (default: the Appearance panel's active item), else its first effect, else its topmost fill (compound paths as select.same.fillColor)
- `select.globalEdit` (Select): {appearance?: bool, size?: bool, artboards?: "all" \| range such as "1, 3-5", includeCanvas?: bool} (each defaults to the Global Edit Options) while Global Edit is off: start it on the one selected object (a path, compound path, symbol instance or group of them), with the objects of the same shape (for a group: the groups holding objects like its own, at the same places) (and, with `appearance`, the same fill and stroke; with `size`, the same width and height) on the artboards given (and, with `includeCanvas`, off every artboard), at most 10000 → {active: true, count, ids}; while on: stop it (Escape does the same) → {active: false}
- `select.globalEditOptions`: {appearance?: bool, size?: bool, artboards?: "all" \| range such as "1, 3-5", includeCanvas?: bool} set the options Start Global Edit matches with (defaults: shape only, all artboards, objects on the canvas included) → {appearance, size, artboards, includeCanvas, count: the objects Start Global Edit would take now (0 unless one supported object is selected)}
- `select.magicWand`: {id, mode?: set\|add\|subtract} select objects similar to `id` by the Magic Wand settings → {count}
- `select.font`: {family, style?} select the text objects using the font, and the instances of the symbols whose art uses it, that are not hidden or locked, in a hidden or locked group or layer, or on a locked artboard → {count}
- `select.same.symbolInstance` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.same.linkBlockSeries` (Select › Same): {} select every frame of the threads of the selected type objects (and of the type inside selected groups); type in no thread selects itself; type and frames that are hidden or locked, inside a hidden or locked object or layer, on a template layer, or outside the isolated group or layer neither count as selected nor are selected → {count}
- `select.same.fontFamily` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.same.fontFamilyStyle` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.same.fontFamilyStyleSize` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.same.fontSize` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.same.textFillColor` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.same.textStrokeColor` (Select › Same): {} → {count}
- `select.object.directionHandles` (Select › Object): {} direct-select every anchor (showing all handles) of the selected paths and the paths inside the selected objects, leaving out paths that are hidden or locked, inside a hidden or locked object or layer, on a template layer, or outside the isolated group or layer → {anchors}
- `select.object.pointText` (Select › Object): {} → {count}
- `select.object.areaText` (Select › Object): {} → {count}
- `select.object.brushStrokes` (Select › Object): {} → {count}
- `select.object.bristleBrushStrokes` (Select › Object): {} objects whose topmost stroke has a bristle brush (type bristle in brush.list), whatever the brush is named → {count}
- `select.save` (Select): {name?} save the current selection under a name, in the document (default "Selection N"; an existing name is replaced; at most 25, names up to 255 characters) → {name}
- `select.update` (Select): {} make the saved selection last recalled (`select.recall`) hold the selected objects instead of its own, in one undo step → {name}
- `select.recall` (Select): {name} select the objects of a saved selection, leaving out those deleted since and those that are hidden or locked, inside a hidden or locked object or layer, on a template layer, or outside the isolated group or layer → {count}
- `select.editSaved` (Select): {name, newName?: rename, delete?: bool} \| {edits: [{name, newName?, delete?}…]} rename or delete saved selections, all in one undo step; names are those before the edit and must stay unique
- `select.savedList`: {} → [name…] for the active document
- `select.object.slices` (Select › Object): {} select every user and object slice → {count}

## shape

- `shape.rectangle`: {x, y, width, height, radius?: pt, rotation?: deg (about centre)} → {id}
- `shape.ellipse`: {x, y, width, height, rotation?: deg (about centre)} → {id}
- `shape.polygon`: {cx, cy, radius, sides=6, rotation?: deg} → {id}
- `shape.star`: {cx, cy, radius1, radius2, points=5, rotation?: deg} → {id}
- `shape.flare`: {cx, cy, diameter=100, opacity=50 (%), brightness=30 (%), growth=20 (%), fuzziness=50 (%), rays=15, longest=300 (%), rayFuzziness=100 (%), rotation=0 (deg, turns the rays clockwise), x2?, y2? (ring end; else pathLength at direction), pathLength=300, rings=10, largest=50 (%), direction=45 (deg), seed?} → {id}
- `shape.line`: {x1, y1, x2, y2, fill?: bool (Fill Line: the current fill, else none)} → {id}
- `shape.spiral`: {cx, cy, radius, decay=80 (%), segments=10, clockwise?} → {id}
- `shape.arc`: {x1, y1, x2, y2, closed?, slope?} or {x, y, lengthX, lengthY, reference?, baseAlong?: x\|y, slope?, closed?, fillArc?} → {id}
- `shape.rectangularGrid`: {x, y, width, height, rows=5, columns=5, rowSkew=0, columnSkew=0 (−500…500 %: + crowds the horizontal dividers toward the top, the vertical ones toward the right), frame=true (the outside as one rectangle, else its four sides), fill=false (the current fill, else none), rotation?: deg (about centre)} → {id}
- `shape.polarGrid`: {x, y, width, height, concentric=5, radial=5, concentricSkew=0, radialSkew=0 (−500…500 %: + crowds the ellipses toward the outside, the radial dividers clockwise of the top one), compound=false (the ellipses pair up into compound-path rings), fill=false (the current fill, else none), rotation?: deg (about centre)} → {id}

## shapeBuilder

- `shapeBuilder.merge`: {ids?\|id?: [id…] (default: selection), points: [[x,y]…] (drag path; one point = click; may be left out with rect), rect?: [x, y, width, height] (Shift-drag marquee from where the drag started, x and y, to x+width and y+height; width and height may be negative: touches every region whose outline crosses the rectangle or has an anchor inside it and the region holding the whole rectangle, the region under x, y comes first and the one under the opposite corner comes next; with points, it also touches the regions along them; for pickColorFrom, x, y is the drag's first point and the opposite corner is its last), erase?: bool (erase mode, Alt: deletes the regions touched, and the pieces of open paths that cut regions and the edges (outline pieces between the points where paths meet; see shapeBuilder.regions) within tolerance, except that a point in a region no more than twice the tolerance across there touches the region; a deleted edge opens its path there, and edges bounding a deleted region go with it; what is left of each path keeps its look, with gradients fitted to its bounds kept where they were on it, its name, opacity mask and attributes, and its fill rule unless merged regions shaped it), splitStroke?: false (In Merge Mode, Clicking Stroke Splits the Path; not with erase or rect: when the one point of a click is within tolerance of a stroke, the path that stroke is on splits in two instead of a merge: the path keeps the rest of it, and a copy of the path holding the run of it from the anchor or crossing point before the click to the one after it, in the path's direction, goes directly above it; both keep the path's gradients where they were on it; the other objects stay as they are; with pickColorFrom "swatches" or fill, the run's stroke takes that paint; a path in a Live Paint group or a Shaper Group does not split, and nor does a path the run is all of, such as a lone line, or all but 0.05 pt or less of (a ten-thousandth of the larger side of a path over 500 pt)), tolerance?: pt (2, erase or splitStroke), gap?: pt (0–72; 0: Gap Detection off), openFilledAsClosed?: true (Consider Open Filled Paths as Closed: a filled open path bounds a region as if an invisible edge closed it; false: it cuts the regions it crosses like an unfilled one), pickColorFrom?: "artwork" (default: the merged shape takes the look of the front-most object under the first point, else under the last point, else of the front-most object) \| "swatches" (the current fill, with the rest of the look of the front-most object over the first region the drag touches that an object covers, else of the front-most object), fill?: "#rrggbb" \| {color\|swatch\|gradient\|none} (overrides pickColorFrom)} replaces the paths it uses where they are; other selected objects (type, hidden or locked objects, clipping paths) stay, and a group goes only when it is left empty → {ids (selected after: each selected object still there, or what took its place), merged, regions, lines, edges (counts deleted, merged or split), split?: id (the run split off, after a split)}; after a split, ids are the roots, with the rest and the run in place of the path when it was one
- `shapeBuilder.regions`: {ids?\|id?, gap?: pt (0–72; 0: Gap Detection off), openFilledAsClosed?: true (as shapeBuilder.merge)} → {regions: [{bounds: [x0,y0,x1,y1], area, sources: [id…]}], lines: [{bounds, source: id}], edges: [{bounds, source: id}]} faces of the planar arrangement (unfilled open paths cut them, and filled ones too with openFilledAsClosed false; sources is empty for an area only they enclose), the pieces of those open paths and the edges (the other paths' outlines cut where paths meet; source: the front-most path along it), which erasing deletes

## shaper

- `shaper.scribble`: {points: [[x,y]…], ids?: [id…] (default: nearby art), tolerance?: pt (4)} interior scribbles remove fills; scribbles through several regions merge with the origin fill; ending outside removes fills and boundary strokes; outside scribbles erase stroke pieces. Originals remain editable → {id, regions, strokes}
- `shaper.select`: {point: [x,y], source?: bool} click selects a group, then a face; source enters construction isolation and selects an original (while an opacity mask or a pattern is edited, it selects the original and the editing layer stays isolated); while a group's originals are isolated, it selects one of that group's originals → {id}
- `shaper.release` (Object › Shaper): {id? (default: selection)} restore the original paths → {ids}
- `shaper.expand` (Object › Shaper): {id? (default: selection)} keep the visible result as ordinary paths → {id}
- `shaper.releaseSource`: {source: id, group?: id (default: the Shaper Group that holds `source`)} take one original shape out of its Shaper Group: it becomes a path just above the group, keeps its look and is selected, and Construction mode ends; the group's result is computed again from the shapes left, and a group left with none is removed. In Construction mode, a Selection-tool move that ends with an original shape wholly outside the bounds the group's original shapes had does the same in the move's undo step (object.transform's `releaseOutside`) → {id}

## shortcutSet

- `shortcutSet.read`: {path? \| data?: the file's text \| dataBase64?, name?: the file's name (default: the path's)} read a shortcut set another app saved as text (.kys, starting "/Menus {") without changing anything: each entry is a block that sets /Key, with the names of the blocks around it as its section → {set: the file's name without its extension, entries: [{section: [names], name, key, modifiers, context, represent, line, target?, chord?}] (codes as the file writes them; null where the entry doesn't set one; target: what importMatches says the name stands for; chord: the shortcut its codes stand for, as Edit › Keyboard Shortcuts writes it, when importMatches decodes them, an entry without /Modifiers taking none), codes: {keys: [{value, count, key?}], modifiers: [{value, count, mods?}]} (key and mods from importMatches), unread: [{line, text}] (tokens that aren't read, at most 1000), truncated: true when a cap stopped the reading (4 MiB, 16 open blocks, 20,000 entries, 8 MiB of names over the entries and their sections; names past 1,024 bytes are cut)}. A file in another form (such as XML) is an error

## slice

- `slice.list`: {} the slices as laid out, numbered left to right and top to bottom → {slices: [{number, id (null for auto slices), source: "user"\|"object"\|"auto", name, x, y, width, height, options, selected (selected with the Slice Selection tool or a slice command, or an object slice whose object is selected)}], clipToArtboard, hidden, locked}

## stroke

- `stroke.set` (Window › Stroke): {weight?: pt, cap?: butt\|round\|square, join?: miter\|round\|bevel, miterLimit?, align?: center\|inside\|outside, dash?: [d,g,…]\|null (a 0 dash with a round or projecting cap draws dots or squares; a new pattern keeps the current offset and alignment), dashOffset? (exact dashes only), alignDashes?: bool (true: dashes fitted to corners and path ends, every run between them holding whole periods with a dash centred on each corner and end; false, the default for a new pattern: exact lengths), startArrow?, endArrow?: Arrow\|ArrowOpen\|Barbed\|Concave\|DoubleArrow\|HalfArrowLeft\|HalfArrowRight\|Chevron\|Feather\|Swallowtail\|Triangle\|TriangleOpen\|TriangleReverse\|TriangleBar\|Kite\|Leaf\|Drop\|Circle\|CircleOpen\|HalfCircle\|Oval\|Target\|Square\|SquareOpen\|Tag\|TagOpen\|Diamond\|DiamondOpen\|Hexagon\|HexagonOpen\|Star\|Cross\|Plus\|Bar\|DoubleBar\|DotOnBar\|Slash\|DoubleSlash\|Bracket\|Fork\|null (HalfArrowLeft/Right: one barb, left or right of the direction the head points), arrowAlign?: "extend" (tip past the end point, default)\|"tip" (tip on the end point; the stroke is shortened), profile?: "uniform"\|"lens"\|"taperStart"\|"taperEnd"\|"pinch"\|"teardrop"\|"wave"\|a saved profile's name (stroke.widthProfile.list), item?: stroke item index\|null (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item if it is a stroke, else the top stroke, created when missing), ids?\|id?, target?: "object"\|"contents"} Without a stroke item, type takes weight, cap, join, miterLimit, align (around its glyph outlines; the type stays live) and the dash options as its characters' stroke (every run; text.setRangeStyle `strokeOptions` styles a range), and images and symbol instances (also in groups) are left alone. With nothing selected (and no ids) they set up the next object drawn (appearance.newArt; weight always does) → {objects: how many objects took the stroke options}
- `stroke.setAdvanced`: {arrowScale?: [start %, end %], swapArrows?: bool, flipProfile?: "along"\|"across", brush?: name\|null, item?: stroke item index\|null, ids?\|id?, target?: "object"\|"contents"} Stroke panel extras (with nothing selected, all but brush set up the next object drawn)
- `stroke.widthProfile.add`: {name?} save the selected stroke's variable width to the Profile list (kept with the preferences) under `name` (default "Width Profile N"; unique, not a built-in's) → {name}
- `stroke.widthProfile.delete`: {name?} remove a saved profile from the Profile list (default: the selected stroke's); built-in profiles can't be deleted, and strokes keep their widths → {deleted}
- `stroke.widthProfile.reset`: {} remove every saved profile, leaving the built-ins → {removed: count}
- `stroke.widthProfile.list`: {} → {profiles: [{id (stroke.set `profile`), label, builtIn, points: [[t, left, right]…]}], current: the selected stroke's profile id or name (nothing selected: the next object drawn's), "custom" when it isn't listed, null without a stroke}
- `stroke.widthPoint.set`: {id, t: 0..1 (fraction of the path length), left, right: side widths in pt, index?: width point to edit or move, adjustAdjoining?: bool (the nearest points either side change their widths in proportion, using the sampled widths for a new point)} → {index}. Without index a point already at t is replaced; a point moved onto another one's t joins it as a discontinuous point (on the side it came from), so the width steps there. Creates a uniform profile first if needed
- `stroke.widthPoint.remove`: {id, index \| indices: [..]} remove width points (removing the last one leaves a uniform stroke)
- `stroke.widthPoint.copy`: {id, index: the width point to copy, t: 0..1 where the copy goes} → {index} (Alt-drag with the Width tool). Landing on another point makes a discontinuous point, as a stroke.widthPoint.set move does
- `stroke.widthProfile.set`: {ids?\|id?, points: [[t, left, right]…] (width factors, 1 = the stroke weight) \| null = uniform}

## svgInteractivity

- `svgInteractivity.info`: {ids?\|id?} → {ids, events: [{event, script}…] in the panel's order \| null (the objects' events differ), files: [the linked JavaScript files…], eventNames: [the events the panel lists…]} the SVG Interactivity panel's values for ids or the selection
- `svgInteractivity.setEvent`: {event: "onclick"\|"onmouseover"\|… (svgInteractivity.info's eventNames), script: string (the JavaScript; "" removes the event), ids?\|id?} set the JavaScript an event runs on ids or the selection, replacing the event's script where it has one, as one undo step; SVG export writes it as the event attribute → {changed: objects}
- `svgInteractivity.removeEvent`: {event, ids?\|id?} remove an event and its JavaScript from ids or the selection, as one undo step → {changed: objects}
- `svgInteractivity.clearEvents`: {ids?\|id?} remove every event from ids or the selection, as one undo step → {changed: objects}
- `svgInteractivity.addFile`: {file: path or URL (written as given, without surrounding spaces)} link a JavaScript file to the document, after the others: SVG export writes a <script xlink:href> for each linked file. A file already linked stays where it is; a document links at most 64 files → {files}
- `svgInteractivity.removeFile`: {file} unlink a JavaScript file from the document → {files}

## swatch

- `swatch.new` (Window › Swatches): {name?, color? \| colors?: [colour] (one swatch each, in one undo step) \| swatch? \| gradient? \| pattern?: name (default: the current fill), tint?: 0..100 (% of the global or spot `swatch`, or of the one the current fill links to), mode?: "gray"\|"rgb"\|"hsb"\|"lab"\|"cmyk"\|"web" (convert the colour; lab: CIE L*a*b*, how spot colours are usually defined), global?, spot? (a spot colour, always global), group?: colour group name (solid colours only; default: ungrouped), none?: true (the None paint), paint?: paint.setFill params, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill)} save a colour, gradient or pattern as a swatch. A tint of a global or spot colour (below 100%, without mode, global or spot) becomes a tint swatch, "Name 40%", linked to its base: it follows edits to the base and applies as that tint of it. Names are unique ("Sky 2"); a colour's default name is its values ("C=10 M=20 Y=30 K=0", "R=255 G=128 B=0", "Gray K=40", "L=55 a=60 b=40") → {name, names: [every new swatch]}
- `swatch.delete` (Window › Swatches): {name? \| names?: [swatch or colour group names] (a group goes with its swatches), unlink?: true (art using a deleted global swatch keeps its colour, unlinked; false keeps the stale link)} delete in one undo step → {deleted, unlinked: paints unlinked}
- `swatch.newGroup` (Window › Swatches): {name?, swatches?: [names] (solid colours move into the group; gradients, patterns and None stay), colors?: [colour] (added as new swatches), keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), fromArtwork?: false (add the unique colours of the selected art: fills, strokes, text, gradient stops and mesh points; a colour linked to a global swatch moves that swatch into the group), toGlobal?: true (with fromArtwork: the new swatches are global and the selected art's matching unlinked colours link to them), includeTints?: false (with fromArtwork: tints of global swatches also get swatches of their own)} → {name, swatches: [names in the group], linked: paints linked}
- `swatch.duplicate` (Window › Swatches): {name}
- `swatch.sortByName` (Window › Swatches): {}
- `swatch.edit` (Window › Swatches): {name, newName?, color? (e.g. {l, a, b} for a Lab colour), keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill; the swatch's own color and a color given with mode keep their model), mode?: "gray"\|"rgb"\|"hsb"\|"lab"\|"cmyk"\|"web" (convert the colour), global?, spot? (spot colours are always global), paint?: paint.setFill params ({color}, {gradient}, {swatch} or {pattern}) replacing the swatch's colour; gradient or pattern with one of the same kind} edit a swatch in any colour group, as one undo step. Fills, strokes, text, gradient stops and tint swatches linked to a global swatch take its new colour (at their own tint) and name; turning Global off unlinks them (they keep their colour). Colour, mode and spot apply to solid colours only; a tint swatch only takes a new name (edit its base) → {name, relinked: paints changed}
- `swatch.list`: {group?: name (only that colour group's swatches)} → {swatches: [{name, kind: "none"\|"color"\|"gradient"\|"pattern", group, global, spot, color?, hex?, tintOf?: base swatch of a tint swatch, tint?: its %, gradient?, pattern?}] (the built-in [Registration] after None: it prints on every plate and can't be edited, moved or deleted), groups: [{name, swatches: [names]}]}
- `swatch.move` (Window › Swatches): {name? \| names?: [swatch names, in order; or colour group names to reorder the groups], to?: index in the destination (they go before the swatch or group now there; default: the end), group?: destination colour group (default: the ungrouped swatches; groups hold solid colours only)} reorder swatches or move them into or out of colour groups, as one undo step → {moved}
- `swatch.addUsedColors` (Window › Swatches): {selection?: false (only the selected art's colours; default: all the art's), global?: false (the new swatches are global and the matching unlinked colours in that art link to them)} add a swatch for each colour used (fills, strokes, text, gradient stops, mesh points) that no solid swatch has yet, as one undo step → {added: [names], linked}
- `swatch.unused` (Window › Swatches): {} → {names: [the swatches nothing uses, in panel order]}: a colour is used when a paint links to it or an unlinked paint, gradient stop or mesh point has its colour; a gradient when a gradient links to it or has its stops; a pattern when it fills or strokes anything (art, symbols, pattern tiles and graphic styles count). None is never listed
- `swatch.merge` (Window › Swatches): {names: [two or more solid-colour swatches], name?: the merged swatch's name (one swatch kept)} keep the first (its name and colour) and delete the others; paints linked to them take its colour and link to it (or unlink when it isn't global), as one undo step → {name, merged: [deleted names], relinked}
