- `swatch.ungroup` (Window › Swatches): {name: colour group} move its swatches to the end of the ungrouped swatches and remove the group, as one undo step → {swatches: [names]}
- `swatch.sortByKind` (Window › Swatches): {} order the swatches of each list by kind (None first, then process colours, spot colours, gradients, patterns; colour groups stay after them), keeping their order within a kind, as one undo step
- `swatch.spotOptions` (Window › Swatches): {useLab?: bool (omitted: unchanged)} the document's Spot Colors options: spot colours defined in Lab show, print and export from their Lab values (true, the default: PDF Separation spaces get a Lab alternate) or from their working-CMYK equivalents (false: a DeviceCMYK alternate, matching older files). Art, gradient stops and tint swatches linked to Lab spot swatches take the chosen colour at their tint, as one undo step → {useLab, relinked: paints changed}
- `swatch.editGroup` (Window › Swatches): {group: colour group, colors: [colour] (hex, key, {c,m,y,k} or {l,a,b}; the group's swatches take them in order, extra colours become new swatches, swatches past the end are removed; built-in swatches can't change; a color the swatch already has keeps its model), keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), rename?: new group name} rewrite a colour group as one undo step; art linked to its global swatches takes their new colours (and keeps its colour, unlinked, when its swatch goes) → {name, swatches: [names], relinked}
- `swatch.setSpot`: {name, spot?: true} make a swatch a spot colour (prints on its own plate; spot swatches are global) → {name, spot}
- `swatch.library.list`: {} → {libraries: [{id, name, category: "builtIn"\|"gradients"\|"patterns"\|"user" (User Defined: the library files in the user library folder, rescanned now)\|"loaded" (swatch.library.load), count, path?}], userFolder} every library the library panel opens, in menu order
- `swatch.library.get`: {library: id or name} → {id, name, category, swatches: [{name, kind, group, global, spot, color?, hex?, gradient?, pattern?}] (as swatch.list), groups: [{name, swatches: [names]}]}
- `swatch.library.add` (Window › Swatch Libraries): {library: id or name, names?: [swatch or colour group names in the library] (default: all of it; a group comes as a colour group, a single swatch ungrouped), apply?: "fill"\|"stroke" (also apply the first one to the selection and the defaults, as paint.setFill / paint.setStroke), focus?: true (with apply: false keeps the active proxy)} copy library swatches into the document as one undo step. A swatch the document already has (same name and paint) isn't added again; a name taken by another swatch gets a number ("Clay 1 2"). A pattern swatch brings its pattern, as pasting art painted with it does: the same pattern the document has is used, and a name another pattern or swatch has gets a number → {library, added: [names in the document], existing: [names already there], applied?: name}
- `swatch.resetDefaults` (Window › Swatch Libraries): {replace?: false} bring back the default swatches and colour groups of the document's colour mode that are missing (by name), as one undo step; replace: true makes the swatches exactly the defaults (art linked to a removed global swatch keeps its colour, unlinked) → {added: [names]}
- `swatch.library.save` (Window › Swatches): {path?, format?: "vcswatches" (lossless JSON: colour models, global, spot, gradients, groups) \| "gpl" (8-bit RGB palette; groups as `# Group:` comments) \| "ase" (swatch exchange, binary: solid colors in their own model (RGB, CMYK, Lab, Gray) as global, spot or process colors, and color groups; gradients are left out, and a tint swatch is saved as the color it shows) \| "css" (custom properties on :root) (default: the path's extension, else vcswatches), names?: [swatch or colour group names] (default: all; None and patterns are never saved), name?: library name (default: the document's), user?: false (save into the user library folder, listed under User Defined)} save the document's swatches as a library → {path, format, count: the swatches written (gpl and ase hold solid colors only, and css leaves freeform gradients out), library?: id when saved to the user folder}; without path or user → {data: the file's text, format, count}, or {dataBase64, format, count} for ase
- `swatch.library.load` (Window › Swatch Libraries): {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, name?: file name (default: the path's)} load a .vcswatches, .gpl, .ase (swatch exchange) or .acb (color book, read only) library, or the swatches of any document VectorCraft opens (see document.formats), for the library panel (Window → Swatch Libraries lists it until the app quits; a file of the user library folder, or a file with the same extension and bytes as one there, opens as that User Defined library) → {library: id, name, count}
- `swatch.library.copyToUser` (Window › Swatch Libraries): {library: id or name of a loaded library (category "loaded", swatch.library.load)} copy it into the user library folder of the desktop app, whose libraries VectorCraft lists as User Defined every time it starts (a loaded library is listed until the app quits): a library file as it is, the swatches of a document or a library loaded from data as a .vcswatches file. A name the folder already has gets a number ("Brand 2.ase") and no file is replaced; when the folder already holds the same file, that file is used. The library is then listed as User Defined only, and until the app quits commands given its loaded id use the copy; a User Defined library comes back as it is → {library: its User Defined id, name, count, path, copied: false when nothing was written}

## symbol

- `symbol.list`: {} → {symbols: [{name, instances, size: [w,h], registration: 0..8, exportType: movieClip\|graphic, nineSlice, type: static\|dynamic}], current}
- `symbol.new`: {name?, ids?\|id?, registration?: 0..8 (9-point grid, 4 = centre; Symbol Options › Registration), exportType?: movieClip (default)\|graphic, nineSlice?: bool, nineSliceGuides?: array, type?: static (default)\|dynamic (Symbol Options › Symbol Type)} make a symbol from the selection and replace it with an instance; a frame of threaded text in the selection leaves its thread with the text it shows, which the symbol's art holds; nineSlice is Symbol Options › Enable Guides for 9-Slice Scaling, nineSliceGuides [left, top, right, bottom] as fractions 0..1 of the natural size (default a quarter each); an instance of a symbol with guides keeps its corners' size when scaled, its edges stretch along their length and its center in both directions → {name, id, registration, exportType, nineSlice, type}
- `symbol.options`: {name?, newName?, registration?: 0..8, exportType?: movieClip\|graphic, nineSlice?: bool, nineSliceGuides?: array, type?: static\|dynamic} rename a symbol and/or set Symbol Options › Registration, Export Type, 9-slice guides (nineSliceGuides [left, top, right, bottom] fractions 0..1) and Symbol Type (a dynamic symbol's instances may change the appearance of objects in its art and stay linked; making it static drops those changes); default name: current → {name, registration, exportType, nineSlice, type}
- `symbol.place`: {name?, x?, y?} place an instance so its registration point is at (x, y) (default: artboard centre; name default: current) → {id}
- `symbol.breakLink`: {ids?\|id?} expand selected instances into plain art (with their appearance changes to a dynamic symbol), placed through the symbol's 9-slice guides when it has them → {ids}
- `symbol.edit`: {id?, ids?: [id…] (instead of id), name?, x?, y?, artboard?: index=0 (the active artboard; the app passes it), viewCenter?: [x, y] (the app passes the view's center)} edit a symbol's art in symbol editing mode: the instance `id` (default: the selected instances, which must share one symbol) where it shows the art, else the symbol `name` (default: the current symbol) centered on (x, y) (default: the center of artboard `artboard`, or of the first artboard when there is no such artboard; in a document without artboards, viewCenter, else (0, 0)); every instance follows each edit and the instance stays linked (symbol.breakLink expands one); refused while a pattern or an opacity mask is edited → {name, layer, id?}
- `symbol.exitEditing`: {} leave symbol editing mode; after an edit, select the instance it was entered from; leaving without having changed the art adds no undo step and restores the selection from before entry (object.exitIsolation also leaves it) → {name?}
- `symbol.update`: {name, ids?\|id?} replace the symbol's art with the selection (instances update, keeping their appearance changes to the objects at paths the new art still has; the selection becomes an instance; a frame of threaded text in the selection leaves its thread with the text it shows, which the symbol's art holds) → {name, id}
- `symbol.delete`: {name \| names: [name…], expandInstances?: true} delete a symbol (or several, in one undo step); its instances are expanded (or deleted with false)
- `symbol.duplicate`: {name, newName?} → {name}
- `symbol.replace`: {name, ids?\|id?} swap the symbol of the selected instances (an instance given a different symbol drops its appearance changes; one given its own symbol keeps them) → {ids}
- `symbol.instance.setAppearance`: {id?\|ids?: [one id] (default: the one selected object), path: [i…] (the object in the symbol's art: child indices in paint order from the art's root down through its groups; [] the root), fill?: "#rrggbb"\|{…paint params of paint.setFill: color, none, swatch, tint, gradient, keepModel}\|null (null: the symbol's own again), stroke?: as fill, opacity?: 0..100\|null, blend?: name\|null} change the appearance of one object of a dynamic symbol instance's art, for that instance only: it stays linked, so editing the symbol reshapes it and keeps the change; refused for a static symbol or a path naming no object → {id, overrides: how many objects the instance changes}
- `symbol.instance.reset`: {ids?\|id?} drop the selected dynamic symbol instances' own appearance changes, so they show the symbol's appearance again → {ids: the instances that had changes}
- `symbol.setCurrent`: {name} the symbol the symbolism tools spray (not an undo step)
- `symbol.unused`: {} → {names: [the symbols nothing places, in panel order]} (the Symbols panel selects them): instances in the art (opacity masks included), graph designs, pattern tiles (with the tiles that pattern editing's Cancel puts back) and brush art count, and so do instances in the art of a symbol that is used
- `symbol.selectInstances`: {name} select every instance of a symbol → {count}
- `symbol.spray`: {points: [[x,y]…], name?, radius?: 40, density?: 1..10 (5), scale?: 1, alt?: bool (remove instances)} spray instances into a symbol set → {id, count}
- `symbol.adjust`: {tool: shift\|scrunch\|size\|spin\|stain\|screen\|style, points: [[x,y]…], radius?: 40, intensity?: 1..10 (5), alt?: bool, color?, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), style?: name} adjust instances near the points (selection, else all) → {count}
- `symbol.libraries`: {} → {libraries: [{id, name, category: "builtIn"\|"user" (User Defined: the .vectorcraft files in the user library folder, rescanned now)\|"loaded" (symbol.loadLibrary), count, path?}], userFolder} every symbol library the library panel opens, in menu order
- `symbol.library`: {library: id or name} → {id, name, category, symbols: [{name, size: [w, h], registration: 0..8, exportType}] (as symbol.list)}
- `symbol.addFromLibrary` (Window › Symbol Libraries): {library: id or name, name? \| names?: [symbol names in the library] (default: all of it), place?: false (also place an instance of the first one, its registration point at x, y: default the first artboard's centre), x?, y?, swatchConflict? (as edit.paste)} copy library symbols into the document's Symbols as one undo step (with the place), with their Symbol Options and the resources they use (the symbols they nest, patterns, swatches, brushes, graphic styles, images). A symbol the document already has (same name and art) isn't added again; a name another symbol has gets a number ("Star 2"). The first one becomes the current symbol → {library, names: [each symbol's name in the document], added: [names added], existing: [names already there], placed?: id}
- `symbol.saveLibrary` (Window › Symbols): {path?, names?: [symbol names] (default: all), name?: library name (default: the document's), user?: false (save into the user library folder, listed under User Defined)} save the document's symbols, with their Symbol Options and the resources they use, as a symbol library: a .vectorcraft document holding only them → {path, count, library?: id when saved to the user folder}; without path or user → {dataBase64: the file's bytes, count}
- `symbol.loadLibrary` (Window › Symbol Libraries): {path? \| data?: file text \| dataBase64?, name?: file name (default: the path's)} load the symbols of any document VectorCraft opens (see document.formats), a saved symbol library included, for the library panel (Window → Symbol Libraries lists it until the app quits; a file of the user library folder, or a file with the same bytes as one there, opens as that User Defined library) → {library: id, name, count}

## text

- `text.create`: {x, y, text, rotation?: deg (about x, y), vertical?: bool = false, size?: pt, font?: family, style? (default Regular, or the family's closest style when it has no Regular), color?, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), area?: {width, height, fit?: none\|autoHeight\|shrinkText, fitMinPercent?}, placeholder?: bool (fill it with placeholder text instead, as the Type tools do with prefs placeholderText), justify?: "auto" (default: the start of each paragraph's direction)\|"left"\|"center"\|"right"\|"justifyAll" (point type: aligned on x, so "center" centres each line on the point and "right" ends it there; text.setStyle changes it later), leadingModel?: "romanBaseline"\|"emBoxTop", charAlign?: "romanBaseline"\|"emBoxTop"\|"emBoxCenter"\|"emBoxBottom"\|"icfTop"\|"icfBottom" (default: emBoxTop and emBoxCenter while the interface is in Japanese, else romanBaseline)} → {id, bounds: {x, y, width, height} (the new type's box as document.inspect reports it, after justify), overflow?: [id] (area type whose text doesn't fit its frame: the lines past it aren't drawn; left out when it fits), warning?: text (the font or style given isn't available: the type is drawn in the fallback font or the family's closest style)}; new area type gets Auto Size (fit autoHeight) when the autoSizeAreaType preference is on
- `text.setText`: {id?\|ids?, text} replace the contents of text objects (keeps the first run's style) → {ids, overflow?: [id…] (area type and type on a path whose text no longer fits; left out when all fits)}
- `text.areaOptions` (Type): {ids?\|id?, width?: pt, height?: pt, rows?, columns?, gutter?: pt (between rows and columns), rowGutter?: pt\|null (between rows when it differs from gutter, which is then between columns; gutter given alone sets both; the reply gives the rows' gutter either way), inset?: pt, firstBaseline?: ascent\|capHeight\|xHeight\|leading\|fixed, firstBaselineMin?: pt, verticalAlign?: top\|center\|bottom\|justify, flow?: columns\|rows (Text Flow: down each column's rows, then across, the default; or across each row's columns, then down), fit?: none\|autoHeight\|shrinkText, fitMinPercent?: 10..100, …the keys of its own reply (it can be passed back; the values it only reports are ignored)} set the selected area type's options; width and height size the type area from its top-left corner, along the type's own axes, and the text reflows at its size (with fit autoHeight, Auto Size, the frame's height follows the text after every edit and height is ignored; setting the height by hand turns it off); fit shrinkText scales overflowing text down (size, leading, baseline shift) by the largest factor down to fitMinPercent % (default 50) that makes it fit (none given: query) → the first object's options, plus overflow (the text doesn't fit its frame) and fitScale (Shrink Text's factor, 1 unshrunk)
- `text.reshapeArea`: {id, anchors: [[subpath, anchor]…], dx, dy} or {items: [{id, anchors}…], dx, dy} move anchors of area type's frame (the type area) by dx, dy points, with their handles; the text reflows at its size, through its thread too (what Direct Selection does dragging a frame corner or edge, or the frame anchors its marquee selected, and the arrow keys nudging those); with items, every listed frame moves in one step
- `text.reshapePath`: {id, anchors: [[subpath, anchor]…], dx, dy} move anchors of type on a path's path by dx, dy points, with their handles; the start and end brackets keep their fractions of the path and the text reflows along it (what Direct Selection does dragging an anchor of the path)
- `text.fitHeadline` (Type): {ids?\|id?} track the first line of area type so it fills the frame width → {tracking}
- `text.setStyle`: {ids?\|id?, font?, style? (font alone: the style is kept when the new family has it, else its closest), size?: pt, leading?: pt\|"auto", tracking?: 1/1000 em, justify?: "auto" (the start of each paragraph's direction)\|"left"\|"center"\|"right"\|"justifyAll", fill?: colour, keepModel?: bool (as paint.setFill), features?: ["dlig", "-liga", …] OpenType, featureChanges?: ["tnum", "-onum", …] (OpenType features turned on, or with - off, on top of each character's own; its other features are kept), start?: byte, end?: byte} (size/leading are document points after object scaling; with a range: the character attributes style that range and justify the paragraphs it touches; without: all the text) → {ids, overflow?: [id…] (area type and type on a path whose text no longer fits; left out when all fits), warning?: text (the font or style set isn't available: its type is drawn in the fallback font or the family's closest style)}
- `text.editRange`: {id, start: byte, end: byte, insert?: string, runs?: [{text, style}]} replace bytes start..end of the plain text (inserted text takes the replaced text's style unless styled `runs` are given) → {id, caret}
- `text.startList`: {id, at?: byte (default: the end of the text)} start a list from typed characters, what the Type tool runs when a space is typed on its own: when the text from the start of the paragraph that `at` is in up to `at` is "1. " or "1) " (a numbered list in 1, 2, 3), "A. " or "A) " (a numbered list in A, B, C), or "* ", "# ", "> " or "+ " (a bulleted list with the filled dot), and the paragraph is in no list, those characters are removed and the paragraph joins that list as one undo step (left and first-line indents of 0 become 18 and -18, as text.setFormat sets them); otherwise nothing changes → {id, started: bool, caret: byte (the paragraph's start when started, else `at`)}
- `text.setRangeStyle`: {id? (default: the one selected type object), start?: byte, end?: byte (default: all text), font?, style?, size?: pt, leading?: pt\|"auto", tracking?, kerning?: 1/1000 em\|"auto"\|"optical", baselineShift?: pt, hScale?: %, vScale?: %, rotation?: deg, fill?: colour\|"none", stroke?: colour\|"none", keepModel?: bool (fill and stroke, as paint.setFill), strokeWidth?: pt, strokeOptions?: {weight?, cap?, join?, miterLimit?, align?, dash?, dashOffset?, alignDashes?} (as stroke.set: the character stroke), underline?, strikethrough?, allCaps?: bool, smallCaps?: bool, position?: "normal"\|"superscript"\|"subscript" (sizes from Document Setup), features?: ["dlig", "-liga", …], featureChanges?: ["tnum", "-onum", …] (OpenType features turned on, or with - off, on top of each character's own; its other features are kept), charAlign?: "romanBaseline"\|"emBoxTop"\|"emBoxCenter"\|"emBoxBottom"\|"icfTop"\|"icfBottom", proportionalMetrics?: bool (full-width glyphs on the font's proportional widths: `palt` in horizontal type, `vpal` in vertical type), tateChuYoko?: bool (Character panel menu › Tate-chu-yoko, with Show East Asian Options: in vertical type those characters stand side by side across one em of the column; kept on the characters and ignored in horizontal type), tcyUpDown?, tcyLeftRight?: pt -1296..1296 (Character panel menu › Tate-chu-yoko Settings: in vertical type a block of tate-chu-yoko characters moves up by a positive Up/Down and down by a negative one, right by a positive Left/Right and left by a negative one, as one piece with the values of its first character; 0 by default; automatic digit blocks and horizontal type do not move; neither turns tateChuYoko on), standardVerticalRoman?: bool (Character panel menu › Standard Vertical Roman Alignment, with Show East Asian Options: in vertical type those half-width characters, such as Latin letters and digits, stand upright one below the other instead of being rotated; spaces and tate-chu-yoko blocks are not changed; kept on the characters and ignored in horizontal type; off by default), noBreak?: bool (Character panel menu › No Break: these characters stay on one line, including across a space inside the run), charDirection?: "default"\|"leftToRight"\|"rightToLeft" (Character panel menu › Character Direction: the characters are set left to right or right to left inside their paragraph, as a Unicode bidi embedding; default: as the bidi algorithm sets them), digits?: "default"\|"arabic"\|"hindi"\|"farsi"\|"devanagari"\|"bengali"\|"gurmukhi"\|"gujarati"\|"odia"\|"tamil"\|"telugu"\|"kannada"\|"malayalam" (Character panel menu › Digits: the numeral system every digit is drawn in, whatever was typed: Western, Arabic-Indic (hindi), Eastern Arabic-Indic (farsi) or an Indic script's own digits), fontHeightReference?: "emBox"\|"capHeight"\|"xHeight"\|"icfBox" (Character panel › Set Font Height Reference: the height of the font that size measures, the em box by default; with a font that has no such height, or one outside 0.1 to 2 em, the em box), language?: name\|tag (Character panel › Language: one of Document Setup's languages by name or BCP 47 tag, case-insensitive, such as "Turkish" or "tr"; the characters are shaped in it, so the font uses that language's forms (OpenType `locl`); saved as the tag, English: USA, the default, as none; new type takes the document's language), fontAxes?: {"wght": 650, …} (Character panel › Variable Font: the setting of each variation axis named, in the axis's units and clamped to its range; null: back to the style's own setting; other axes keep theirs; tags the font has no axis for are left out; a font or style change clears them; text.fontList {family, style} lists the axes)} style a character range (size/leading/baselineShift are document points and hScale includes the object transform; runs are split at the range ends) → {id, runs, warning?: text (the font or style set isn't available: its type is drawn in the fallback font or the family's closest style)}
- `text.touchType`: {id, start: byte (a character of it), dx?: pt, dy?: pt, hScale?: %, vScale?: %, rotation?: deg} change one character (a ligature, one glyph for several characters, as a whole) as a Touch Type tool drag does, as one undo step: dx moves it along its line in reading order by changing the kerning of the character before it on the line and its own kerning, and the characters after it stay where they are (an error for the first character of a line and for a right-to-left character; limited to what kerning from -1000 to 10000 can give); dy raises it (baseline shift, up from its line whatever its rotation, also for the first character of a line and a right-to-left character); dx and dy are document points; hScale (including the object transform), vScale and rotation set its scales and rotation as text.setRangeStyle does. The kerning of the two characters also keeps the character and the ones after it where they were along the line when the font kerned them with their neighbors; a wider or narrower character moves the ones after it by the change in its width → {id, start, end} (the character's bytes)
- `text.getRange`: {id, start?, end?, effective?: bool = false} → {text, runs: [{text, style}], length}; effective uses document-point size/leading/baseline shift and transformed horizontal scale; default returns stored local styles for rich-text copying
- `text.createInPath`: {path: id, mode: "area"\|"onPath", text?: "", vertical?: bool = false, at?: [x, y] (on-path start: nearest point), size?, font?, style?, color?, keepModel? (as text.create), fit?: none\|autoHeight\|shrinkText, fitMinPercent? (area: as text.areaOptions; default autoHeight when the autoSizeAreaType preference is on), placeholder?: bool (placeholder text instead, as text.create), justify?, leadingModel?, charAlign? (as text.create)} turn a path into an area-type frame or a type-on-a-path baseline (the path's paint is dropped) → {id, bounds: {x, y, width, height} (as text.create), overflow?: [id] (the text doesn't fit; left out when it fits)}
- `text.discardEmpty`: {id} remove text object `id` if it has no characters (what the Type tool does with point type it placed when editing ends). When nothing but that text changed since the step that created it, the steps since are dropped instead, leaving no trace in the history → {removed}
- `text.insertInline` (Type): {id?: text id (default: the text the Type tool edits), at?: byte offset (default: the Type tool's selection, which it replaces; clamped to the text), symbol?: symbol name (default: the Symbols panel's current symbol), scale?: art height ÷ font size = 1 (0 < scale ≤ 100), shift?: pt raise = 0} place a document symbol inline in the text as one character (U+FFFC) that flows like a glyph: its art is scaled to `scale` × the font size tall, its vertical centre on the middle of the cap height raised by `shift`. Typing, Backspace and Delete over it remove it whole → {id, caret}
- `text.fonts`: {selectionOnly?} (the type in the art of the symbols in scope included: all of the document's, or those the selected instances show) → [{family, style, runs, objects, missing, status: exact\|substitute\|missing, resolved: {family, style}, missingGlyphs, version?: the installed version the type names (when it names one)}] sorted by name. A font is found by any of its names (ヒラギノ角ゴシック = Hiragino Sans) among the loaded and installed fonts; substitute: the family is there but not the style (resolved names the stand-in); missing: the family is unknown (the fallback family stands in); missingGlyphs: characters of its text the resolved font lacks (drawn by a fallback font)
- `text.replaceFont`: {from: {family, style?}, to: {family (any of its names; stored as the font's own family name), style?}, selectionOnly?} (style omitted: every style of the family / keep the closest style); the type in the art of the symbols in scope changes too (all of the document's, or those the selected instances show, so every instance of them follows); text that is hidden or locked, in a hidden or locked group or layer (or group of a symbol's art), or on a locked artboard keeps its font → {runs, skipped?: the text objects left in the font, warning?: text (some text was left in its font, or the style set isn't available: its type is drawn in the family's closest style)}
- `text.fontList`: {family?, style?} → {families: [names]} sorted: the bundled fonts, fonts added and the fonts installed on the system (none on the web), each the family's English (canonical) name for `text.setStyle` and documents (without the system's hidden families, whose names start with a dot; they still resolve by name). Preferences › Type › Show Font Names in English only changes the UI menu labels, not this list; with family: {family, styles: [names] (upright styles by weight, then italics; an error when the family isn't available), axes: [{tag, name, min, default, max, value, hidden}] (a variable font's variation axes, the Character panel's Variable Font sliders, with `value` the setting of `style`, else of the first style; empty for a static font; `hidden`: the font asks for the axis not to be shown)}
- `text.rescanFonts`: {} scan the system font folders again, for fonts installed or removed since the app started; text in a font that became available redraws in it, and text in a family that was removed redraws in the fallback font (no fonts are installed on the web) → {families, faces} (families listed, installed faces found)
- `text.missingFonts`: {} → {fonts: [{family, style, status: missing\|substitute, resolved: {family, style}}] (sorted by name; at most 1000), count, fontsNextToDocument?} the fonts the active document's type uses, in its layers and its symbols, that aren't available as named: missing (the family is unknown and the fallback family stands in) or substitute (the family is there but not the style; resolved names the stand-in), as text.fonts says. CSS generic families such as sans-serif and names longer than 256 bytes are left out. fontsNextToDocument: the Fonts folder next to the saved document (File › Package writes it), when there is one and a search may start there (not in another app's folders, such as a mail app's attachments)
- `text.findFontFiles`: {folder?: an absolute path, fonts?: [{family, style?}] (1 to 1000; default: the active document's missing fonts, as text.missingFonts lists them), maxSeconds?: 60 (1 to 600), stop?: false (true: stop the last search)} with folder: look in it and its subfolders for font files that make the fonts available, on separate threads, and answer at once (state: searching); without folder: the last search's state, with the files found so far. A search reads the table directories and the name, fvar and OS/2 tables of .ttf, .otf, .ttc and .otc files of at most 256 MB and, on macOS, of suitcase fonts (files without an extension or with .suit, whose fonts are in a resource fork of at most 8 MB), and lists a file for a font when the file, added with text.addFontFiles, makes the font resolve exactly as text.fonts resolves it; fonts that resolve exactly already are left out. It doesn't enter app packages, folders named Program Files (also x86 and Arm), ProgramData, $Recycle.Bin or System Volume Information, the system's folders at the root of a volume, the Shared and Public folders in Users, a home folder's Library, AppData, Applications, snap and dot folders, a Library folder's app data folders or the folders the environment names for apps' data, and a search of a folder inside one of them fails; the walk also skips other hidden folders, folders whose contents are in the cloud and folders more than 64 levels deep, and follows no links (a picked path is resolved through its links); none on the web → {id (absent while idle), state: idle\|searching\|done\|stopped\|failed, folder?, fonts: [{family, style, status, files: [paths]}], searched?: {folders, files, fontFiles}, skipped?, unreadable?, stopped?: stop\|time\|limit, error?, seconds?}
- `text.addFontFiles`: {files: [paths the last text.findFontFiles search found] (1 to 1000)} copy the font files into VectorCraft's own Fonts folder (next to the preferences; the desktop app and vectorcraft-cli read it), never replacing a file there: the names Name.otf, Name 2.otf … Name 99.otf are tried in turn until one is free, which gets the copy, or is a file with the same contents, which is kept; then scan the fonts again, as text.rescanFonts does, and type set in them redraws. Only regular .ttf, .otf, .ttc, .otc, .woff and .woff2 files and suitcase fonts (with their resource fork) are copied, at most 256 MB a file and 1 GB in all; any other file is skipped. Add only fonts you own or are licensed to install; none on the web → {folder, copied: [{from, to}], kept: [{from, to}], skipped: [{file, reason}], families?, faces?}
- `text.thread.create` (Type › Threaded Text): {} thread the selected area type and type on a path (and closed paths, which become area frames) in stacking order → {ids}
- `text.thread.add`: {from, to?, rect?: [x, y, width, height], at?: [x, y], before?} thread area type or type on a path `from` (a click on its out port) into one more frame, put after it in its thread, or before it with `before: true` (a click on its in port: the story starts there and flows on into `from`): closed path, area type or type on a path `to`, a new rectangle `rect`, or a new frame of `from`'s kind, shape and size with its top-left at `at` (give one of the three) → {id}; the two frames are selected
- `text.thread.releaseSelection` (Type › Threaded Text): {} take the selected frames out of their threads (the story re-flows through the rest)
- `text.thread.remove` (Type › Threaded Text): {} break the selected frames' threads; each frame keeps the text it shows
- `text.thread.break`: {id, port: "in"\|"out"} break the thread of area type or type on a path `id` before it (its in port) or after it (its out port), as a double-click on that port does: the story flows into the frames before the break, and the frames after it are left empty (still threaded with each other when two or more)
- `text.thread.info`: {} → [[id…]] every thread in order
- `text.tabs.set` (Window › Tabs): {stops: [{position: pt, align?: left\|center\|right\|decimal, leader?: ". ", alignOn?: "."}], ids?\|id?, start?: byte, end?: byte} replace the tab stops of the selected text (with a range: of the paragraphs it touches)
- `text.tabs.get`: {ids?\|id?, start?: byte} → {stops} of the first selected text object (its paragraph at `start`; default the first)
- `text.tabs.clear` (Window › Tabs): {ids?\|id?, start?: byte, end?: byte}
- `text.setFormat`: {ids?\|id?, kerning?: 1/1000 em\|"auto"\|"optical", baselineShift?: pt, hScale?: %, vScale?: %, rotation?: deg, underline?, strikethrough?, allCaps?, smallCaps?: bool, position?: "normal"\|"superscript"\|"subscript" (sizes from Document Setup), leftIndent?, rightIndent?, firstLineIndent?, spaceBefore?, spaceAfter?: pt (±1296), list?: "none"\|"bullet"\|"number", bullet?: "none"\|"dot"\|"dash"\|"circle"\|"square", number?: "none"\|"arabic"\|"upperLetters"\|"lowerLetters"\|"upperRoman"\|"lowerRoman"\|"twoDigits"\|"threeDigits" (Paragraph panel › Bullets and Numbered List: each paragraph of a list starts with its marker, drawn in the font, size and color of the paragraph's first character, and its text starts at the next tab stop after the marker, the left indent counting as a stop; a bulleted list's marker is its bullet, and a numbered list's is the paragraph's number and a period: 1, 2, 3; A to Z, then AA, BB; a to z, then aa, bb; I, II, III; i, ii, iii; 01 to 99, then 100; 001 to 999, then 1000; letters and roman numerals past 3999 in Arabic digits; the numbers count the story's numbered paragraphs, its threaded frames included, from 1 after a paragraph in no list, and an empty or bulleted paragraph is not counted; list "bullet" keeps a list's bullet, else a dot, and list "number" keeps a numbered list's style, else arabic; without list, a number style makes a numbered list and a bullet makes a bulleted one; "none" in any of the three turns the list off; a paragraph that joins a list with left and first-line indents of 0 gets 18 and -18 unless the call sets an indent; a horizontal right-to-left list paragraph has its indents mirrored, measured from the right), hyphenate?: bool, wordsLongerThan?: letters 1..25 (5), afterFirst?, beforeLast?: letters 1..15 (3), hyphenLimit?: lines 0..25 (0: no limit), hyphenationZone?: pt 0..1296 (0; Single-line Composer only), hyphenateCapitalized?: bool (true) (Paragraph panel menu › Hyphenation…: only words longer than wordsLongerThan break, keeping afterFirst letters before the hyphen and beforeLast after it; at most hyphenLimit lines in a row end with a hyphen; a line whose text already ends within hyphenationZone of its right edge breaks before the next word instead of hyphenating it; the counts are rounded, and every value is clamped to its range), wordSpacing?: % 0..1000 (80, 100, 133), letterSpacing?: % of a word space -100..500 (0, 0, 0), glyphScaling?: % 50..200 (100, 100, 100): each [min, desired, max] or {min?, desired?, max?} (Paragraph panel menu › Justification…: Desired applies to every line, Minimum and Maximum bound what justified lines may use; values are clamped to their range, and Minimum ≤ Desired ≤ Maximum must hold afterwards or nothing changes), autoLeading?: % of the size 0..500 (120; the leading of characters whose leading is Auto), singleWordJustification?: "fullJustify"\|"alignLeft"\|"alignCenter"\|"alignRight" (a justified line holding one word), mojikumi?: "none"\|"lineEndHalf" (Japanese punctuation spacing), kinsoku?: "none"\|"hard"\|"soft" (Kinsoku Set: Soft lets 々, ー and small kana start a line), direction?: "auto"\|"leftToRight"\|"rightToLeft" (paragraph direction; auto: from each paragraph's first strong character), leadingModel?: "romanBaseline"\|"emBoxTop" (leading measured baseline to baseline, or em box top to top), charAlign?: "romanBaseline"\|"emBoxTop"\|"emBoxCenter"\|"emBoxBottom"\|"icfTop"\|"icfBottom" (where characters smaller than the largest on their line line up with it), proportionalMetrics?: bool (full-width glyphs on the font's proportional widths: `palt` in horizontal type, `vpal` in vertical type), tateChuYoko?: bool (Character panel menu › Tate-chu-yoko, with Show East Asian Options: in vertical type those characters stand side by side across one em of the column; kept on the characters and ignored in horizontal type), tcyUpDown?, tcyLeftRight?: pt -1296..1296 (Character panel menu › Tate-chu-yoko Settings, as text.setRangeStyle: a tate-chu-yoko block moves up or down, right or left), standardVerticalRoman?: bool (Character panel menu › Standard Vertical Roman Alignment, as text.setRangeStyle), noBreak?: bool (Character panel menu › No Break: these characters stay on one line, including across a space inside the run), charDirection?: "default"\|"leftToRight"\|"rightToLeft" (Character panel menu › Character Direction: the characters are set left to right or right to left inside their paragraph, as a Unicode bidi embedding; default: as the bidi algorithm sets them), digits?: "default"\|"arabic"\|"hindi"\|"farsi"\|"devanagari"\|"bengali"\|"gurmukhi"\|"gujarati"\|"odia"\|"tamil"\|"telugu"\|"kannada"\|"malayalam" (Character panel menu › Digits: the numeral system every digit is drawn in, whatever was typed: Western, Arabic-Indic (hindi), Eastern Arabic-Indic (farsi) or an Indic script's own digits), fontHeightReference?: "emBox"\|"capHeight"\|"xHeight"\|"icfBox" (Character panel › Set Font Height Reference, as text.setRangeStyle: the height of the font that the size measures), language?: name\|tag (Character panel › Language, as text.setRangeStyle), fontAxes?: {"wght": 650, …} (Character panel › Variable Font, as text.setRangeStyle: each axis named set in its units and clamped to its range, null back to the style's own setting), burasagari?: "none"\|"standard"\|"forced" (Paragraph panel menu › Burasagari None/Regular/Force: an East Asian comma or full stop ending an area type line hangs outside it: when it doesn't fit, or always; new type: "standard"), romanHangingPunctuation?: bool (Paragraph panel menu › Roman Hanging Punctuation: quotation marks, hyphens, full stops and commas starting or ending a line hang outside the margin, asterisks, tildes, ellipses, dashes, colons and semicolons by half; off by default), kashida?: "none"\|"short"\|"medium"\|"long"\|"stylistic" (Paragraph panel › Insert Kashidas: justified lines of horizontal area type lengthen each Arabic word with a kashida between its last pair of joined letters, at most a quarter, half or whole em long, or whole tatweels up to an em for stylistic, before they widen their word spaces; none by default), composer?: "singleLine"\|"everyLine" (line breaking, Every-line by default), start?: byte, end?: byte} (baselineShift is in document points and hScale includes the object transform; groups include their text descendants; with a range: the character attributes style that range and the paragraph attributes apply to the paragraphs it touches; without: all the text)
- `text.convertListToText`: {ids?\|id?, start?: byte, end?: byte} (Type › Bullets and Numbering › Convert to Text: the paragraphs of the selected type, or of `ids`, or those a start/end range touches) the marker of each list paragraph becomes characters of its text: the bullet, or the number and its period as the paragraph showed it, then a tab, at the start of the paragraph in the marker's font, size and color; the list is turned off. When the marker's tab went to the left indent, the paragraph gets a left-aligned tab stop without a leader there, in place of any stop there, which keeps the text after the tab where it started; when it went to a tab stop and a stop there or past it has a leader or is not left-aligned, or a right, center or decimal stop lies between the marker and that place, the paragraph gets a left-aligned stop without a leader where the marker's tab ended, in place of any stop there and of those right, center and decimal stops before it (past the left indent only in a paragraph with no tab of its own). In threaded type the numbers are those counted through the story's frames, and a paragraph that continues into a later frame is converted where its text starts. An empty list paragraph has no marker and only leaves the list. A paragraph with no direction set gets the direction it ran in when the characters would change it (a letter or roman numeral before right-to-left text). With the Every-line Composer, a paragraph with a tab is broken line by line, so a converted paragraph can break its lines at other places. While the Type tool edits, its typing ends first and its anchor and caret move past the characters inserted at or before them; in threaded type, when the caret's character moved to another frame, the tool edits that frame, which becomes the selection when it was not selected. One undo step → {ids, converted: paragraphs, inserted: [{id, at: byte before the change, length: bytes}]}

## tool

- `tool.select`: {tool} e.g. selection, directSelection, pen, rectangle, ellipse, polygon, star, lineSegment
- `tool.setOption`: {key?, value? \| values?: {key: value…}, tool?: id (default: the active tool)} → the tool's options (`{}` reads them); kept options last across tool switches

## transparency

- `transparency.set` (Window › Transparency): {ids?\|id?, opacity?: 0..100, blend?: name, isolate?, knockout?: "on"\|"off"\|"neutral"\|bool (true = on, false = neutral), knockoutShape?: bool, item?: appearance item index\|null, target?: "object"\|"contents", aligned?: bool (index and item are rows of the first target's stack: each other target edits its equal one, as the Appearance panel does for a mixed selection)} for `ids`, the selection, or the object whose opacity mask is being edited; opacity and blend go to the targeted fill/stroke item (omitted: the Appearance panel's active item, else the objects; a gradient mesh point last clicked with the Mesh tool or Direct Selection takes the opacity itself)
- `transparency.makeOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {ids?, clip?, invert?} the topmost of the selected objects (or of `ids`) becomes the mask of the others (grouped if several; a layer among several stands for its art, so the topmost object of a layer on top is the mask; an object in `ids` that is hidden, locked or on a locked artboard is refused). One object gets an empty mask and enters mask editing: draw the mask, then transparency.stopEditingOpacityMask; one target (an object or a layer) is refused in isolation mode and in pattern editing mode → {id, editing}
- `transparency.releaseOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {id?\|ids?} put the mask art back above each masked object (default: the selection, or the object whose mask is being edited, which leaves mask editing)
- `transparency.disableOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {id?\|ids?} keep the mask but stop applying it
- `transparency.enableOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {id?\|ids?}
- `transparency.unlinkOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {id?\|ids?} the object moves without its mask
- `transparency.linkOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {id?\|ids?}
- `transparency.setOpacityMask`: {id?\|ids?, clip?, invert?, disabled?, linked?} change the opacity masks of `ids`, the selection, or the object whose mask is being edited
- `transparency.toggleNewMasksClipping` (Window › Transparency): {value?} → {value}
- `transparency.toggleNewMasksInverted` (Window › Transparency): {value?} → {value}
- `transparency.opacityMaskInfo`: {id?\|ids?} → [{id, clip, invert, disabled, linked, art}] the opacity masks of `ids`, the selection, or the object whose mask is being edited
- `transparency.info`: {id?\|ids?} → {ids, opacity: 0..100, blend, isolate, knockout: "on"\|"off"\|"neutral", knockoutShape, editingMask, pageIsolatedBlending, pageKnockoutGroup} the Transparency panel's values for `ids`, the selection, or the object whose mask is being edited. A value is null where those objects differ (or there are none); editingMask is the id of the object whose mask is being edited, or null; the page values are the document's
- `transparency.togglePageIsolatedBlending` (Window › Transparency): {value?} make the page an isolated group (default: toggle): blend modes of top-level objects don't blend with what lies under the page; saved with the document and written to PDF → {value}
- `transparency.togglePageKnockoutGroup` (Window › Transparency): {value?} make the page a knockout group (default: toggle): its layers (the contents of neutral layers) hide what they cover instead of showing it through their transparency; saved with the document and written to PDF → {value}
- `transparency.editOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {id?} edit the mask art of `id` (default: the first selected object with a mask) in place, as clicking the mask thumbnail in the Transparency panel does; refused in isolation mode and in pattern editing mode → {layer}
- `transparency.stopEditingOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {} leave mask editing (the object thumbnail) → {id}
- `transparency.viewOpacityMask` (Window › Transparency): {on?: bool (default: toggle), id?} show only the opacity mask of `id` (default: the object whose mask is edited, else the first selected object with a mask) on the canvas, as greyscale coverage, and edit it, as Alt-clicking the mask thumbnail does; off shows the artwork again (still editing); refused in isolation mode and in pattern editing mode → {on, id}

## type

- `type.orientation.vertical` (Type › Orientation): {ids?\|id?} set vertical writing
- `type.orientation.horizontal` (Type › Orientation): {ids?\|id?} set horizontal writing
- `type.createOutlines` (Type): {} convert the selected text to groups of compound paths (one per glyph); a frame of threaded text leaves its thread with the text it shows → {ids}
- `type.scaleTextAttributes` (Type): {ids?\|id?} bake each selected type object's transform scale into its character sizes (leading, baseline shift and horizontal scale included), leaving rotation and position in the affine → {ids, changed}
- `type.opticalMarginAlignment` (Type): {ids?\|id?, on?: bool} turn Optical Margin Alignment on or off for the selected type and every frame of its threads (on omitted: the opposite of the first object's setting): in every paragraph of point and area type, quotation marks, hyphens, full stops and commas at a line's start or end hang wholly outside the margin there; asterisks, tildes, ellipses, en and em dashes, colons and semicolons hang by half; and A, V, W, Y, v, w and y hang by a tenth of their advance; a left-aligned line hangs at its left margin, a right-aligned line hangs at its right margin, and centered and justified lines hang at both; Auto aligns a right-to-left line at its right; type on a path hangs at the start bracket when its line is placed there, hangs at the end bracket when the line is placed there, and hangs at both when it is centered → {on, ids}
- `type.systemLayout`: {on?: bool} turn System Layout on or off for the document (on omitted: the opposite of the document's setting) and every text object in it. On, each character that takes any space advances by a whole number of points, at least one, so small type does not run together; a character with no width stays at none. Off (the default, and what a document from before it reads as), spacing uses fractions of a point. New point, area and path type inherits the document's setting → {on}
- `type.fractionalWidths`: {ids?\|id?, on?: bool} turn Fractional Widths on or off for the selected type and every frame of its threads (on omitted: the opposite of the first object's setting). On (the default, and what a document from before it reads as), spacing between characters uses fractions of a point. Off, each character that takes any space advances by a whole number of points, at least one, so small type does not run together; a character with no width stays at none. The setting is the whole type object, not a range of characters → {on, ids}
- `type.ruby`: {ids?\|id?, start?: byte, end?: byte, text?: reading, position?: above\|below (vertical type: right\|left), align?: start\|center\|end, size?: 1–100 (percent of the base text's size, default 50), overhang?: bool (default true), remove?: bool} set the ruby (furigana) over the selected text's bytes in `start..end` (without them: all its text): the reading over the base characters, above them in horizontal type and right of them in vertical type; `remove` takes the ruby off the range instead → {ids, ruby: [{start, end, text, position, align, sizePercent, overhang}…] (the first object's ruby)}
- `type.changeCase` (Type › Change Case): {case: "upper"\|"lower"\|"title"\|"sentence", ids?\|id?} → {ids}
- `type.smartPunctuation` (Type): {quotes?: true (straight quotes become the Document Setup quotes), dashes?: true (-- → en dash, --- → em dash), ellipsis?: true (... → …), scope?: "selection"\|"document"} → {changed}
- `type.convertToAreaType` (Type): {ids?\|id?} point type → area type framed by its current layout bounds → {ids}
- `type.convertToPointType` (Type): {ids?\|id?} area type → point type; soft line wraps become line breaks, and a frame of threaded text leaves its thread with the text it holds → {ids}
- `type.pathOptions` (Type › Type on a Path): {ids?\|id?, start?: 0..1, end?: 0..1\|null, flip?: bool, effect?: rainbow\|skew\|3dRibbon\|stairStep\|gravity, alignToPath?: ascender\|descender\|center\|baseline, spacing?: pt} set the selected type on a path's options: start and end place its start and end brackets as fractions of the path's length (end null: the end of the path, or once round a closed path), and flip then turns the type to the other side of its path (the path runs the other way, the brackets swap ends); none given: query → the first object's {start, end, flip: false, effect, alignToPath, spacing}
- `type.fillPlaceholder` (Type): {ids?\|id?} replace selected text with placeholder text (area type is filled to its frame) → {ids}
- `type.insert` (Type): {char: "bullet"\|"copyright"\|"ellipsis"\|"paragraph"\|"registered"\|"section"\|"trademark"\|"emDash"\|"enDash"\|"discretionaryHyphen"\|"nonBreakingHyphen"\|"doubleLeftQuote"\|"doubleRightQuote"\|"singleLeftQuote"\|"singleRightQuote"\|"emSpace"\|"enSpace"\|"hairSpace"\|"sixthSpace"\|"thinSpace"\|"nonBreakingSpace"\|"figureSpace"\|"punctuationSpace"\|"thirdSpace"\|"quarterSpace"\|"tab"\|"forcedLineBreak"\|"paragraphReturn" \| text: string, ids?\|id?: object ids (default: the selection)} append to the selected text objects
- `type.fitHeadline` (Type): {ids?\|id?} track the first line of area type so it fills the frame width → {ids, tracking}
- `type.step`: {attribute: "size"\|"leading"\|"tracking"\|"kerning"\|"baselineShift", by?: steps (1; negative steps down), id?, start?: byte, end?: byte, ids?: [id…] (instead of id)} step type by the Preferences › Type increments (size and leading: typeSizeIncrement, pt; tracking and kerning: trackingIncrement, 1/1000 em; baseline shift: baselineShiftIncrement, pt), what the Type tool's Alt+arrows do (Cmd/Ctrl too: five steps; horizontal: ←/→ kerning at the caret or tracking of the selection, ↑/↓ leading, Shift+↑/↓ baseline shift; vertical: ↓/↑ with or without Shift kerning or tracking, ←/→ leading, Shift+→/← baseline shift; mixed selected text objects use the horizontal mapping). The range of `id`, else the type objects in `ids`, else the text the Type tool has selected (at a caret: kerning steps the character before it, leading its paragraph), else every selected type object → {ids}
- `type.size.increase`: {by?: steps (1)} type.step {attribute: size}
- `type.size.decrease`: {by?: steps (1)} type.step {attribute: size, by: -by}
- `type.size.increaseByFive`: {} type.step {attribute: size, by: 5}: five times the Size/Leading increment (typeSizeIncrement)
- `type.size.decreaseByFive`: {} type.step {attribute: size, by: -5}: five times the Size/Leading increment (typeSizeIncrement)
- `type.resetTracking`: {ids?: [id…]} set tracking to 0 and clear manual kerning without changing the automatic method in all the text of the type objects in `ids`, else in the text the Type tool has selected (at a caret: only the kerning of the character before it), else in every selected type object, as one undo step → {ids}
- `type.resetHorizontalScale`: {ids?: [id…]} set the horizontal scale to 100% (as the Character panel shows it, the object's own transform included) in all the text of the type objects in `ids`, else in the text the Type tool has selected (nothing at a caret), else in every selected type object, as one undo step → {ids}

## variable

- `variable.define` (Window › Variables): {name, kind: text\|visibility\|image\|graph} define a variable (graph: CSV data on a graph root); refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.delete` (Window › Variables): {names?} delete variables with their bindings and values (default: the highlighted one); refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.rename` (Window › Variables): {name, newName} rename a variable, with its bindings and its values in every dataset; refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.list`: {} → variables with kinds and binding counts, and whether they are locked → {variables, locked}
- `variable.makeTextDynamic` (Window › Variables): {} define a text variable over the selection, named after it and bound to it; refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.makeVisibilityDynamic` (Window › Variables): {} define a visibility variable over the selection, named after it and bound to it; refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.makeImageDynamic` (Window › Variables): {} define an image variable over the selection, named after it and bound to it; refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.makeGraphDynamic` (Window › Variables): {} define a graph variable over the selected graph root(s), named after it and bound to it; each dataset row supplies CSV as graph.setData; refused while the variables are locked
- `variable.bind` (Window › Variables): {variable, ids?} bind objects (default: the selection) to a variable; text needs type, image needs an image object, graph needs a graph root, locked art is refused. An object may hold several variables, and binding it to one it already has changes nothing → {bound, already}
- `variable.unbind` (Window › Variables): {ids?, variable?} drop bindings (default: the selection): `variable` drops that one, else every variable the object has
- `variable.lock` (Window › Variables): {locked?: bool} lock or unlock the variables (default: toggle); saved with the document; a change is one undo step. While locked, variable.define, variable.delete, variable.rename, variable.make…Dynamic and a variable.loadLibrary that would delete or define a variable are refused; binding, unbinding and the dataset commands still run, except a dataset.import whose header would define a variable → {locked}
- `variable.highlight`: {variable?} highlight one Variables panel row (a click); what the panel's Delete, Options… and Select Bound Object act on. An empty or missing `variable` clears it. Not an undo step → {variable}
- `variable.loadLibrary` (Window › Variables): {path? \| xml? \| dataBase64?} replace the document's variables and data sets with a variable library's (XML: `variableSets` › `variableSet` › `variables` of `variable` elements with `varName` and `trait`, and `sampleDataSets` of `sampleDataSet` elements with `dataSetName`) in one undo step (none when the document already holds what the library gives). Every variable of the document is deleted with its bindings and values, and every data set is deleted; then variables whose `trait` is `textcontent` are defined as text variables, unbound, and each data set takes a value for a variable from the `p` elements of the element named after it, one paragraph each. Variables of other kinds are left out and named in `skipped` and `warning`. Refused while the variables are locked when it would delete or define a variable → {variables, deleted, deletedDataSets, names, skipped, warning?}
- `variable.saveLibrary` (Window › Variables): {path?} write the document's text variables and every data set as a variable library, the XML that variable.loadLibrary reads: a `variable` element with `varName` and `trait="textcontent"` per text variable, and a `sampleDataSet` element with `dataSetName` per data set, holding an element named after each text variable it has a value for, with one `p` element per line of the value. Variables of other kinds are left out with their values and named in `skipped` and `warning`. Refused without a text variable, for a text variable whose name isn't an XML name (letters, digits, `-`, `_` and `.`, starting with a letter or `_`), for a data set name or value that holds a control character, for a data set name with white space at either end, for a name longer than 255 characters or used twice, and past variable.loadLibrary's caps (256 variables, 1,000 data sets, 8 MiB). Without path → {xml, variables, datasets, skipped, warning?}; with it → {path, variables, datasets, skipped, warning?}

## view

- `view.globalRulers` (View › Rulers): {global?: bool (default: toggle)} global rulers measure the whole document from one zero point; artboard rulers (a new document's) from the active artboard's top-left corner, or the zero point set for it. Saved with the document → {global}
- `view.drawMode`: {mode?: normal\|behind\|inside} (inside needs one selected path, compound path or type; no param cycles normal → behind → inside → normal, going from behind back to normal when Draw Inside can't draw into the selection; when the Draw Inside target is isolated, drawing or pasting into it moves isolation to its clip group)
- `view.proofSetup` (View › Proof Setup): {target?: "workingCmyk"\|"cmyk:<profile>"\|"legacyMacRgb"\|"srgb"\|"monitorRgb"\|"protanopia"\|"deuteranopia"\|"tritanopia", intent?, simulatePaper?, proof?: bool (also turn Proof Colors on)} → proof state
- `view.proofColors` (View): {on?: bool (default: toggle)} → proof state
- `view.overprintPreview` (View): {on?: bool (default: toggle)} → proof state
- `view.separationsPreview`: {on?: bool, plates?: [names], toggle?: plate, only?: plate} Separations Preview: show the chosen plates (one plate = greyscale ink coverage) → {on, plates: [{name, spot, visible, rgb}]}
- `view.capsLock`: {on?: bool} set or read the Caps Lock state the desktop app reports; while it is on, the drawing tools show the precise crosshair, and Use Precise Cursors keeps its saved value → {on}
- `view.saved.new` (View): {name?, center: [x, y], zoom: (1 = 100%), rotation?: deg} save a view in the document (the app fills in the current one; default "View N"; at most 25, names up to 255 characters, each name once) → {name, index}
- `view.saved.edit` (View): {name, newName?: rename, delete?: bool} \| {edits: [{name, newName?, delete?}…]} rename or delete saved views, all in one undo step; names are those before the edit and must stay unique; no params → {views}
- `view.saved.list`: {} → {views: [{name, center, zoom, rotation}]}
- `view.transparencyGrid` (View): {on?: bool (default: toggle)} show the transparency grid behind the active document's artboards (each document has its own setting) → {on}
- `view.snapToGlyph.options`: {baseline?: bool, xHeight?: bool, bounds?: bool, proximity?: bool, angular?: bool, anchors?: bool} the Character panel's Snap to Glyph options, kept in the preferences (the glyphSnap group, all on by default): which guides View › Snap to Glyph gives — the baseline and x-height of a single line of type, Glyph Bounds (each glyph's edges), Proximity Guides (both edges of each character's width), Angular Guides (a glyph's slanted strokes, and every guide of rotated type) and the glyphs' anchor points; {} reads them → the options
- `view.guides.make` (View › Guides): {} turn the selected paths into guides → {count}
- `view.guides.release` (View › Guides): {index?: a ruler guide's index, ids?: [guide path id…]} turn guides into paths: index releases that ruler guide and ids release those guide paths (and the guide paths inside them); otherwise the selected guide paths and ruler guides are released, and with no guide selected every guide path is released; a ruler guide becomes a straight path with no fill or stroke, across its artboard or, for a canvas guide, across the bounds of all the artboards; index and ids need guides unlocked; one undo step, the released paths selected → {count}
- `view.guides.lock` (View › Guides): {locked?: bool} toggle (or set) the session's guide lock; locking deselects the ruler guides and guide paths in every open document → {locked}
- `view.guides.clear` (View › Guides): {} delete all ruler guides and guide paths → {count}
- `view.slices.hide` (View): {hidden?: bool} toggle (or set) whether the canvas hides the slices (session view state); hiding deselects the slices in every open document, and showing them again leaves them deselected and does not restore the selection that was cleared; while they are hidden no command selects one (an object that is an object slice can still be selected as art) → {hidden}
- `view.slices.lock` (View): {locked?: bool} toggle (or set) the slice lock: locked slices can't be selected or edited with the Slice Selection tool; locking deselects the slices in every open document, and unlocking leaves them deselected and does not restore the selection that was cleared; while it is on no command selects one (an object that is an object slice can still be selected as art) → {locked}
- `view.printTiling` (View): {on?: bool (default: toggle)} show the pages of the document's print settings on the canvas (each document has its own setting; the Print Tiling tool shows them too) → {on}

## webExport

- `webExport.settings`: {reset?: false, preset?, …the settings of document.exportForWeb} remember settings for the Save for Web dialog (it opens on them): the keys given change the remembered ones, a preset replaces them, reset goes back to the defaults; {} reads them → {settings}
- `webExport.presets.list`: {} → {presets: [{name, builtIn, settings}]} the built-in presets, then the saved ones; any name works as `preset` in document.exportForWeb
- `webExport.presets.save`: {name, newName?: rename it, preset?: start from this preset (default: the saved preset `name`, else the defaults), settings?: {…}, …the settings of document.exportForWeb} create or change a saved preset (built-in ones can't change) → {name, settings, created}
- `webExport.presets.delete`: {name} delete a saved preset (built-in ones stay) → {deleted: name}
